### UpdateSpeakerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.Update` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/Update/UpdateSpeakerHandler.cs:15` · Level 10 · class

- **What it is**: the handler for [`UpdateSpeakerCommand`](#updatespeakercommand). It loads the [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker), stamps the concurrency token, filters the organizer-only field, delegates to the aggregate's `Update`, saves, and returns the DTO.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`UpdateSpeakerHandler.cs:6,18`), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`:5,16`), [`SpeakerDTOMapper`](#speakerdtomapper) (`:2,17`), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:7`), the [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) aggregate (`:3`), and `ILogger<T>` (`:1,18`).
- **Concept introduced**: **field-level authorization inside the handler.** The single most instructive line is `var isTopSpeaker = command.CallerIsOrganizer ? command.Request.IsTopSpeaker : entity.IsTopSpeaker;` (`:40`). A non-organizer's submitted value is discarded and the stored value is passed through unchanged, so a BR-214 self-edit cannot feature its own speaker no matter what the body says. The comment block above it (`:34-39`) spells out both halves of the design: `IsTopSpeaker` is organizer curation, so "a crafted request body cannot feature a speaker", and `LinkedUserId` "is not part of this request at all" because the governed `/link` and `/unlink` endpoints carry the BR-208 uniqueness check and raise the events that keep Identity's `User.LinkedSpeakerId` in sync. Two different techniques for two different risks: remove the field from the contract when no caller should ever set it, filter it in the handler when some callers may. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether privileged fields are protected server-side: neither protection can be bypassed by a client. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: the handler makes the decision, the controller only supplies the fact.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`:21`) gets the typed repository (`:25`), loads by id (`:26`), and returns `Error.NotFound` tagged with source and target when absent (`:27-28`). It stamps the client's row version (`:32`), with the same comment about `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` mapping to `409` (`:30-31`) that the other two update handlers carry. It computes `isTopSpeaker` (`:40`), then calls `entity.Update(...)` with the ten request fields and the filtered flag as the eleventh argument (`:42-53`), propagating the aggregate's errors on failure (`:55-56`). On success it saves with `ConfigureAwait(false)` (`:58`), logs (`:60`), and returns `Result.Success(dtoMapper.MapToDTO(entity))` (`:62`). The `[LoggerMessage]` (`:65-66`) declares "Speaker {SpeakerId} updated".
- **Why it's built this way**: passing every field positionally to `entity.Update` keeps the aggregate the only place that can mutate speaker state, so invariants are checked in one place and the handler stays a pure orchestrator. The handler opens no transaction and evicts no cache: the decorators do, driven by [`UpdateSpeakerCommand`](#updatespeakercommand)'s markers.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`SpeakersController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakerscontroller) as `ICommandHandler<UpdateSpeakerCommand, Result<SpeakerDTO>>` (`SpeakersController.cs:47`) and invoked on `PUT` after the BR-214 organizer-or-self check (`:311-316`).

### UpdateSessionHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.Update` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/Update/UpdateSessionHandler.cs:17` · Level 11 · class

- **What it is**: the most guarded update handler in the module. It loads the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session), stamps the concurrency token, rejects an attempt to move the session between events (BR-140), loads the parent [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) to validate the room assignment and double-booking (BR-130), delegates to the aggregate, computes the BR-86 date-range advisory, saves, and returns an [`UpdateSessionResult`](#updatesessionresult).
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`UpdateSessionHandler.cs:7,20`), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`:6,18`), [`SessionDTOMapper`](#sessiondtomapper) (`:2,19`), [`SessionRoomScheduling`](#sessionroomscheduling) (`:3,57`), the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) and [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregates (`:4-5`), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:8`), and `ILogger<T>` (`:1,20`).
- **Concept introduced**: **four distinct guard styles in one method, each chosen to fit the rule.** This handler is worth reading line by line because it demonstrates the codebase's whole vocabulary of write-time checks:
  1. **The immutability guard** (`:37-44`). `command.Request.EventId != entity.EventId` returns `Error.UnprocessableEntity` with the stable code `"Session.EventId.Immutable"` and the message "Session cannot be moved between events." The handler owns this rule, not the aggregate, because only the handler sees both the submitted value and the stored one. `UnprocessableEntity` rather than `Validation` is the right classification: the payload is well-formed, the requested state transition is not allowed.
  2. **The delegated cross-aggregate check** (`:57-65`). Room ownership and slot overlap are pushed into the shared static [`SessionRoomScheduling.ValidateRoomAssignmentAsync`](#sessionroomscheduling), which the create path calls too, with `excludeSessionId: command.Id` (`:63`) so a session does not collide with itself. Errors propagate unchanged (`:66-67`).
  3. **The domain guard** (`:69-86`). Everything expressible from the session's own state goes to `entity.Update(...)`, whose [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) failure is propagated as-is.
  4. **The advisory** (`:89-91`). BR-86 does not block anything: `IsOutsideEventDateRange` returns a bool that rides out on [`UpdateSessionResult`](#updatesessionresult) and becomes an `X-Warning` header.
  Also note the read strategy on the parent event (`:48-52`): `GetByIdAsync(entity.EventId, includes: [nameof(Event.Rooms)], asTracking: false, ...)`. The rooms are eager-loaded because the room validation needs them in memory, and tracking is off because the event is read-only in this flow, so it must not join the change set. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]` assesses where rules live: state-local rules are in the aggregate, cross-aggregate rules are in the handler or a shared validator. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`: one targeted include, no tracking, one extra aggregate read. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: stable error codes and a non-fatal warning channel.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`:23`) gets the session repository (`:27`), loads by id (`:28`), and returns `Error.NotFound` when absent (`:29-30`). It stamps the row version (`:34`). The BR-140 check runs next (`:37-44`), before any further I/O, so an illegal move costs one query. It then loads the parent event with rooms, untracked (`:47-52`), returning `Error.NotFound` targeted at `Event` when the parent is missing (`:53-54`). Room validation follows (`:57-65`), passing the repository, the parent event, the requested `RoomId`, `StartsAt`, `EndsAt`, the self-exclusion id, and the handler name as the error source. `entity.Update(...)` receives the fourteen mutable fields (`:69-83`) and its failure is propagated (`:85-86`). `hasDateRangeWarning` is computed **before** the save (`:89-91`) from the requested times and the event's `StartDate` / `EndDate`. Then `SaveChangesAsync` with `ConfigureAwait(false)` (`:93`), `LogSessionUpdated` (`:95`), and `Result.Success(new UpdateSessionResult(dtoMapper.MapToDTO(entity), hasDateRangeWarning))` (`:97`). The private static `IsOutsideEventDateRange` (`:104-113`) converts each nullable `DateTime` with `DateOnly.FromDateTime` and returns `true` when the start falls before the event's first day (`:108-109`) or the end falls after its last day (`:110-111`), otherwise `false` (`:112`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the guard order is deliberate cost ordering. The cheapest rejections (not found, immutable event) come first, the extra database read for the parent event happens only once those pass, and the aggregate's own validation runs last. The comparison uses `DateOnly` rather than the raw `DateTime` because an event's dates are calendar days (`DateOnly`), so comparing a timestamp against them without truncation would report a session starting on the morning of day one as out of range.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`SessionsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#sessionscontroller) as `ICommandHandler<UpdateSessionCommand, Result<UpdateSessionResult>>` (`SessionsController.cs:45`) and invoked on `PUT` (`:306-308`); the controller appends the `X-Warning` header when the flag is set (`:314-317`), then returns `result.Value.Session` (`:320`).

### AddCategoryItemCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.AddCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/AddCategoryItem/AddCategoryItemCommand.cs:14` · Level 7 · record

- **What it is**: the write intent for adding one child item to an existing conference [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) aggregate. It carries the owning category id, an optional item id, the display name, and the sort order.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) from `MMCA.Common.Application.UseCases` (`AddCategoryItemCommand.cs:2,18`) and the [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) domain type, referenced only to build the cache key prefix (`:1,21`). `ConferenceCategoryIdentifierType` and `CategoryItemIdentifierType` are the module identifier aliases, both `int` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/MMCA.ADC.Conference.GlobalUsings.IdentifierType.cs:5-6`; see [primer](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)).
- **Concept introduced**: **the child-add command shape.** A positional `record` is the whole request: it holds no behavior, and the aggregate decides what the add means. Two details carry weight. First, `CategoryItemId` is nullable (`CategoryItemIdentifierType?`, `:16`), and the XML doc says why (`:11`): the Sessionize import supplies the source-assigned id, while a manual add leaves it `null` for database-generated identity. The domain honors both branches, because [`CategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitem) is marked `[IdValueGenerated]` yet still keeps a supplied id when one is given (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/CategoryItem.cs:13,61`). Second, implementing [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) opts the command into the caching decorator of the CQRS pipeline, so a successful add evicts the category read cache without the handler touching a cache API. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` assesses whether writes are explicit intents flowing through one uniform pipeline: this record is the intent, and the marker interface is how a cross-cutting concern attaches to it declaratively. `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting]`: cache eviction is declared on the contract and applied centrally, never hand-rolled per handler.
- **Walkthrough**: the positional parameters are `CategoryId` (`:15`), the nullable `CategoryItemId` (`:16`), `Name` (`:17`), and `Sort` (`:18`); the record is `sealed` and implements [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`:14,18`). The single member, `CachePrefix` (`:21`), returns `$"{typeof(Category).FullName}:"`, the key namespace the caching decorator wipes on success. Keying off `typeof(Category)` rather than `typeof(CategoryItem)` is deliberate: items are read as part of their parent, so the parent's cached reads are the stale ones.
- **Why it's built this way**: a positional record gives value equality and immutability for free, and deriving the prefix from the entity's `FullName` keeps producer (this command) and consumer (the query cache) agreed on one string with no shared constant to drift.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by [`CategoryItemsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#categoryitemscontroller) on `POST` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/CategoryItemsController.cs:126-130`), validated by [`AddCategoryItemCommandValidator`](#addcategoryitemcommandvalidator), and handled by [`AddCategoryItemHandler`](#addcategoryitemhandler).

### CategoryItemDTOMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/DTOs/CategoryItemDTOMapper.cs:12` · Level 7 · class

- **What it is**: the Mapperly-generated mapper that turns a [`CategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitem) domain entity into its wire-facing [`CategoryItemDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitemdto).
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) from `MMCA.Common.Application.Interfaces` (`CategoryItemDTOMapper.cs:3,13`), [`CategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitem) and [`CategoryItemDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitemdto) (`:1-2`), and the Mapperly source generator (`Riok.Mapperly.Abstractions`, `:4`).
- **Concept introduced**: **source-generated DTO mapping ([ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html)).** The class is `sealed partial` and carries `[Mapper]` (`:11-12`); Mapperly writes the body of the `partial CategoryItemDTO MapToDTO(...)` declaration (`:16`) at compile time by name-matching properties, so there is no runtime reflection, no expression tree, and no hand-written field copy to fall behind the entity. This is the split ADR-001 describes: property-name-parallel entity/DTO pairs get generated mappers, and only genuine mismatches need hand-written code. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether the domain model is shielded from the wire contract: the entity and the DTO stay two separate shapes, so a domain rename cannot silently change the API payload (it breaks the build instead). `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`: compile-time mapping costs no reflection at runtime.
- **Walkthrough**: `MapToDTO` (`:16`) is the generated single-entity conversion. `MapToDTOs` (`:19`) is hand-written because the interface asks for a collection overload: it null-guards with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(entityCollection)` (`:21`), then projects each element through `MapToDTO` into a materialized array with the collection expression `[.. entityCollection.Select(MapToDTO)]` (`:22`). Materializing rather than returning a lazy sequence matters, because the caller may dispose the DbContext before enumeration.
- **Why it's built this way**: implementing the framework's [`IEntityDTOMapper`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) contract is what lets the mapper be discovered by assembly scanning and injected, so no caller ever `new`s it or hardcodes a conversion.
- **Where it's used**: composed as the child mapper inside [`ConferenceCategoryDTOMapper`](#conferencecategorydtomapper) and injected into [`AddCategoryItemHandler`](#addcategoryitemhandler) to shape the newly added item for the response.

### ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/Create/ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest.cs:10` · Level 7 · record

- **What it is**: the request DTO a client posts to create a conference [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category). It carries four inbound fields (`Id`, `Title`, `Sort`, `Type`) and doubles as the command that flows through the CQRS pipeline; there is no separate `CreateCategoryCommand` wrapper.
- **Depends on**: [`ICreateRequest`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) and [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest.cs:2-3,10`) plus the [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) type for the cache prefix (`:1,13`). `ConferenceCategoryIdentifierType` is the module alias.
- **Concept introduced**: **the request-is-the-command idiom.** Rather than a command record wrapping a request record, the create path lets one `record class` be both the bound wire contract and the handler input. Implementing [`ICreateRequest`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) is what lets the generic entity-request-mapper machinery recognize it (the type's own `<remarks>` says exactly that, `:8-9`), and [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) opts it into cache eviction on success. Contrast the update path, which does wrap a request in a command ([`UpdateConferenceCategoryCommand`](#updateconferencecategorycommand)) because an update needs the route id and the concurrency token alongside the body. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]`: writes are explicit intents, and marker interfaces are how cross-cutting behavior attaches to them declaratively. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: the create shape is a first-class named type with compiler-enforced required fields, not an anonymous bag.
- **Walkthrough**: `CachePrefix` (`:13`) returns the same `Category` full-name prefix the item commands use. `Id` (`:16`) is `init`-only and optional (the doc comment at `:15` calls it auto-generated when not provided). `Title` (`:19`) is `required string`, the one field the create validator guards. `Sort` (`:22`) is an `int` display order defaulting to zero. `Type` (`:25`) is an optional discriminator string, documented as "session" or "speaker" (`:24`).
- **Why it's built this way**: `required` plus `init` gives an immutable request whose one mandatory field cannot be omitted at the compiler level, matching the codebase-wide immutability convention.
- **Where it's used**: bound from the body by [`ConferenceCategoriesController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#conferencecategoriescontroller) (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/ConferenceCategoriesController.cs:94`), validated by [`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestValidator`](#conferencecategorycreaterequestvalidator), translated by [`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestMapper`](#conferencecategorycreaterequestmapper), and handled by [`CreateConferenceCategoryHandler`](#createconferencecategoryhandler).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: because `Id` is a non-nullable `int`, an omitted id binds as `0` and reaches `Category.Create` as `0`, not `null`, so the `id ?? ...` fallback at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/Category.cs:69` is not what supplies the key on this path. [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) is annotated `[IdValueGenerated]` (`Category.cs:15`), so a zero id is the unset sentinel the persistence layer replaces on insert.

### QuestionTextRules<T>

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Questions.Validation` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/Validation/QuestionValidationRules.cs:12` · Level 7 · class

- **What it is**: a reusable FluentValidation rule set for the text of a conference question. It packages the two constraints any question-text field must satisfy (non-empty, bounded length) so several validators can share one definition.
- **Depends on**: `AbstractValidator<T>` from FluentValidation (`QuestionValidationRules.cs:2,13`), `System.Linq.Expressions.Expression<Func<T, string>>` for the property selector (`:1,15`), and [`QuestionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questioninvariants) for the max-length constant (`:3,18`).
- **Concept introduced**: **the parameterized rule set.** Instead of repeating `RuleFor(x => x.Text).NotEmpty().MaximumLength(...)` inside every validator that carries question text, the constraints live once in a small `AbstractValidator<T>` subclass whose constructor takes a property `selector`. A concrete validator folds it in with FluentValidation's `Include(...)`. Because the rule set is generic in `T` and receives the selector, the same type validates a create request or an update request, each pointing the selector at its own property. This is the hand-rolled cousin of the framework's [`RequiredStringRules<T>`](group-06-validation.md#requiredstringrulest); the difference is that this one also pins the error codes. `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]` assesses whether input constraints are declared once and applied consistently: centralizing the question-text contract here keeps every entry path in agreement. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: one rule set, one responsibility, reused rather than copied.
- **Walkthrough**: the class is `sealed` and generic in `T` (`:12`). The expression-bodied constructor (`:15`) binds `RuleFor(selector)` and chains two clauses: `NotEmpty()` with the message "You must enter a Question Text" and error code `Question.QuestionText.Required` (`:17`), then `MaximumLength(QuestionInvariants.QuestionTextMaxLength)` (1000 characters, `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Questions/QuestionInvariants.cs:13`) with a length message and error code `Question.QuestionText.MaxLength` (`:18`). The stable `WithErrorCode(...)` strings are what the API error-mapping layer keys on, so they are part of the contract, not decoration.
- **Why it's built this way**: sourcing the bound from [`QuestionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questioninvariants) rather than a literal keeps the application-layer validator and the domain guard (`QuestionInvariants.cs:59`) citing one constant, so a limit change cannot drift between layers.
- **Where it's used**: `Include`d by `QuestionCreateRequestValidator` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/UseCases/Create/QuestionCreateRequestValidator.cs:10`) and `QuestionUpdateRequestValidator` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/UseCases/Update/QuestionUpdateRequestValidator.cs:10`), each binding the selector to its own `QuestionText` property.

### RemoveCategoryItemCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.RemoveCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/RemoveCategoryItem/RemoveCategoryItemCommand.cs:12` · Level 7 · record

- **What it is**: the intent to remove one item from a conference [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category), identified by the owning `CategoryId` and the target `CategoryItemId`.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) and the [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) type for the cache prefix (`RemoveCategoryItemCommand.cs:1-2,14,17`).
- **Concept introduced**: none new; this is the child-removal member of the command shape [`AddCategoryItemCommand`](#addcategoryitemcommand) introduces, reduced to the two ids. Note what it does **not** carry: no confirmation flag, no cascade option, no owner claim. Deletion is a soft delete decided inside the aggregate (`Category.RemoveCategoryItem` calls `categoryItem.Delete()`, `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/Category.cs:192-206`), so the command has nothing to say about it. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]`: the mutation is an explicit intent and routes through the aggregate root rather than deleting a child row directly.
- **Walkthrough**: two positional parameters, `CategoryId` (`:13`) then `CategoryItemId` (`:14`), on a `sealed record` implementing [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`:12,14`); `CachePrefix` (`:17`) returns the `Category` full-name prefix. Unlike [`AddCategoryItemCommand`](#addcategoryitemcommand), the item id here is **not** nullable: you cannot delete an item you cannot name.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by [`CategoryItemsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#categoryitemscontroller) on `DELETE` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/CategoryItemsController.cs:177`) and handled by [`RemoveCategoryItemHandler`](#removecategoryitemhandler), which tolerates a `CategoryId` of `0`.

### UpdateCategoryItemCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.UpdateCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/UpdateCategoryItem/UpdateCategoryItemCommand.cs:14` · Level 7 · record

- **What it is**: the intent to change an existing category item's display name and sort order, addressed by the owning `CategoryId` plus the `CategoryItemId`.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) and the [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) type for the cache prefix (`UpdateCategoryItemCommand.cs:1-2,18,21`).
- **Concept introduced**: none new; it is [`AddCategoryItemCommand`](#addcategoryitemcommand) with a required (non-nullable) item id, which is the whole difference between "create this child, maybe with a given id" and "change that child". It carries no `RowVersion`, so unlike the parent-level [`ConferenceCategoryUpdateRequest`](#conferencecategoryupdaterequest) an item edit is not optimistically concurrency-checked; the aggregate's own uniqueness rule (BR-138, `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/Category.cs:172-176`) is what stops two edits from converging on the same item name.
- **Walkthrough**: four positional parameters, `CategoryId` (`:15`), `CategoryItemId` (`:16`), `Name` (`:17`), and `Sort` (`:18`), on a `sealed record` implementing [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`:14,18`); `CachePrefix` (`:21`) evicts the same `Category`-scoped keys as its add and remove siblings.
- **Why it's built this way**: sending both the name and the sort on every update makes the command a full replacement of the item's editable state, so the handler never has to distinguish "not supplied" from "cleared".
- **Where it's used**: constructed by [`CategoryItemsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#categoryitemscontroller) on `PUT`, which takes the item id from the route and the category id from the body (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/CategoryItemsController.cs:153-157`), validated by [`UpdateCategoryItemCommandValidator`](#updatecategoryitemcommandvalidator), and handled by [`UpdateCategoryItemHandler`](#updatecategoryitemhandler).

### AddCategoryItemCommandValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.AddCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/AddCategoryItem/AddCategoryItemCommandValidator.cs:7` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`AddCategoryItemCommand`](#addcategoryitemcommand), run by the pipeline before the add handler executes. It checks the new item's `Name` and `Sort`.
- **Depends on**: `AbstractValidator<AddCategoryItemCommand>` (FluentValidation, `AddCategoryItemCommandValidator.cs:1,7`) and two shared rule sets from the module's `Categories.Validation` namespace (`:2`), [`CategoryItemNameRules<T>`](#categoryitemnamerulest) and [`CategoryItemSortRules<T>`](#categoryitemsortrulest).
- **Concept introduced**: **rule-set composition via `Include`.** Rather than restating the item-field rules inline, the constructor folds two reusable, selector-parameterized rule objects into this validator: `Include(new CategoryItemNameRules<AddCategoryItemCommand>(p => p.Name))` (`:11`) and `Include(new CategoryItemSortRules<AddCategoryItemCommand>(p => p.Sort))` (`:12`). Each rule set is the same device [`QuestionTextRules<T>`](#questiontextrulest) teaches, applied to category items: the name rule enforces non-empty plus a 500-character bound sourced from `CategoryInvariants.CategoryItemNameMaxLength` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/Validation/ConferenceCategoryValidationRules.cs:30-33`, constant at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/CategoryInvariants.cs:17`), and the sort rule enforces `GreaterThanOrEqualTo(0)` (`ConferenceCategoryValidationRules.cs:43-45`). `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]` and `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: the item's field rules live in exactly one place, so a length or range change updates every command that edits those fields at once.
- **Walkthrough**: the constructor body (`:9-13`) is two `Include` calls and nothing else. Neither id field is validated: the owning category's existence is a database question, answered by [`AddCategoryItemHandler`](#addcategoryitemhandler) with an `Error.NotFound`, and uniqueness of the name within the category is a domain question, answered by the aggregate (BR-138).
- **Why it's built this way**: splitting the checks by who can answer them is the whole idea. Format rules that need no data run here, before the transaction opens; rules that need the aggregate run inside it.
- **Where it's used**: discovered by assembly scanning and invoked by the pipeline's validation decorator ahead of [`AddCategoryItemHandler`](#addcategoryitemhandler).

### AddCategoryItemHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.AddCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/AddCategoryItem/AddCategoryItemHandler.cs:15` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the handler for [`AddCategoryItemCommand`](#addcategoryitemcommand): load the owning [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category), delegate the add to the root, persist, log, and return the new item's DTO.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`AddCategoryItemHandler.cs:6,18`), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`:5,16`), [`CategoryItemDTOMapper`](#categoryitemdtomapper) (`:2,17`), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:7`), and `ILogger<AddCategoryItemHandler>` (`:1,18`).
- **Concept introduced**: **routing a child mutation through the aggregate root.** The handler never inserts a `CategoryItem` row; it loads the `Category` and calls `category.AddCategoryItem(...)` (`:30`), so the aggregate enforces its own consistency (the DDD boundary rule from [Group 02](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md)). What lives behind that one call is substantial: the case-insensitive name-uniqueness rule BR-138, the [`CategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitem) factory, and a `CategoryItemChanged` domain event (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/Category.cs:131-153`). The handler is correspondingly thin, because validation, caching, and the transaction are applied by the decorator pipeline around it (see [primer](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to) and [Group 05](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult)). `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]`: mutations go through the root. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the `[LoggerMessage]` source-generated log (`:41-42`) is compile-time and allocation-free, no boxing and no runtime format parsing.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`:21`) gets the typed repository from the unit of work (`:25`) and loads the category with the plain `GetByIdAsync` (`:26`), with no eager include, because the aggregate root can add a child without materializing the existing collection. A `null` category returns `Error.NotFound` tagged with source and target for diagnostics (`:27-28`). It then calls `category.AddCategoryItem(command.CategoryItemId, command.Name, command.Sort)` (`:30`) and short-circuits with the aggregate's own errors on failure (`:31-32`). On success it awaits `SaveChangesAsync` with `ConfigureAwait(false)` (`:34`), emits `LogCategoryItemAdded` (`:36`), and returns `Result.Success(dtoMapper.MapToDTO(result.Value!))` (`:38`), mapping the very item the aggregate just created rather than re-reading it.
- **Why it's built this way**: the handler opens no transaction and evicts no cache itself; the transactional and caching decorators do that, driven by the marker interface on [`AddCategoryItemCommand`](#addcategoryitemcommand). That keeps the handler focused and the cross-cutting behavior uniform across every command in the module.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`CategoryItemsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#categoryitemscontroller) as `ICommandHandler<AddCategoryItemCommand, Result<CategoryItemDTO>>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/CategoryItemsController.cs:63`) and invoked on `POST`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `AddCategoryItem` returns the created entity, so the DTO is mapped from the in-memory instance. Any value the database assigns during `SaveChangesAsync` is reflected only because EF writes the generated key back onto that tracked instance; nothing in this handler re-queries.

### ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/Create/ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestMapper.cs:11` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the request-to-entity mapper that turns a validated [`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest`](#conferencecategorycreaterequest) into a [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) by calling the domain factory.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) (`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestMapper.cs:2,12`), [`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest`](#conferencecategorycreaterequest), [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) (`:1`), and [`Result<T>`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) from `MMCA.Common.Shared.Abstractions` (`:3,15`).
- **Concept introduced**: **the request mapper as the factory gateway.** Unlike a DTO mapper, which is a pure property copy, a request mapper is allowed to fail: `CreateEntityAsync` (`:15`) returns `Task<Result<Category>>` because it delegates to `Category.Create(...)`, the factory that validates the title and returns a failure [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) rather than throwing (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/Category.cs:54-74`). Entity construction and its invariants stay in the domain; the application layer only wires fields across. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]`: an invalid `Category` cannot be constructed because the only path in is the `Result`-returning factory, and this mapper never `new`s one. `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]`: the concrete mapper lives in the application layer but is consumed through a Common-owned interface, so the create handler depends on the abstraction, not on this class.
- **Walkthrough**: `CreateEntityAsync` (`:15`) null-guards the request (`:17`), then returns `Task.FromResult(Category.Create(request.Id, request.Title, request.Sort, request.Type))` (`:19-23`), passing the four request fields positionally in factory order. The work is synchronous and wrapped in `Task.FromResult` because no I/O is involved; the `async` shape exists so other entities can await a lookup here if their factory needs one.
- **Why it's built this way**: the create handler is entity-agnostic by design, so per-entity construction lives behind this small class and stays the one place that knows the `Category.Create` parameter order ([ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html), explicit mapping rather than reflection-driven binding).
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`CreateConferenceCategoryHandler`](#createconferencecategoryhandler) as the [`IEntityRequestMapper`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) for `Category` (`CreateConferenceCategoryHandler.cs:18`).

### ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/Create/ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestValidator.cs:7` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest`](#conferencecategorycreaterequest), run by the pipeline before the create handler.
- **Depends on**: `AbstractValidator<ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest>` (`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestValidator.cs:1,7`) and the shared [`ConferenceCategoryTitleRules<T>`](#conferencecategorytitlerulest) rule set from `Categories.Validation` (`:2`).
- **Concept introduced**: none new; it is the same `Include`-composition idiom [`AddCategoryItemCommandValidator`](#addcategoryitemcommandvalidator) teaches, folding a single rule set. The expression-bodied constructor (`:9-10`) calls `Include(new ConferenceCategoryTitleRules<ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest>(p => p.Title))`, which enforces non-empty plus a 255-character bound taken from `CategoryInvariants.TitleMaxLength` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/Validation/ConferenceCategoryValidationRules.cs:16-19`, constant at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/CategoryInvariants.cs:14`). The update-category validator includes the same rule set, so the title contract exists once. `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]`: validation is single-sourced across every command that edits a category title.
- **Walkthrough**: the entire body is the expression-bodied constructor (`:9-10`): one `Include` bound to `p => p.Title`. `Id`, `Sort`, and `Type` carry no create-time constraints, so nothing else is checked here.
- **Where it's used**: discovered by assembly scanning and invoked by the validation decorator ahead of [`CreateConferenceCategoryHandler`](#createconferencecategoryhandler).

### ConferenceCategoryDTOMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/DTOs/ConferenceCategoryDTOMapper.cs:13` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the Mapperly mapper from the [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) aggregate to its [`ConferenceCategoryDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#conferencecategorydto), including the child `CategoryItems` collection.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`ConferenceCategoryDTOMapper.cs:3,15`), the Mapperly generator (`:4`), and one injected child mapper, [`CategoryItemDTOMapper`](#categoryitemdtomapper) (`:13-14`).
- **Concept introduced**: **mapper composition with `[UseMapper]`.** This is the parent half of the two-mapper pair. The class is `sealed partial` with a primary constructor that takes the [`CategoryItemDTOMapper`](#categoryitemdtomapper) and stores it in a `[UseMapper]`-tagged private field (`:17-18`); that attribute tells Mapperly to call the child mapper for the nested collection instead of generating a second, parallel copy of the item mapping. Composition rather than regeneration is what keeps one entity/DTO pair mapped in exactly one place, however many parents embed it. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: the aggregate's wire shape is assembled from composable per-child mappers. `[Rubric §16, Maintainability]`: adding a field to `CategoryItemDTO` is a one-file change that both mappers pick up.
- **Walkthrough**: the primary constructor (`:13-14`) receives `categoryItemDTOMapper`; the `[UseMapper] private readonly` field (`:17-18`) exposes it to the generator. `MapToDTO` (`:21`) is the generated single-entity conversion, routing child items through the reused mapper. `MapToDTOs` (`:24`) is the same hand-written projection as its child mapper: null-guard (`:26`), then `[.. entityCollection.Select(MapToDTO)]` (`:27`).
- **Why it's built this way**: taking the child mapper through DI rather than `new`ing it keeps both mappers ordinary injectable services, which is also what makes them unit-testable in isolation (`[Rubric §14, Testability]`).
- **Where it's used**: the primary DTO mapper for Conference category reads, resolved with its child auto-injected, and consumed by [`CreateConferenceCategoryHandler`](#createconferencecategoryhandler) to shape its response.

### RemoveCategoryItemHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.RemoveCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/RemoveCategoryItem/RemoveCategoryItemHandler.cs:13` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveCategoryItemCommand`](#removecategoryitemcommand): load the owning [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) with its items and delegate the removal to the aggregate root. It is the one handler in this unit that can find its aggregate two different ways.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`RemoveCategoryItemHandler.cs:4,15`), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`:3,14`), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:5`), and `ILogger<RemoveCategoryItemHandler>` (`:1,15`).
- **Concept introduced**: **resolving the aggregate from the child when the caller cannot name it.** The `DELETE` endpoint binds the category id from the query string as a non-nullable `int` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/CategoryItemsController.cs:173`), and the UI's generic delete sends only the item id, so `CategoryId` arrives as `0`. Instead of failing, the handler branches on `command.CategoryId == default` (`:28`) and reverse-looks-up the owner with `GetAllAsync(..., where: c => c.CategoryItems.Any(ci => ci.Id == command.CategoryItemId), ...)`, taking the first match (`:30-35`); otherwise it loads by id directly (`:39-43`). Both branches eager-load `CategoryItems` and set `asTracking: true`, which is required because the removal mutates a tracked child. The comment at `:24-26` records exactly this contract quirk. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]`: the delete still goes through the root, which soft-deletes the child and raises `CategoryItemChanged` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/Category.cs:192-206`). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: a real client/contract gap is handled explicitly in one named place rather than by silently mis-deleting or by loosening the domain.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`:18`) gets the typed repository (`:22`), takes one of the two load branches (`:28-44`), and returns `Error.NotFound` with source and target when neither finds an owner (`:46-47`). It then calls `entity.RemoveCategoryItem(command.CategoryItemId)` (`:49`) and, **only if that succeeded** (`:50`), awaits `SaveChangesAsync` (`:52`) and logs `LogCategoryItemRemoved` (`:54`). The domain `Result` is returned unchanged (`:57`), so an aggregate-level failure (an unknown item id, for instance) reaches the caller with its own error intact.
- **Why it's built this way**: guarding the save behind `result.IsSuccess` avoids an empty `SaveChangesAsync` round-trip on a failed removal, and returning the domain result verbatim preserves the error code the API layer maps to a status.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`CategoryItemsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#categoryitemscontroller) (`CategoryItemsController.cs:65`) and invoked on `DELETE`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the reverse lookup takes `FirstOrDefault()` (`:35`). Category item ids are unique across categories in practice (the identity column is per-table), so a second match is not expected, but nothing in this handler asserts it.

### UpdateCategoryItemCommandValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.UpdateCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/UpdateCategoryItem/UpdateCategoryItemCommandValidator.cs:7` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`UpdateCategoryItemCommand`](#updatecategoryitemcommand), checking the same two editable fields the add path checks.
- **Depends on**: `AbstractValidator<UpdateCategoryItemCommand>` (`UpdateCategoryItemCommandValidator.cs:1,7`) and the shared [`CategoryItemNameRules<T>`](#categoryitemnamerulest) and [`CategoryItemSortRules<T>`](#categoryitemsortrulest) rule sets (`:2`).
- **Concept introduced**: none new; the constructor (`:9-13`) is line-for-line the same pair of `Include` calls as [`AddCategoryItemCommandValidator`](#addcategoryitemcommandvalidator), with the generic argument changed to `UpdateCategoryItemCommand` (`:11-12`). That the two validators are structurally identical is the point of the parameterized rule set: the duplication is one line per field per command, not one rule chain per command, and the constraints themselves exist once. `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]`: an item's name and sort obey the same contract whether it is being created or edited.
- **Walkthrough**: two `Include` calls (`:11-12`), nothing else. As on the add path, neither id is validated here: a missing category or item is a `NotFound` from [`UpdateCategoryItemHandler`](#updatecategoryitemhandler), and name uniqueness is the aggregate's BR-138 check.
- **Where it's used**: discovered by assembly scanning and invoked by the validation decorator ahead of [`UpdateCategoryItemHandler`](#updatecategoryitemhandler).

### UpdateCategoryItemHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.UpdateCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/UpdateCategoryItem/UpdateCategoryItemHandler.cs:13` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the handler for [`UpdateCategoryItemCommand`](#updatecategoryitemcommand): load the owning [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) with its items tracked, delegate the edit to the root, and persist only on success. It returns a bare [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), not a DTO.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`UpdateCategoryItemHandler.cs:4,15`), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`:3,14`), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:5`), and `ILogger<UpdateCategoryItemHandler>` (`:1,15`).
- **Concept introduced**: none new beyond the aggregate-root routing [`AddCategoryItemHandler`](#addcategoryitemhandler) teaches, but the load differs in a way worth noticing. Where the add uses a bare `GetByIdAsync`, this one passes `includes: [nameof(Category.CategoryItems)]` and `asTracking: true` (`:23-27`), because `Category.UpdateCategoryItem` has to find the existing child, re-check name uniqueness across its siblings (BR-138, excluding the item being edited), and mutate it in place (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Categories/Category.cs:162-185`). Reading the collection is a precondition of the invariant, so the query shape follows the domain rule rather than a blanket "always include children" habit. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`: each handler loads exactly the graph its aggregate method needs, no more.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`:18`) gets the typed repository (`:22`), loads the category with its items tracked (`:23-27`), and returns `Error.NotFound` with source and target when it is absent (`:28-29`). It calls `entity.UpdateCategoryItem(command.CategoryItemId, command.Name, command.Sort)` (`:31`) and, only on success (`:32`), awaits `SaveChangesAsync` (`:34`) and emits the source-generated `LogCategoryItemUpdated` (`:36,42-43`). The domain result is returned as-is (`:39`), carrying the aggregate's own error when the item id is unknown or the new name collides.
- **Why it's built this way**: the shape mirrors [`RemoveCategoryItemHandler`](#removecategoryitemhandler) deliberately (same load, same success-guarded save, same verbatim result) so the three item mutations read as one family with one thing different each. There is no reverse category lookup here, because the `PUT` body carries the category id (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/CategoryItemsController.cs:154`).
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`CategoryItemsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#categoryitemscontroller) (`CategoryItemsController.cs:64`) and invoked on `PUT`.

### CreateConferenceCategoryHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/UseCases/Create/CreateConferenceCategoryHandler.cs:16` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the handler that creates a conference [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category): build the entity through the request mapper, add it, save it, log it, and return the mapped DTO. It is the highest-level type in this unit because it composes three of the others.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`CreateConferenceCategoryHandler.cs:7,20`), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`:6,17`), the [`IEntityRequestMapper`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) for `Category` (satisfied at runtime by [`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequestMapper`](#conferencecategorycreaterequestmapper), `:5,18`), [`ConferenceCategoryDTOMapper`](#conferencecategorydtomapper) (`:2,19`), and `ILogger<CreateConferenceCategoryHandler>` (`:1,20`).
- **Concept introduced**: **the create-handler shape.** The class is `sealed partial` with a primary constructor for DI (`:16-20`) and implements `ICommandHandler<ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest, Result<ConferenceCategoryDTO>>` (`:20`), the request-is-the-command idiom [`ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest`](#conferencecategorycreaterequest) introduces. Note the asymmetry in how the two collaborators are declared: the request mapper is injected through its **interface** (`:18`), so the handler could serve any entity whose create request implements the contract, while the DTO mapper is injected as the **concrete** [`ConferenceCategoryDTOMapper`](#conferencecategorydtomapper) (`:19`), which is what makes the return type concrete. `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` assesses whether a use case is self-contained: request, validator, request mapper, and handler sit in one `UseCases/Create` folder and are read together. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: `LogConferenceCategoryCreated` (`:42-43`) is a `[LoggerMessage]` source-generated log, compile-time and allocation-free.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`:23`) first awaits `requestMapper.CreateEntityAsync(command, cancellationToken)` (`:27`) and short-circuits with `Result.Failure<ConferenceCategoryDTO>(result.Errors)` if the domain factory rejected the input (`:28-29`), so a broken invariant becomes a failed result rather than an exception. It unwraps the entity (`:31`), gets the typed repository (`:32`), `AddAsync`es it (`:34`), and awaits the single `SaveChangesAsync` (`:35`) that both persists the row and flushes the aggregate's `CategoryChanged` domain event through the outbox interceptors ([ADR-003](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/003-outbox-pattern.html)). Both infrastructure awaits carry `ConfigureAwait(false)` (`:34-35`), the library-code convention ([ADR-049](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/049-library-configureawait-policy.html)); the mapper await at `:27` does not, because it does no I/O. It then logs (`:37`) and returns `Result.Success(dtoMapper.MapToDTO(entity))` (`:39`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the handler opens no transaction and evicts no cache; the transactional and caching decorators do that, driven by the marker interfaces on the request. Delegating construction to the mapper keeps this handler's body identical in shape to every other create handler in the module, which is what makes an unfamiliar one readable at a glance.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`ConferenceCategoriesController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#conferencecategoriescontroller) as `ICommandHandler<ConferenceCategoryCreateRequest, Result<ConferenceCategoryDTO>>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/ConferenceCategoriesController.cs:34`) and invoked on `POST` (`:94`).

### SpeakerFirstNameRules<T>

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.Validation` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/Validation/SpeakerValidationRules.cs:11` · Level 7 · class (sealed, generic)

- **What it is**: the reusable rule set for a speaker's first name. It packages the "required, bounded length" contract once so every speaker command validator can fold it in instead of restating the clauses.
- **Depends on**: [`RequiredStringRules<T>`](group-06-validation.md#requiredstringrulest), the framework base it derives from (`SpeakerValidationRules.cs:3,12`); `System.Linq.Expressions.Expression<Func<T, string>>` for the property selector (`SpeakerValidationRules.cs:1,14`); and [`SpeakerInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerinvariants) for the length bound (`SpeakerValidationRules.cs:2,15`).
- **Concept introduced, specializing a framework rule base instead of re-declaring clauses.** Where a hand-rolled rule set such as [`QuestionTextRules<T>`](#questiontextrulest) writes its own `NotEmpty().MaximumLength(...)` chain, this class derives from the shared generic [`RequiredStringRules<T>`](group-06-validation.md#requiredstringrulest) and passes three things to the base constructor: the selector, a human-facing field label, and the maximum length. The base already encodes the non-empty-plus-max-length pattern and the message wording, so the subclass is a single `base(...)` call. Because the class stays generic in `T` and receives a selector, one instance validates a create request, an update request, or an import mapping, each pointing the selector at its own property. `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]` assesses whether input constraints are declared once and applied consistently across entry paths: here the first-name contract has exactly one definition. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: the rule set has one responsibility and is reused by composition rather than copied.
- **Walkthrough**: `public sealed class SpeakerFirstNameRules<T> : RequiredStringRules<T>` (`SpeakerValidationRules.cs:11-12`). The constructor takes `Expression<Func<T, string>> selector` (`SpeakerValidationRules.cs:14`) and forwards it as `base(selector, "First Name", SpeakerInvariants.FirstNameMaxLength)` (`SpeakerValidationRules.cs:15`). The label "First Name" is what appears in the generated required and length messages; the bound is read from the domain invariants type, not from a literal here.
- **Why it's built this way**: sourcing the maximum length from [`SpeakerInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerinvariants) keeps the application-layer validator and the domain aggregate's own guard citing one constant, so a limit change cannot drift between layers. Deriving from the framework base rather than re-chaining clauses means every "required string" field in the codebase produces the same message shape.
- **Where it's used**: `Include`d by [`SpeakerCreateRequestValidator`](#speakercreaterequestvalidator) (`Speakers/UseCases/Create/SpeakerCreateRequestValidator.cs:11`) and [`SpeakerUpdateRequestValidator`](#speakerupdaterequestvalidator) (`Speakers/UseCases/Update/SpeakerUpdateRequestValidator.cs:11`), so both write paths check a speaker's first name identically.

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.Validation` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/Validation/SpeakerValidationRules.cs:22` · Level 7 · class (sealed, generic)

- **What it is**: the last-name counterpart to [`SpeakerFirstNameRules<T>`](#speakerfirstnamerulest), the reusable "required, bounded length" rule set for a speaker's last name.

The two rule sets share one file so the speaker-name contract is read and changed as a pair.

### AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddEventQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddEventQuestionAnswer/AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:11` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for recording an answer to a conference question against an [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event): which event, which question, the answer text, and optionally an explicit id for the new answer row.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) from `MMCA.Common.Application.UseCases` (`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:2,15`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) domain type used only to build the cache prefix (`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:1,18`), and the module identifier aliases `EventIdentifierType`, `EventQuestionAnswerIdentifierType`, and `QuestionIdentifierType` (see the [primer](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)).
- **Concept introduced, the cache-invalidating child-add command.** A mutation opts into cache eviction by implementing [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) and exposing a `CachePrefix`; the caching decorator in the CQRS pipeline (Group 05) purges every cached read under that prefix once the command succeeds. Two details generalize to every add-child command in this unit. First, the prefix is keyed on the *aggregate root* (`typeof(Event).FullName`, `AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:18`), not on the child type, because a cached event read carries its answers with it. Second, the child id is nullable (`EventQuestionAnswerIdentifierType?`, `AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:13`): a Sessionize import can supply the source-assigned id, while an interactive add leaves it `null`. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` assesses whether writes are explicit intents flowing through one pipeline: the record is the intent and the marker interface is how the cross-cutting cache concern attaches declaratively, so no handler touches the cache. `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting]`: caching is a pipeline concern, not hand-rolled per use case.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed record` with four positional parameters, `EventId`, the nullable `EventQuestionAnswerId`, `QuestionId`, and the `string AnswerValue` (`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:11-15`), plus the single computed `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:18`). There is no behavior here; the upsert rule, the published-event check, and the question-type check all live in [`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#addeventquestionanswerhandler).
- **Why it's built this way**: a positional record gives immutability and value equality for free, and keeping the payload to plain identifiers plus the answer text means the caller cannot smuggle in an owner id: the handler derives the answering user from [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) instead.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the answers controller with a `null` child id (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventQuestionAnswersController.cs:159`), validated by [`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator`](#addeventquestionanswercommandvalidator), and handled by [`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#addeventquestionanswerhandler).

### AddEventSpeakerCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddEventSpeaker` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddEventSpeaker/AddEventSpeakerCommand.cs:10` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for associating an existing speaker with an existing event. It creates the [`EventSpeaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventspeaker) association row, not the speaker.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`AddEventSpeakerCommand.cs:2,13`), [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) for the cache prefix (`AddEventSpeakerCommand.cs:1,16`), and the `EventIdentifierType`, `EventSpeakerIdentifierType`, and `SpeakerIdentifierType` aliases.
- **Concept**: the same cache-invalidating add-child shape introduced by [`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#addeventquestionanswercommand), reduced to its minimum: aggregate id, optional child id, and the one foreign identifier being linked. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]` assesses whether relationships are mutated through an aggregate root: the command names the `Event` first because the association is a child of the event aggregate, and the speaker aggregate is untouched by this write.
- **Walkthrough**: `sealed record AddEventSpeakerCommand(EventIdentifierType EventId, EventSpeakerIdentifierType? EventSpeakerId, SpeakerIdentifierType SpeakerId) : ICacheInvalidating` (`AddEventSpeakerCommand.cs:10-13`), with `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`AddEventSpeakerCommand.cs:16`). The nullable `EventSpeakerId` serves the Sessionize import path exactly as in the sibling command (`AddEventSpeakerCommand.cs:12`).
- **Why it's built this way**: linking rather than nesting keeps the speaker an independent aggregate that survives being removed from an event, and it keeps the duplicate-association rule (enforced in `Event.AddEventSpeaker`, `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:475-479`) inside the event boundary.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the event-speakers controller with a `null` child id (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventSpeakersController.cs:192`), validated by [`AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator`](#addeventspeakercommandvalidator), and handled by [`AddEventSpeakerHandler`](#addeventspeakerhandler).

### AddRoomCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddRoom/AddRoomCommand.cs:15` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for adding a room to an existing event. It is the widest add-child command in this unit: eight positional parameters carrying the target event, an optional explicit room id, and the room's descriptive fields.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`AddRoomCommand.cs:2,23`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) type for the cache prefix (`AddRoomCommand.cs:1,26`), and the `EventIdentifierType` and `RoomIdentifierType` aliases.
- **Concept**: the same cache-invalidating add-child shape as [`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#addeventquestionanswercommand), but here the nullable child id is genuinely load-bearing rather than an import convenience. Room ids are application-assigned (the integer primary key *is* the Sessionize id), so a `null` `RoomId` tells [`AddRoomHandler`](#addroomhandler) to allocate one from a reserved manual range, while a non-null id is respected as an explicit Sessionize id (`AddRoomCommand.cs:17`, and see [`AddRoomHandler`](#addroomhandler) for the allocation). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether inbound contracts are explicit about optionality: the four nullable trailing fields model genuinely optional room metadata rather than overloading empty strings.
- **Walkthrough**: the `sealed record` declares `EventId` and the nullable `RoomId` (`AddRoomCommand.cs:16-17`), the two mandatory room fields `Name` and `Sort` (`AddRoomCommand.cs:18-19`), then the four optional fields `Capacity`, `Floor`, `Location`, and `AccessibilityInfo` (`AddRoomCommand.cs:20-23`). `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`AddRoomCommand.cs:26`) evicts the event read cache, since a room is read as part of its event.
- **Why it's built this way**: one command serves both organizer-created rooms (no id) and Sessionize-imported rooms (explicit id), so the import needs no parallel write path. Cache invalidation as a marker interface keeps the decorator pipeline in charge of the cross-cutting concern.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the rooms controller (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/RoomsController.cs:150`), validated by [`AddRoomCommandValidator`](#addroomcommandvalidator), and handled by [`AddRoomHandler`](#addroomhandler). Its update counterpart is [`UpdateRoomCommand`](#updateroomcommand).

### EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/DTOs/EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:12` · Level 8 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the read-side mapper that turns an [`EventQuestionAnswer`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswer) child entity into an [`EventQuestionAnswerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswerdto) for API responses.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:3,13`), [`EventQuestionAnswer`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswer) and [`EventQuestionAnswerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswerdto) (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:1-2`), and the Mapperly source generator (`Riok.Mapperly.Abstractions`, NuGet, `EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:4`).
- **Concept introduced, compile-time DTO mapping with Mapperly.** The class is `sealed partial` and carries the `[Mapper]` attribute (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:11-12`). The `public partial EventQuestionAnswerDTO MapToDTO(EventQuestionAnswer entity)` declaration (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:16`) has no body: the Mapperly generator emits the property-by-property copy at build time. There is no reflection and no runtime mapping engine, so a shape mismatch between entity and DTO fails the build rather than a request, and the generated code is ordinary C# that the five error-severity analyzers can see. This is the single shared shape for the three flat mappers in this unit; [`EventSpeakerDTOMapper`](#eventspeakerdtomapper) and [`RoomDTOMapper`](#roomdtomapper) are byte-for-byte the same design with a different entity/DTO pair, and [`EventDTOMapper`](#eventdtomapper) extends it with child-mapper composition. `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]` assesses whether the domain leaks outward: the mapper is the one place a domain entity is converted to a wire contract, so controllers never serialize entities. `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]`: generated mapping removes the largest source of hand-copy bugs while staying inspectable. See [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html) for the no-reflection mapping policy.
- **Walkthrough**: two members. `MapToDTO` is the generated single-entity map (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:16`). `MapToDTOs` (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:19-23`) is hand-written: it null-guards with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(entityCollection)` (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:21`) and returns the collection expression `[.. entityCollection.Select(MapToDTO)]` (`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper.cs:22`).
- **Why it's built this way**: Mapperly generates the element map, and the batch loop is trivial enough to hand-write once with a null guard, which keeps the generated surface minimal. Implementing the generic [`IEntityDTOMapper`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) contract is what lets the shared query services and generic controllers stay entity-agnostic.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#addeventquestionanswerhandler) (`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:20`) to shape the success payload, and composed into [`EventDTOMapper`](#eventdtomapper) as a `[UseMapper]` child (`EventDTOMapper.cs:26-27`). Registered by Scrutor assembly scanning through `ScanModuleApplicationServices<ClassReference>()` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:106`), not by an explicit `AddScoped` line.

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/DTOs/EventSpeakerDTOMapper.cs:12` · Level 8 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the Mapperly mapper from an [`EventSpeaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventspeaker) association entity to its [`EventSpeakerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventspeakerdto).
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`EventSpeakerDTOMapper.cs:13`), [`EventSpeaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventspeaker), [`EventSpeakerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventspeakerdto), and Mapperly (NuGet, `EventSpeakerDTOMapper.cs:4`).

A `[Mapper]` `sealed partial` class (`EventSpeakerDTOMapper.cs:11-12`) with a generated `partial MapToDTO` (`EventSpeakerDTOMapper.cs:16`) and a hand-written null-guarded `MapToDTOs` (`EventSpeakerDTOMapper.cs:19-23`). ### PublishedEventSpecification

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.Specifications` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/Specifications/PublishedEventSpecification.cs:11` · Level 8 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: a one-line query filter object that restricts an event query to published events only. It is how BR-108 ("non-privileged readers see only published events") is expressed as data rather than as an `if` inside every read endpoint.
- **Depends on**: [`Specification<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#specificationtentity-tidentifiertype) from `MMCA.Common.Domain.Specifications` (`PublishedEventSpecification.cs:3,11`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate and its `EventIdentifierType` alias (`PublishedEventSpecification.cs:2,11`), and `System.Linq.Expressions` (`PublishedEventSpecification.cs:1,14`).
- **Concept introduced, authorization expressed as a specification.** The specification pattern itself is taught in [Group 03](group-03-querying-specifications.md); what this type introduces is using it as a *security filter*. The base class exposes a `Criteria` expression that the repository composes into the EF query, so the restriction is applied in SQL rather than after materialization: an unpublished event is never loaded, never counted in a page total, and never reaches the serializer. Because the filter is a first-class object, the decision "does this caller get the filter" becomes a nullable value at the call site instead of branching query code. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether authorization is enforced at the data boundary rather than in the view: here a non-privileged reader's query cannot return an unpublished row at all. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`: pushing the predicate into the expression tree keeps paging counts correct and avoids over-fetching.
- **Walkthrough**: the whole type is a single expression-bodied override, `public override Expression<Func<Event, bool>> Criteria => e => e.IsPublished` (`PublishedEventSpecification.cs:14`). There is no constructor and no state, so an instance is free to allocate per request.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping BR-108 in one named type means the four event read endpoints share one definition of "visible event", and the class name makes the business rule greppable. Inheriting from the framework `Specification<TEntity, TIdentifierType>` base lets the same object flow through the generic query service and repository without an ADC-specific overload.
- **Where it's used**: [`EventsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#eventscontroller) builds it through the private helper `GetPublishedEventSpecification()`, which returns `null` (no filter) when `currentUserService.IsPrivilegedConferenceReader()` and a new instance otherwise (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:66-67`). The helper is passed as the `specification:` argument on four read paths (`EventsController.cs:82`, `EventsController.cs:112`, `EventsController.cs:141`, `EventsController.cs:171`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the gate is the read *audience*, not the Organizer role alone. `IsPrivilegedConferenceReader()` also admits `ContentEditor`, because an editor who reads every session must also read the events those sessions belong to (`EventsController.cs:60-65`). The predicate here does not exclude soft-deleted rows; that is handled separately by the EF global query filter (see [Group 07](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md)).

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/DTOs/RoomDTOMapper.cs:12` · Level 8 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the Mapperly mapper from a [`Room`](group-17-conference-domain.md#room) child entity to its [`RoomDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#roomdto).
