Nothing about a room needs a custom conversion, so the generator handles every property.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`AddRoomHandler`](#addroomhandler) (`AddRoomHandler.cs:21`) and [`UpdateRoomHandler`](#updateroomhandler), and composed into [`EventDTOMapper`](#eventdtomapper) as a `[UseMapper]` child (`EventDTOMapper.cs:20-21`).

### AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddEventQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddEventQuestionAnswer/AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator.cs:8` · Level 9 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#addeventquestionanswercommand). It enforces exactly one thing: the answer text is not empty.
- **Depends on**: `AbstractValidator<AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand>` from FluentValidation (NuGet, `AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator.cs:1,8`). It includes no shared rule set.
- **Concept introduced, the inline validator with a stable error code.** Unlike the `Include`-composed validators in this unit ([`AddRoomCommandValidator`](#addroomcommandvalidator)), this one writes its single rule inline because no other command shares an "event answer value" field. The load-bearing detail is `WithErrorCode("EventQuestionAnswer.AnswerValue.Required")` (`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator.cs:14`): that string is what the API error-mapping layer keys on to build the problem-details response, so it is part of the public contract, not just prose. `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]` assesses whether bad input is rejected before it reaches business logic with a message the UI can act on; `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: the stable dotted error code lets clients branch on the failure without parsing English.
- **Walkthrough**: an expression-bodied constructor (`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator.cs:10`) chaining `RuleFor(x => x.AnswerValue).NotEmpty()` with the message "Answer value is required." and the error code above (`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator.cs:11-14`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the validator deliberately checks shape only. The *semantic* rules stay deeper: the 4000-character bound lives on `EventInvariants.AnswerValueMaxLength` and its guard (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/EventInvariants.cs:46`, `EventInvariants.cs:139`) and is mirrored in the EF configuration (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Infrastructure/Persistence/EntityConfiguration/EventQuestionAnswerConfiguration.cs:26`), and the "does this text match the question's type" rule is a domain invariant the handler calls (see [`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#addeventquestionanswerhandler)). The validator cannot express that rule because it would need to load the question first.
- **Where it's used**: resolved and run by the validation decorator in the CQRS pipeline (Group 05) before [`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#addeventquestionanswerhandler) executes.

### AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddEventQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddEventQuestionAnswer/AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:17` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler that records a user's answer to an event question. It is the most rule-dense handler in this unit: it gates on the event being published, cross-checks the question, and then performs an upsert rather than a blind insert.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:18`), [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) (`:19`), [`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper`](#eventquestionanswerdtomapper) (`:20`), `ILogger<AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler>` (`:21`), the [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) contract (`:21`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate with its [`EventQuestionAnswer`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswer) children, [`EventInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventinvariants) (`:38`), [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) plus [`QuestionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questioninvariants) (`:63,75`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result)/[`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept introduced, the add that is really an upsert (BR-107).** A user answering the same question twice must not accumulate rows; the second submission replaces the first. The handler encodes that as an identity lookup on the triple (current user, question, event): the event is already the aggregate being loaded, so the search reduces to scanning the loaded children for `!a.IsDeleted && a.QuestionId == command.QuestionId && a.CreatedBy == userId` (`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:49-50`). The owner is read from [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) (`:48`), never from the command, so a caller cannot answer on someone else's behalf. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether identity is derived server-side: the answering user is ambient, not a request field. `[Rubric §4, DDD]`: the reconciliation happens against children already loaded in the aggregate, so no second round trip and no chance of mutating an answer outside its event. `[Rubric §6, CQRS]`: one command, one handler, one transaction boundary at `SaveChangesAsync`.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:24-56`) loads the event through `unitOfWork.GetRepository<Event, EventIdentifierType>()` with `includes: [nameof(Event.EventQuestionAnswers)]` and `asTracking: true` (`:28-33`), because the domain mutation walks that child collection and EF must be tracking it; a missing event returns `Error.NotFound` sourced to the handler and targeted at `Event` (`:34-35`). BR-108 is checked by delegating to `EventInvariants.EnsureEventIsPublished(entity.IsPublished, ...)` (`:38-40`), which fails with the invariant code `Event.NotPublished` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/EventInvariants.cs:147-154`). The private `ValidateQuestionAsync` (`:58-77`) then loads the [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) by id and fails with the validation code `Question.NotFoundOrWrongEntity` when it is missing or its `QuestionEntity != "Event"` (BR-128, `:63-72`), and otherwise returns `QuestionInvariants.EnsureAnswerValueMatchesQuestionType(question.QuestionType, command.AnswerValue, ...)` (BR-124, `:75-76`), which switches on `"Rating"`, `"Text"`, or `"Email"` and rejects an unknown type outright (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Questions/QuestionInvariants.cs:115-126`). Only then does the upsert branch run: an existing answer routes to `UpdateExistingAnswerAsync` (`:52-53`), which calls `entity.UpdateEventQuestionAnswer(existingAnswer.Id, command.AnswerValue)` and returns the mapped existing row (`:79-92`); otherwise `CreateNewAnswerAsync` calls `entity.AddEventQuestionAnswer(command.EventQuestionAnswerId, command.QuestionId, command.AnswerValue)` and returns the mapped new child (`:94-109`). Both branches persist with a single `SaveChangesAsync(...).ConfigureAwait(false)` and log through the source-generated `LogQuestionAnswerAddedToEvent` (`:89-91`, `:106-108`, `:111-112`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the three gates run in increasing cost order, cheapest first: the aggregate is already loaded, the published flag is in memory, and only the question check costs a second query. Splitting the two upsert outcomes into private methods keeps `HandleAsync` readable as a decision tree while both paths share one save. The `[LoggerMessage]` source generator gives allocation-free structured logging (`[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`), and `ConfigureAwait(false)` on the infrastructure awaits follows the library convention ([ADR-049](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/049-library-configureawait-policy.html)).
- **Where it's used**: dispatched by the answers controller through the CQRS pipeline (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventQuestionAnswersController.cs:58`, `EventQuestionAnswersController.cs:159`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `currentUserService.UserId!.Value` (`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:48`) is null-forgiven, so the handler assumes an authenticated caller; the enforcement of that assumption lives in the controller's authorization attributes, not here. The success log message is identical for the update and insert branches (`:111`), so the log alone does not distinguish an upsert from a new answer.

### AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddEventSpeaker` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddEventSpeaker/AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator.cs:8` · Level 9 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`AddEventSpeakerCommand`](#addeventspeakercommand). It rejects a default (empty) speaker id.
- **Depends on**: `AbstractValidator<AddEventSpeakerCommand>` from FluentValidation (`AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator.cs:1,8`) and the `SpeakerIdentifierType` alias.
- **Concept**: the same inline-validator shape as [`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommandValidator`](#addeventquestionanswercommandvalidator), applied to an identifier instead of a string. `NotEqual(default(SpeakerIdentifierType))` (`AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator.cs:12`) is written against the alias rather than a concrete type, so the rule keeps working if the module's speaker key type changes: the alias is `System.Guid` today, which makes this an "id is not `Guid.Empty`" check. `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]`.
- **Walkthrough**: an expression-bodied constructor (`AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator.cs:10`) with one rule, `RuleFor(x => x.SpeakerId).NotEqual(default(SpeakerIdentifierType)).WithMessage("Speaker ID is required.")` (`AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator.cs:11-13`).
- **Why it's built this way**: an empty identifier is a client bug worth catching before a database round trip; whether the speaker actually exists, and whether it is already linked to this event, are questions only the aggregate can answer, so those stay in `Event.AddEventSpeaker` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:475-479`).
- **Where it's used**: run by the pipeline's validation decorator ahead of [`AddEventSpeakerHandler`](#addeventspeakerhandler).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: unlike its sibling in this unit, this rule sets no `WithErrorCode`, so the failure surfaces with FluentValidation's default code rather than a stable dotted code (`AddEventSpeakerCommandValidator.cs:11-13`). Whether that is deliberate is not stated in source.

### AddEventSpeakerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddEventSpeaker` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddEventSpeaker/AddEventSpeakerHandler.cs:15` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler that associates a speaker with an event. It is the reference "load aggregate, call a domain method, save, map" shape with nothing else layered on.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`AddEventSpeakerHandler.cs:16`), [`EventSpeakerDTOMapper`](#eventspeakerdtomapper) (`:17`), `ILogger<AddEventSpeakerHandler>` (`:18`), the [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) contract returning `Result<EventSpeakerDTO>` (`:18`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate, and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result)/[`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept**: read this one first if you want the skeleton every other handler in the unit decorates. Four moves and no branching beyond the two failure exits: load, delegate, save, map. Note what is *absent*: no ownership check (that is [`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#updateeventquestionanswerhandler)), no id allocation (that is [`AddRoomHandler`](#addroomhandler)), no upsert (that is [`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#addeventquestionanswerhandler)). `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` assesses whether a use case is self-contained: the command, validator, and handler live in one folder and share no base class. `[Rubric §4, DDD]`: the duplicate-association rule lives on the aggregate (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:475-479`), so the handler never re-implements it.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`AddEventSpeakerHandler.cs:21-41`) resolves the event repository from the unit of work and loads by id (`:25-26`), returning `Error.NotFound` sourced to the handler and targeted at `Event` when absent (`:27-28`). It calls `entity.AddEventSpeaker(command.EventSpeakerId, command.SpeakerId)` (`:30-32`) and propagates the aggregate's own errors verbatim on failure (`:33-34`). On success it persists with `SaveChangesAsync(...).ConfigureAwait(false)` (`:36`), logs the source-generated `LogSpeakerAddedToEvent` with both ids (`:38`, `:43-44`), and returns `Result.Success(eventSpeakerDTOMapper.MapToDTO(result.Value!))` (`:40`).
- **Why it's built this way**: loading without `includes` or `asTracking: true` is deliberate here, unlike the answer and room handlers: `Event.AddEventSpeaker` appends to the aggregate's own collection rather than walking a previously persisted child, so the default load is enough. Returning the domain result's errors rather than a handler-authored message preserves the aggregate's error code (`Event.Speaker.Duplicate`) all the way to the API response.
- **Where it's used**: dispatched by the event-speakers controller through the CQRS pipeline (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventSpeakersController.cs:48`, `EventSpeakersController.cs:192`).

### AddRoomCommandValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddRoom/AddRoomCommandValidator.cs:7` · Level 9 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`AddRoomCommand`](#addroomcommand), assembled from six reusable per-field rule sets rather than written inline.
- **Depends on**: `AbstractValidator<AddRoomCommand>` (FluentValidation, `AddRoomCommandValidator.cs:1,7`) and the module's room rule sets [`RoomNameRules<T>`](#roomnamerulest), [`RoomSortRules<T>`](#roomsortrulest), [`RoomCapacityRules<T>`](#roomcapacityrulest), [`RoomFloorRules<T>`](#roomfloorrulest), [`RoomLocationRules<T>`](#roomlocationrulest), and [`RoomAccessibilityInfoRules<T>`](#roomaccessibilityinforulest) from `Events.Validation` (`AddRoomCommandValidator.cs:2,11-16`).
- **Concept introduced, rule composition via `Include`.** FluentValidation's `Include(...)` folds another validator's rules into this one. Because each rule set is generic in the request type and takes a property selector, the same `RoomNameRules<T>` object validates a room name on this command, on [`UpdateRoomCommand`](#updateroomcommand), and on the Sessionize import path, each pointing at its own property. The alternative, restating the clauses per command, is what lets a constraint drift between the add and update endpoints. `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]` assesses duplication: there is exactly one definition of each room-field constraint. `[Rubric §16, Maintainability]`: a constraint change is a one-file edit that propagates to every command that composed the rule.
- **Walkthrough**: the constructor is six `Include` calls in field order, each constructing a rule set bound to the matching property: `RoomNameRules` on `p => p.Name` (`AddRoomCommandValidator.cs:11`), `RoomSortRules` on `Sort` (`:12`), `RoomCapacityRules` on `Capacity` (`:13`), `RoomFloorRules` on `Floor` (`:14`), `RoomLocationRules` on `Location` (`:15`), and `RoomAccessibilityInfoRules` on `AccessibilityInfo` (`:16`). No bespoke rule appears in this class; every constraint lives in the included types.
- **Why it's built this way**: rooms are written from two directions (organizer create and Sessionize import), and shared rule sets are the mechanism that keeps both honest without a base validator class. Its update counterpart [`UpdateRoomCommandValidator`](#updateroomcommandvalidator) composes the same six sets, which is the clearest demonstration that the composition is the point.
- **Where it's used**: resolved and invoked by the pipeline's validation decorator (Group 05) for [`AddRoomCommand`](#addroomcommand), before [`AddRoomHandler`](#addroomhandler) runs.

### AddRoomHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.AddRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/AddRoom/AddRoomHandler.cs:18` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler that adds a room to an event. It carries two concerns no other handler in this unit has: it allocates the room's primary key itself from a reserved range, and it retries in a fresh DI scope when a concurrent add wins the same key.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`AddRoomHandler.cs:19`), `IServiceScopeFactory` from `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection` (`:1,20`), [`RoomDTOMapper`](#roomdtomapper) (`:21`), `ILogger<AddRoomHandler>` (`:22`), the [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) contract (`:22`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) and [`Room`](group-17-conference-domain.md#room) types, [`EventInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventinvariants) for the reserved-range constants (`:87,93,95`), [`IReadRepository<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ireadrepositorytentity-tidentifiertype) via `GetReadRepository` (`:84`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result)/[`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept introduced, reserved-range key allocation with a bounded collision retry.** Room ids are application-assigned: the integer primary key *is* the Sessionize id, so the database identity column cannot own the value or an import would overwrite an organizer's room. The codebase resolves this by reserving a high block for manually created rooms, `EventInvariants.RoomManualIdRangeStart = 999_999_000` through `RoomManualIdRangeEnd = 999_999_999` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/EventInvariants.cs:53,56`), and having the handler pick the next free id inside it. That read-then-write is inherently racy across concurrent requests, so the handler wraps the whole attempt in a bounded retry keyed on a duplicate-key failure. `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses id strategy and ownership of key space: a reserved range keeps two writers (the app and Sessionize) in one integer column without a coordination service. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether transient conflicts are absorbed rather than surfaced: the retry is bounded to three attempts (`AddRoomHandler.cs:25`) so a genuine exhaustion or a real bug still fails fast rather than spinning. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the collision path logs a warning naming the attempt number (`:138-139`), so the race is visible in telemetry instead of silent.
- **Walkthrough**: three parts. (1) `HandleAsync` (`AddRoomHandler.cs:28-61`) is pure retry policy. An explicit `command.RoomId` short-circuits to a single attempt with no recomputation, because a collision on a caller-supplied id is a genuine caller error (`:32-35`). Otherwise it loops: attempt 1 runs against the ambient unit of work (`:43-44`); every later attempt opens `scopeFactory.CreateAsyncScope()` and resolves a fresh [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) from it (`:51-53`), because the ambient `DbContext` still tracks the failed insert and the whole body (including the event load) must re-run against a clean context (`:46-50`). The `catch` filter is narrow, `attempt < MaxManualIdAttempts && IsUniqueKeyViolation(ex)` (`:55`), so anything else propagates. (2) `AddRoomCoreAsync` (`:68-117`) is one attempt: load the event and fail `NotFound` if absent (`:73-76`); when `command.RoomId is null`, take a read repository for `Room` and `GetAllAsync` every room in the reserved range with `ignoreQueryFilters: true` so soft-deleted rows still reserve their ids (`:84-89`), compute `Max(r => r.Id) + 1` or the range start when empty (`:91-93`), and fail with `Error.Failure(..., "Manual room ID range exhausted.")` if that passes the range end (`:95-96`); then delegate to `entity.AddRoom(roomId, command.Name, command.Sort, command.Capacity, command.Floor, command.Location, command.AccessibilityInfo)` (`:101-108`), propagate domain errors (`:109-110`), `SaveChangesAsync` (`:112`), log `LogRoomAdded` (`:114`, `:135-136`), and return the mapped [`RoomDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#roomdto) (`:116`). (3) `IsUniqueKeyViolation` (`:124-133`) walks the whole `InnerException` chain looking for the substring "duplicate key" with `OrdinalIgnoreCase` (`:126-129`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the aggregate owns room creation invariants, while the handler owns the one concern no single event can decide, namely an id range that is global across all events (`AddRoomHandler.cs:78-80`). Detection is message-based rather than typed on a SQL exception because the Application layer is not allowed to reference EF Core or provider types (`:119-123`), which is Clean Architecture's dependency rule paying a small cost in precision; the comment records that both SQL Server errors 2601 and 2627 report "duplicate key". Retrying in a *new* scope instead of reusing the failed one is the load-bearing detail: reusing the attempt's already-mutated `Event` would append a second room and re-raise its domain event, and attaching that instance to another context is not permitted (`:46-50`).
- **Where it's used**: dispatched by the rooms controller through the CQRS pipeline for [`AddRoomCommand`](#addroomcommand) (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/RoomsController.cs:150`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the retry treats every attempt after the first as scope-fresh, so an attempt that fails for a non-duplicate reason is not retried at all (the filter excludes it) and surfaces as a thrown exception to the pipeline's exception middleware. `IsUniqueKeyViolation` matches on message text, so a provider that words its duplicate-key error differently would not be recognized; that risk is acknowledged in the comment (`AddRoomHandler.cs:119-123`) but not guarded in source.

### EventDTOMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/DTOs/EventDTOMapper.cs:14` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the mapper from an [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate to an [`EventDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventdto). Unlike the three flat mappers in this unit it composes the child mappers for rooms, speakers, and answers, and it hand-patches the one property Mapperly cannot generate.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (`EventDTOMapper.cs:3,18`), the three child mappers [`RoomDTOMapper`](#roomdtomapper), [`EventSpeakerDTOMapper`](#eventspeakerdtomapper), and [`EventQuestionAnswerDTOMapper`](#eventquestionanswerdtomapper) (`EventDTOMapper.cs:15-17`), [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) and [`EventDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventdto) (`EventDTOMapper.cs:1-2`), Mapperly (NuGet, `EventDTOMapper.cs:4`), and `System.Globalization.CultureInfo` (`EventDTOMapper.cs:39`).
- **Concept introduced, `[UseMapper]` composition plus a post-generation patch.** Two Mapperly techniques appear here that the flat mappers do not need. First, `[UseMapper]` on a field tells the generator "when you need to map this child type, call that mapper", so a parent map reuses the child maps instead of re-declaring them (`EventDTOMapper.cs:20-27`). Second, when one property needs a conversion the generator cannot express, the pattern is not to abandon generation: the public method is hand-written, it calls a *private* generated map, and it patches the one property afterwards with a `with` expression. The private generated map is told to skip that property via `[MapperIgnoreTarget]` (`EventDTOMapper.cs:50-51`), so the generator does not emit a competing (and failing) conversion. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` assesses composition over duplication: the room map is defined once in [`RoomDTOMapper`](#roomdtomapper) and reused here. `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]`: the hand-written escape hatch is one property wide and adjacent to a comment naming exactly why it exists (`EventDTOMapper.cs:35`), rather than a full hand-rolled map.
- **Walkthrough**: the primary constructor takes the three child mappers (`EventDTOMapper.cs:14-17`) and stores each in a `[UseMapper]`-attributed readonly field (`:20-27`). The public `MapToDTO` (`:30-41`) null-guards the entity (`:32`), calls the private generated `MapToDTOGenerated(entity)` (`:33`), then returns `dto with { LastSessionizeRefreshBy = entity.LastSessionizeRefreshBy?.ToString(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture) }` (`:36-40`): the entity holds a nullable `UserIdentifierType` while the DTO exposes a nullable string, a conversion the comment records as not expressible in Mapperly (`:35`). `MapToDTOs` (`:44-48`) is the standard null-guarded batch over the public `MapToDTO`, so every element gets the patch too. The generated map itself is the private `partial EventDTO MapToDTOGenerated(Event entity)` carrying `[MapperIgnoreTarget(nameof(EventDTO.LastSessionizeRefreshBy))]` (`:50-51`).
- **Why it's built this way**: routing the batch through the public `MapToDTO` rather than the generated one is what keeps the patch from being silently skipped on list endpoints, which is the failure mode this shape avoids. Delegating child collections to `[UseMapper]` keeps each child map in one file and means a change to `RoomDTO` is picked up by the event map with no edit here. Using `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` on the `ToString` keeps the wire value stable regardless of server locale, which the analyzers enforce anyway.
- **Where it's used**: injected into `CreateEventHandler` (`Events/UseCases/Create/CreateEventHandler.cs:19`) and `UpdateEventHandler` (`Events/UseCases/Update/UpdateEventHandler.cs:18`), and resolved for the event read paths through the generic query service. Registered by Scrutor assembly scanning (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:106`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the DTO exposes the last Sessionize refresh actor as a stringified identifier, not a display name; no lookup to Identity happens here, so consumers get the raw id text.

### EventCreateRequest

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Create/EventCreateRequest.cs:10` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the inbound contract for creating a conference [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event). It is both the HTTP request body and the command that the CQRS pipeline dispatches: there is no separate `CreateEventCommand`.
- **Depends on**: [`ICreateRequest`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) and [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) from `MMCA.Common.Application.UseCases` / `.Interfaces` (`EventCreateRequest.cs:2-3,10`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) domain type used only to build the cache prefix (`EventCreateRequest.cs:1,13`), the `EventIdentifierType` alias (`= int`, `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/MMCA.ADC.Conference.GlobalUsings.IdentifierType.cs:7`), and `DateOnly` (BCL).
- **Concept introduced, the create request as the command.** Every other write in this unit has a hand-written `XxxCommand` record. Create does not: implementing the marker [`ICreateRequest`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) (`EventCreateRequest.cs:10`) is what lets the generic create machinery in Group 12 accept this record straight off the wire, hand it to an [`IEntityRequestMapper`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype), and dispatch it as `ICommandHandler<EventCreateRequest, Result<EventDTO>>`. One record therefore carries three roles: the JSON contract, the validation target, and the command. `required` on `Name`, `StartDate`, `EndDate`, and `TimeZone` (`EventCreateRequest.cs:19,25,28,31`) makes the mandatory set a compile-time property of the type rather than a convention the deserializer has to be trusted with, and every member is `init`-only so the request cannot be mutated after model binding. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether inbound contracts are explicit about shape and optionality: the split between four `required` members and six nullable ones is the API's optionality documentation. `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` assesses whether a feature's request, validator, mapper, and handler live together: all four Create types sit in one folder.
- **Walkthrough**: `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`EventCreateRequest.cs:13`) is the eviction key the caching decorator purges on success, keyed on the aggregate root exactly as the child commands below do. `Id` (`EventCreateRequest.cs:16`) is a non-nullable `EventIdentifierType`, so an omitted id binds to `0`; the domain factory discards whatever arrives when `Event`'s key is store-generated (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:151,164`). Then the four required fields, `Name`, `StartDate`, `EndDate`, `TimeZone` (`EventCreateRequest.cs:19,25,28,31`), where `TimeZone` is an IANA identifier rather than a UTC offset, and finally the optional `Description`, `SessionizeCode`, `VenueAddress`, `VenueMapUrl`, `WiFiInfo` (`EventCreateRequest.cs:22,34,37,40,43`). `SessionizeCode` is the hook that later lets [`RefreshFromSessionizeCommand`](#refreshfromsessionizecommand) pull an agenda for this event.
- **Why it's built this way**: collapsing request and command removes a mapping step that would have no behavior of its own, while the marker interfaces keep caching and pipeline participation declarative instead of hand-coded in the handler. Storing an IANA zone id rather than a fixed offset is what lets session times render correctly across a daylight-saving boundary.
- **Where it's used**: bound by the events controller (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:233`) and injected there as `ICommandHandler<EventCreateRequest, Result<EventDTO>>` (`EventsController.cs:46`); validated by [`EventCreateRequestValidator`](#eventcreaterequestvalidator), converted by [`EventCreateRequestMapper`](#eventcreaterequestmapper), handled by [`CreateEventHandler`](#createeventhandler). Its edit-side counterpart is [`EventUpdateRequest`](#eventupdaterequest).

### PublishEventCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Publish` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Publish/PublishEventCommand.cs:12` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for flipping an [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) to published, which is what makes it visible to attendees. It carries the event id and, optionally, the concurrency token the client last saw.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`PublishEventCommand.cs:2,12`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) type for the cache prefix (`PublishEventCommand.cs:1,15`), and the `EventIdentifierType` alias.
- **Concept introduced, the optional optimistic-concurrency token on a state-transition command.** `byte[]? RowVersion = null` (`PublishEventCommand.cs:12`) is a defaulted positional parameter, so the command compiles and dispatches with or without it. When present it is the SQL Server `rowversion` the client received on its last read; the handler stamps it back as the row's *original* value so the `UPDATE` matches zero rows if anyone else changed the event in the meantime, and the save surfaces a 409 Conflict. When it is `null` the check is skipped entirely. Making it opt-in rather than mandatory is the deliberate choice recorded in [ADR-035](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/035-optimistic-concurrency.html): a state transition is the classic lost-update target (two organizers looking at the same stale draft), but internal callers such as the Sessionize import do not need to carry a token. `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses how concurrent writes are reconciled: the token turns a silent last-writer-wins into an explicit conflict the caller must resolve. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses contract explicitness: the token is part of the command, not an ambient header the handler has to go looking for.
- **Walkthrough**: two positional parameters, `EventIdentifierType Id` and `byte[]? RowVersion` (`PublishEventCommand.cs:12`), plus the computed `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`PublishEventCommand.cs:15`). There is no validation and no behavior on the record; the "already published" rule lives on the aggregate (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:226-233`).
- **Why it's built this way**: publishing is a domain transition, not a field edit, so it gets its own command rather than riding on the update request. That keeps the authorization surface, the audit log line, and the cache eviction distinct from a generic edit, and it lets the API expose a purpose-named endpoint instead of a PATCH of a boolean.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the events controller from the route id plus the request body's token (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:279`, handler injected at `:48`) and handled by [`PublishEventHandler`](#publisheventhandler). Its inverse is [`UnpublishEventCommand`](#unpublisheventcommand).

### RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.RemoveEventQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/RemoveEventQuestionAnswer/RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:9` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for deleting one answer to a conference question from an [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event). It names the owning event and the answer to drop, and nothing else.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:2,11`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) type for the cache prefix (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:1,14`), and the `EventIdentifierType` / `EventQuestionAnswerIdentifierType` aliases (both `= int`).
- **Concept introduced, the remove-child command shape.** This is the mirror of the add-child shape taught by [`AddEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#addeventquestionanswercommand), and it is deliberately narrower. An add carries the child's payload and an optional child id; a remove carries only the aggregate id plus the child id, because the child already exists and the only decision left is which one. The pair `(EventId, EventQuestionAnswerId)` is what makes the operation aggregate-scoped: the handler loads the *event* and asks it to remove the answer, so a caller cannot delete an answer by id alone and bypass the aggregate's rules. The cache prefix is again keyed on `Event`, not on the child, because a cached event read carries its answers inline. [`RemoveEventSpeakerCommand`](#removeeventspeakercommand) and [`RemoveRoomCommand`](#removeroomcommand) are the same two-identifier record with a different child. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]` assesses whether children are mutated through their root: the command's shape makes any other access path impossible to express. `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting]`: cache eviction attaches by marker interface, so no removal handler contains cache code.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed record` with two positional parameters, `EventId` and `EventQuestionAnswerId` (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:9-11`), and the single computed `CachePrefix` (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:14`). Notably there is no owner id and no `RowVersion`: ownership is derived server-side from the ambient user by [`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#removeeventquestionanswerhandler), and there is no accompanying validator class in the folder because two required identifiers have nothing to check beyond model binding.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the payload to two identifiers means the ownership rule (BR-52/BR-53) cannot be spoofed by the caller; the handler reads the answer's `CreatedBy` from the database instead of trusting the request.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the answers controller (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventQuestionAnswersController.cs:194`, handler injected at `:60`) and handled by [`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#removeeventquestionanswerhandler). Its update counterpart is [`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#updateeventquestionanswercommand).

### RemoveEventSpeakerCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.RemoveEventSpeaker` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/RemoveEventSpeaker/RemoveEventSpeakerCommand.cs:9` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for detaching a speaker from an event. It removes the [`EventSpeaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventspeaker) association row, never the speaker.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`RemoveEventSpeakerCommand.cs:2,11`), [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) for the cache prefix (`RemoveEventSpeakerCommand.cs:1,14`), and the `EventIdentifierType` / `EventSpeakerIdentifierType` aliases.
- **Concept**: nothing new; the remove-child shape introduced by [`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#removeeventquestionanswercommand), applied to the speaker association. Note that the second parameter is the `EventSpeakerIdentifierType` of the *association*, not the `SpeakerIdentifierType` of the speaker (`RemoveEventSpeakerCommand.cs:11`); that asymmetry with [`AddEventSpeakerCommand`](#addeventspeakercommand), which takes the speaker id, is what keeps the operation unambiguous when the same speaker could appear more than once on an agenda. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]`: the association is a child of the event aggregate, so deleting it leaves the independently-rooted speaker untouched.
- **Walkthrough**: `sealed record RemoveEventSpeakerCommand(EventIdentifierType EventId, EventSpeakerIdentifierType EventSpeakerId) : ICacheInvalidating` (`RemoveEventSpeakerCommand.cs:9-11`) with `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`RemoveEventSpeakerCommand.cs:14`). There is no validator class in the folder.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the event-speakers controller (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventSpeakersController.cs:215`, handler injected at `:49`) and handled by [`RemoveEventSpeakerHandler`](#removeeventspeakerhandler).

### RemoveRoomCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.RemoveRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/RemoveRoom/RemoveRoomCommand.cs:9` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for removing a [`Room`](group-17-conference-domain.md#room) from an event.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`RemoveRoomCommand.cs:2,11`), [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) for the cache prefix (`RemoveRoomCommand.cs:1,14`), and the `EventIdentifierType` / `RoomIdentifierType` aliases.
- **Concept**: the remove-child shape from [`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#removeeventquestionanswercommand) again. Unlike the speaker case, a room *is* a child of the event rather than an association to an independent aggregate, so the second identifier is the room's own id (`RemoveRoomCommand.cs:11`) and removing it removes the room itself, soft-deleted like every other entity (see the [primer](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to) on soft-delete). `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]`: the row survives as a soft-deleted record, so a session that referenced the room keeps a resolvable history.
- **Walkthrough**: `sealed record RemoveRoomCommand(EventIdentifierType EventId, RoomIdentifierType RoomId) : ICacheInvalidating` (`RemoveRoomCommand.cs:9-11`) plus `CachePrefix` (`RemoveRoomCommand.cs:14`). No validator class accompanies it.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the rooms controller (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/RoomsController.cs:205`, handler injected at `:89`) and handled by [`RemoveRoomHandler`](#removeroomhandler). Its add and update counterparts are [`AddRoomCommand`](#addroomcommand) and [`UpdateRoomCommand`](#updateroomcommand).

### UnpublishEventCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Unpublish` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Unpublish/UnpublishEventCommand.cs:12` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the exact inverse of [`PublishEventCommand`](#publisheventcommand): it returns a published [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) to draft, hiding it from attendees.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`UnpublishEventCommand.cs:2,12`), [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) for the cache prefix (`UnpublishEventCommand.cs:1,15`), and the `EventIdentifierType` alias.
- **Concept**: nothing new; the optional-concurrency-token transition command taught by [`PublishEventCommand`](#publisheventcommand). The two records are declaration-for-declaration identical apart from their names: `(EventIdentifierType Id, byte[]? RowVersion = null)` (`UnpublishEventCommand.cs:12`) and the same `CachePrefix` (`UnpublishEventCommand.cs:15`). They are kept as two types rather than one `SetPublishedCommand(bool)` because each maps to a distinct domain method with its own invariant and its own log line, and because a named endpoint is easier to authorize and to audit than a boolean argument.
- **Walkthrough**: two positional parameters and one computed property, all at `UnpublishEventCommand.cs:12-16`. The "already unpublished" rule lives on the aggregate (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:246-253`).
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the events controller (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:301`, handler injected at `:49`) and handled by [`UnpublishEventHandler`](#unpublisheventhandler).

### UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.UpdateEventQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/UpdateEventQuestionAnswer/UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:10` · Level 8 · record (sealed)

- **What it is**: the write intent for editing the text of an existing answer to a conference question on an [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event).
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:2,13`), [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) for the cache prefix (`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:1,16`), and the `EventIdentifierType` / `EventQuestionAnswerIdentifierType` aliases.
- **Concept**: the update-child shape, which sits between the add and remove shapes: it carries the aggregate id and the child id like a remove, plus exactly the one field that may change. `AnswerValue` is a non-nullable `string` (`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:13`), so a caller cannot use this command to blank an answer by omission; clearing an answer is a removal. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether an edit contract states precisely what is mutable: the record is the whole answer of "what can this endpoint change".
- **Walkthrough**: three positional parameters, `EventId`, `EventQuestionAnswerId`, and `AnswerValue` (`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:10-13`), with `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand.cs:16`). Like the remove command it carries no owner id, because the BR-52/BR-53 ownership check is resolved server-side.
- **Why it's built this way**: a narrow, single-field update command keeps the write surface auditable and lets the caching decorator evict on a well-defined boundary; a general "patch the answer entity" contract would have neither property.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by the answers controller from the request body and route id (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventQuestionAnswersController.cs:178`, handler injected at `:59`) and handled by [`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#updateeventquestionanswerhandler).

### EventCreateRequestMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Create/EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:11` · Level 9 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the one adapter that turns an [`EventCreateRequest`](#eventcreaterequest) into an [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) domain entity, by calling the aggregate's `Create` factory and returning whatever [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) it produces.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) from `MMCA.Common.Application.Interfaces` (`EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:2,11-12`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate and its `EventIdentifierType` alias (`EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:1`), and [`Result<T>`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) from `MMCA.Common.Shared.Abstractions` (`EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:3,15`).
- **Concept introduced, request-to-entity mapping as a separate injectable role.** The generic create pipeline never constructs entities itself; it resolves an `IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>` and asks it for one. That indirection is what lets one handler shape serve every aggregate while each aggregate keeps its own construction rules. Two properties of this mapper matter. First, it is a plain `sealed class` with no `[Mapper]` attribute (`EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:11`): unlike the read-side DTO mappers, request-to-entity conversion is deliberately hand-written, because it must go through a factory that can *fail*, which a property-copy generator cannot express. Second, it returns `Task<Result<Event>>` rather than an `Event`, so an invalid request produces a failure value that flows back through the handler as a 400-class response instead of an exception. `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]` assesses whether the domain stays independent of the delivery mechanism: the controller knows a request type, the domain knows a factory, and this class is the only thing that knows both. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]`: the factory stays the single construction path, so no invariant can be bypassed by `new`. See [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html) for the no-reflection mapping policy.
- **Walkthrough**: one method. `CreateEntityAsync(EventCreateRequest request, CancellationToken)` (`EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:15`) null-guards with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(request)` (`EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:17`), then returns `Task.FromResult(Event.Create(...))` forwarding all ten request fields positionally, `Id`, `Name`, `Description`, `StartDate`, `EndDate`, `TimeZone`, `SessionizeCode`, `VenueAddress`, `VenueMapUrl`, `WiFiInfo` (`EventCreateRequestMapper.cs:19-29`). The factory's eleventh parameter, `questionModerationDefault`, is not forwarded and takes its declared default of `QuestionModerationDefault.Pending` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:142`), so a newly created event starts with moderated questions. The method is synchronous in substance: `Task.FromResult` satisfies the async contract without allocating a state machine, because the factory does no I/O. The forwarded `Id` is discarded by the factory when the aggregate's key is store-generated (`Event.cs:151,164`).
- **Why it's built this way**: pushing construction into `Event.Create` means invariant checks (`EventInvariants.EnsureNameIsValid`, `EnsureTimeZoneIsValid`, `EnsureDateRangeIsValid`, combined at `Event.cs:144-147`) run even for callers that never touch the HTTP layer, such as the Sessionize import. The mapper adds no rules of its own, which is exactly what makes it safe to have several entry points.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`CreateEventHandler`](#createeventhandler) as `IEntityRequestMapper<Event, EventCreateRequest, EventIdentifierType>` (`CreateEventHandler.cs:18`), registered by the module's assembly scanning rather than an explicit `AddScoped` line (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs`).

### EventCreateRequestValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Create/EventCreateRequestValidator.cs:7` · Level 9 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator the pipeline runs against an [`EventCreateRequest`](#eventcreaterequest) before [`CreateEventHandler`](#createeventhandler) sees it. It contains no rules of its own: it is three `Include` calls.
- **Depends on**: `FluentValidation.AbstractValidator<T>` (NuGet, `EventCreateRequestValidator.cs:1,7`) and the three shared rule objects [`EventNameRules<T>`](#eventnamerulest), [`EventTimeZoneRules<T>`](#eventtimezonerulest), and [`EventDateRangeRules<T>`](#eventdaterangerulest) from `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.Validation` (`EventCreateRequestValidator.cs:2,11-13`).
- **Concept introduced, rule composition by `Include` with a property selector.** FluentValidation's `Include(otherValidator)` copies every rule from another `AbstractValidator<T>` into this one, and it requires both validators to be generic over the *same* `T`. That is why the rule classes are generic in the containing type and take an expression selector in their constructor: `new EventNameRules<EventCreateRequest>(p => p.Name)` (`EventCreateRequestValidator.cs:11`) says "apply the event-name rules to *this* type's `Name` property". The same three rule classes are re-included by [`EventUpdateRequestValidator`](#eventupdaterequestvalidator) against a different request type, so a length or format constraint is written once and both slices inherit it: there is no way for create and update to drift apart on what a valid event name is. Note also which rules exist and which do not: name, time zone, and the start-before-end date range are validated, while the optional URL and venue fields are not constrained here. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` assesses whether invalid input is rejected at the boundary with actionable messages: the request never reaches the aggregate when a rule fails. `[Rubric §16, Maintainability]`: a shared rule object is one edit point instead of N. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: each rule class has one reason to change, and validators compose them rather than inherit a fat base.
- **Walkthrough**: the whole type is a constructor. `EventCreateRequestValidator()` (`EventCreateRequestValidator.cs:9-14`) calls `Include(new EventNameRules<EventCreateRequest>(p => p.Name))` (`:11`), `Include(new EventTimeZoneRules<EventCreateRequest>(p => p.TimeZone))` (`:12`), and `Include(new EventDateRangeRules<EventCreateRequest>(p => p.StartDate, p => p.EndDate))` (`:13`). The date-range rule takes two selectors because it is a cross-field rule, which is the reason it cannot be expressed as a per-property attribute. The rule bodies live in `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/Validation/EventValidationRules.cs:13,25,55` and delegate to the same `EventInvariants` constants the domain factory uses.
- **Why it's built this way**: validating at the pipeline boundary gives the caller a complete, field-addressed error list in one round trip, while the domain factory keeps its own invariant checks as the backstop for non-HTTP callers. The duplication is intentional and cheap because both sides read the same `EventInvariants` limits.
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the validation decorator around [`CreateEventHandler`](#createeventhandler); the update-side sibling is [`EventUpdateRequestValidator`](#eventupdaterequestvalidator).

### PublishEventHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Publish` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Publish/PublishEventHandler.cs:13` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`PublishEventCommand`](#publisheventcommand). It loads the event, stamps the client's concurrency token, asks the aggregate to publish itself, and saves only if the aggregate agreed.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`PublishEventHandler.cs:3,14`), `ILogger<T>` with a source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` (`PublishEventHandler.cs:1,15,41-42`), [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`PublishEventHandler.cs:4,15`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate (`PublishEventHandler.cs:2`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) / [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`PublishEventHandler.cs:5`).
- **Concept introduced, applying an optimistic-concurrency token to a loaded entity.** The interesting line is `repository.SetOriginalRowVersion(entity, command.RowVersion)` (`PublishEventHandler.cs:29`). EF Core decides whether an `UPDATE` succeeded by comparing the row's *original* tracked `rowversion` against the database. Normally the original value is whatever the row had when it was just loaded, which is by definition current, so a concurrency check would always pass. This call overwrites the tracked original with the token the *client* last saw ([`IRepository<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#irepositorytentity-tidentifiertype) declares it at `MMCA.Common.Application/Interfaces/Infrastructure/IRepository.cs:173`), so a decision made against a stale view fails the save and surfaces as 409 Conflict rather than silently overwriting a concurrent edit. Passing `null` is the documented way to skip the check (`PublishEventHandler.cs:27-29`). This is [ADR-035](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/035-optimistic-concurrency.html) in three lines. `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses concurrency and consistency handling: the check is in the database, not in an application-side read-then-compare that could itself race. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the source-generated log message means the transition is recorded with structured fields at zero allocation when the level is disabled.
- **Walkthrough**: primary-constructor injection of `IUnitOfWork` and `ILogger<PublishEventHandler>` (`PublishEventHandler.cs:13-15`), and the class implements `ICommandHandler<PublishEventCommand, Result>`. `HandleAsync` (`PublishEventHandler.cs:18-39`) gets the typed repository from the unit of work (`:22`), loads by id with the default no-tracking-free read (`:23`), and returns `Error.NotFound.WithSource(nameof(PublishEventHandler)).WithTarget(nameof(Event))` when the row is absent (`:24-25`). Note there is no `includes` array and no `asTracking: true` here: a publish touches a scalar on the root only, unlike the child-removal handlers below. It then stamps the row version (`:29`), calls `entity.Publish()` (`:31`), and only on success awaits `unitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync(cancellationToken)` and logs `LogEventPublished` (`:32-36`). The aggregate's own [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) is returned unchanged (`:38`), so an "already published" invariant failure (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:228-232`) reaches the caller with its domain error code intact. `LogEventPublished` is declared `partial` with `[LoggerMessage(Level = LogLevel.Information, ...)]` (`:41-42`) and its body is generated at build time.
- **Why it's built this way**: the save-only-on-success shape is the module's canonical command body. It means a rejected transition writes nothing at all, so the single `SaveChangesAsync` stays the one boundary that stamps audit fields, captures domain events, and writes the outbox row for [`EventChanged`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventchanged) raised inside `Event.Publish` (`Event.cs:237`).
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the events controller as `ICommandHandler<PublishEventCommand, Result>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:48`) and invoked at `:279`. Its inverse is [`UnpublishEventHandler`](#unpublisheventhandler).

### RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.RemoveEventQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/RemoveEventQuestionAnswer/RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:14` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#removeeventquestionanswercommand). Beyond the usual load-mutate-save shape it enforces BR-52/BR-53: an Attendee may delete only their own answer, an Organizer may delete any.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:3,15`), [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:6,16`), `ILogger<T>` (`:17,54-55`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate and its [`EventQuestionAnswer`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswer) child (`:2`), [`RoleNames`](group-08-auth.md#rolenames) (`:6,35`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) / [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:5`).
- **Concept introduced, row-level ownership authorization inside the handler.** Role-based authorization on the controller can answer "may this caller call this endpoint"; it cannot answer "may this caller touch *this row*". That second question needs the row, so it is asked here, after the aggregate is loaded and before the mutation. The check has three moving parts (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:34-42`): find the non-deleted answer by id in the loaded collection, ask [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) whether the caller holds the Organizer role, and compare the answer's audit field `CreatedBy` with the caller's `UserId`. The identity is read from the ambient authenticated principal, never from the command, which is why [`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#removeeventquestionanswercommand) has no owner field to spoof. Failure returns `Error.Forbidden(code: "EventQuestionAnswer.NotOwner", ...)` (`:37-41`), a distinct error kind that the API layer maps to 403 rather than 404. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether authorization is enforced where the data is, not only at the edge: the ownership decision is made against the persisted `CreatedBy`, so a forged request cannot win it. `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting]`: the ambient user arrives through one injected abstraction, so the same rule reads identically in the update sibling.
- **Walkthrough**: primary-constructor injection of the unit of work, the current-user service, and the logger (`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:14-17`). `HandleAsync` (`:20-52`) loads the event *with its answers and with tracking on*, `includes: [nameof(Event.EventQuestionAnswers)], asTracking: true` (`:25-29`), which is required because the removal walks the child collection and EF must be tracking it for the change to be persisted. Missing event yields `Error.NotFound` (`:30-31`). The ownership check follows (`:34-42`); note it is written defensively as `answer is not null && ...`, so a request naming an answer that does not exist falls through to the domain method rather than returning 403, and the domain reports the not-found condition. `entity.RemoveEventQuestionAnswer(command.EventQuestionAnswerId)` (`:44`) performs the soft delete inside the aggregate, and only on success does the handler save and log (`:45-49`). The result of the domain call is returned as-is (`:51`).
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the ownership rule in the handler rather than in the aggregate keeps `Event` free of any notion of "who is calling", which is an application concern, while keeping it out of the controller means every caller of this command gets the rule, including future non-HTTP entry points.
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the answers controller as `ICommandHandler<RemoveEventQuestionAnswerCommand, Result>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventQuestionAnswersController.cs:60`) and invoked at `:194`. Its update sibling is [`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#updateeventquestionanswerhandler); its add counterpart is [`AddEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#addeventquestionanswerhandler).

### RemoveEventSpeakerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.RemoveEventSpeaker` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/RemoveEventSpeaker/RemoveEventSpeakerHandler.cs:13` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveEventSpeakerCommand`](#removeeventspeakercommand). It loads the event with its speaker associations and delegates the removal to the aggregate.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`RemoveEventSpeakerHandler.cs:3,14`), `ILogger<T>` (`:15,41-42`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate (`:2`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) / [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:5`).
- **Concept**: the canonical child-removal handler body, and the clearest place to see the include-plus-tracking rule that separates removal handlers from the transition handlers above. `GetByIdAsync(command.EventId, includes: [nameof(Event.EventSpeakers)], asTracking: true, ...)` (`RemoveEventSpeakerHandler.cs:23-27`) is load-bearing in both halves: without the `includes` the child collection is empty and the domain method finds nothing to remove, and without `asTracking: true` the change would be made on an untracked graph and silently lost at `SaveChangesAsync`. Compare [`PublishEventHandler`](#publisheventhandler), which needs neither because it only touches a root scalar. Unlike its question-answer sibling there is no ownership check here: detaching a speaker is an organizer operation authorized at the controller. `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]` assesses whether the aggregate owns its invariants: the handler never manipulates the collection itself, it calls `entity.RemoveEventSpeaker(...)` (`:31`). `[Rubric §14, Testability]`: with only a unit of work and a logger injected, the handler is exercised in the unit tier against a faked repository (see `AddEventSpeakerHandlerTests` in [Group 27](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md)).
- **Walkthrough**: primary-constructor injection (`RemoveEventSpeakerHandler.cs:13-15`), implementing `ICommandHandler<RemoveEventSpeakerCommand, Result>`. `HandleAsync` (`:18-39`) resolves the repository (`:22`), loads with includes and tracking (`:23-27`), returns `Error.NotFound.WithSource(nameof(RemoveEventSpeakerHandler)).WithTarget(nameof(Event))` when absent (`:28-29`), calls `entity.RemoveEventSpeaker(command.EventSpeakerId)` (`:31`), and on success saves and emits the generated `LogSpeakerRemovedFromEvent` (`:32-36`, declaration at `:41-42`). The domain result is returned unchanged (`:38`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the `WithSource`/`WithTarget` decoration on `Error.NotFound` is what makes a generic error usable in an operational log: the response carries which handler decided and which type was missing, without a bespoke error type per use case.
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the event-speakers controller as `ICommandHandler<RemoveEventSpeakerCommand, Result>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventSpeakersController.cs:49`) and invoked at `:215`. Its add counterpart is [`AddEventSpeakerHandler`](#addeventspeakerhandler).

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.RemoveRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/RemoveRoom/RemoveRoomHandler.cs:13` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveRoomCommand`](#removeroomcommand). Structurally identical to [`RemoveEventSpeakerHandler`](#removeeventspeakerhandler), differing only in which collection it includes and which domain method it calls.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`RemoveRoomHandler.cs:3,14`), `ILogger<T>` (`:15,41-42`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate (`:2`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) / [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:5`).
- **Concept**: nothing new; the child-removal shape taught by [`RemoveEventSpeakerHandler`](#removeeventspeakerhandler). The one substitution is `includes: [nameof(Event.Rooms)]` (`RemoveRoomHandler.cs:25`) and `entity.RemoveRoom(command.RoomId)` (`:31`). Reading the three removal handlers side by side is the fastest way to internalize the convention: the only things that vary between them are the included navigation, the domain method, and the log message.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`RemoveRoomHandler.cs:18-39`) resolves the repository (`:22`), loads the event with `includes: [nameof(Event.Rooms)]` and `asTracking: true` (`:23-27`), fails with a decorated `Error.NotFound` when absent (`:28-29`), calls the domain method (`:31`), and saves plus logs `LogRoomRemoved` only on success (`:32-36`, declaration `:41-42`).
- **Why it's built this way**: removing a room is not a cascade the handler orchestrates; the aggregate decides what a removal means for the room and for anything referencing it, which is why the handler stays this thin.
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the rooms controller as `ICommandHandler<RemoveRoomCommand, Result>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/RoomsController.cs:89`) and invoked at `:205`. Its add and update counterparts are [`AddRoomHandler`](#addroomhandler) and [`UpdateRoomHandler`](#updateroomhandler).

### UnpublishEventHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Unpublish` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Unpublish/UnpublishEventHandler.cs:13` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`UnpublishEventCommand`](#unpublisheventcommand), the mirror image of [`PublishEventHandler`](#publisheventhandler).
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`UnpublishEventHandler.cs:3,14`), `ILogger<T>` (`:15,41-42`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate (`:2`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) / [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error) (`:5`).
- **Concept**: nothing new; the concurrency-checked transition handler taught by [`PublishEventHandler`](#publisheventhandler). The bodies are line-for-line the same apart from three substitutions: the command type, `entity.Unpublish()` in place of `entity.Publish()` (`UnpublishEventHandler.cs:31`), and `LogEventUnpublished` in place of `LogEventPublished` (`:35,41-42`). The `SetOriginalRowVersion` stamp with its ADR-035 comment appears verbatim at `:27-29`.
- **Walkthrough**: `HandleAsync` (`UnpublishEventHandler.cs:18-39`) resolves the repository (`:22`), loads by id with no includes and no tracking flag (`:23`), returns `Error.NotFound.WithSource(nameof(UnpublishEventHandler)).WithTarget(nameof(Event))` when absent (`:24-25`), stamps the client token (`:29`), calls `entity.Unpublish()` (`:31`), and saves plus logs only on success (`:32-36`). The aggregate rejects the call with `Error.Invariant(code: "Event.AlreadyUnpublished", ...)` when the event is already a draft (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:248-252`), and that failure is returned unchanged (`:38`).
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping publish and unpublish as two symmetric handlers rather than one parameterized handler costs a few duplicated lines and buys two independently authorizable endpoints, two distinct log events, and two domain methods each guarding its own precondition.
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the events controller as `ICommandHandler<UnpublishEventCommand, Result>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:49`) and invoked at `:301`.

### UpdateEventQuestionAnswerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.UpdateEventQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/UpdateEventQuestionAnswer/UpdateEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:14` · Level 9 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerCommand`](#updateeventquestionanswercommand). It runs the same BR-52/BR-53 ownership check as the removal sibling, then delegates the edit to the aggregate.

- **Concept**: nothing new; the row-level ownership check introduced by [`RemoveEventQuestionAnswerHandler`](#removeeventquestionanswerhandler), applied to an edit. The check is byte-for-byte the same predicate (`UpdateEventQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:34-42`) with only the message changed to "You can only update your own answers." (`:39`) and the same `EventQuestionAnswer.NotOwner` error code reused (`:38`). Sharing the code across delete and update is intentional: a client can branch on one code for both operations. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: the answer's persisted `CreatedBy` audit field, not any request value, is what the caller's `UserId` is compared against. `[Rubric §16, Maintainability]`: the duplication between the two handlers is small and local, which is the trade chosen over an abstraction that would obscure a security-relevant rule.

The domain result is returned unchanged (`:53`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the aggregate, not the handler, decides whether the target answer exists and whether the new value is acceptable, so the handler's entire contribution is authorization plus persistence orchestration.
