### CreateEventHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Create/CreateEventHandler.cs:16` · Level 10 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler that creates a conference [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event). It is the highest-level type in this unit because it composes four of the others: the request, the request mapper, the repository, and the read-side DTO mapper.
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (`CreateEventHandler.cs:6,17`), [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) satisfied by [`EventCreateRequestMapper`](#eventcreaterequestmapper) (`CreateEventHandler.cs:5,18`), [`EventDTOMapper`](#eventdtomapper) (`:2,19`), `ILogger<T>` (`:1,20,42-43`), [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) (`:7,20`), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregate (`:3`), [`EventDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventdto) from the Shared project (`:4`), and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) (`:8`).
- **Concept introduced, the generic create slice assembled end to end.** Read the four injected dependencies as a pipeline (`CreateEventHandler.cs:16-20`): the request mapper turns the wire contract into a validated entity, the unit of work supplies the typed repository and owns the transaction boundary, and the DTO mapper turns the persisted entity back into a wire contract. The handler itself contributes only sequencing and a log line, and there is no `new Event(...)` anywhere in it. Note also what is *absent*: no validator call (the pipeline's validation decorator already ran [`EventCreateRequestValidator`](#eventcreaterequestvalidator)), no cache eviction (the caching decorator reads `CachePrefix` off [`EventCreateRequest`](#eventcreaterequest)), and no `try`/`catch` (failures arrive as [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) values). That absence is the point of the decorator pipeline taught in [Group 05](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md). `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` assesses whether a use case is self-contained: the four Create types live in one folder and this handler is the slice's entry point. `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]`: the Application layer depends on abstractions (`IUnitOfWork`, `IEntityRequestMapper`) and on the Domain, never on EF Core or ASP.NET. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]`: one command type, one handler, one write path, and the `EventChanged` domain event raised inside the factory (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:168`) is captured by the same `SaveChangesAsync`.
- **Walkthrough**: primary-constructor injection of the four collaborators (`CreateEventHandler.cs:16-20`), declaring `ICommandHandler<EventCreateRequest, Result<EventDTO>>`. `HandleAsync` (`:23-40`) awaits `requestMapper.CreateEntityAsync(command, cancellationToken)` (`:27`) and short-circuits on failure by re-wrapping the errors into the correct generic shape, `Result.Failure<EventDTO>(result.Errors)` (`:28-29`), which is how a factory-level invariant failure becomes an API error without an exception. It then unwraps `result.Value!` (`:31`), gets `unitOfWork.GetRepository<Event, EventIdentifierType>()` (`:32`), awaits `repository.AddAsync(entity, cancellationToken)` and then the single `unitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync(cancellationToken)` (`:34-35`), both with `.ConfigureAwait(false)`. After the save it emits the generated `LogEventCreated(logger, entity.Id, entity.Name)` (`:37`, declaration `:42-43`), which is placed after the save so the logged id is the store-generated key rather than a placeholder. It returns `Result.Success(dtoMapper.MapToDTO(entity))` (`:39`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the single `SaveChangesAsync` is the one place audit fields are stamped, domain events are captured, and outbox rows are written, so the handler deliberately owns exactly one call to it. Returning an [`EventDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventdto) rather than the entity keeps the domain type from crossing the API boundary, per [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html).
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the events controller as `ICommandHandler<EventCreateRequest, Result<EventDTO>>` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:46`) behind the create action at `:233`. Its update and delete counterparts are [`UpdateEventHandler`](#updateeventhandler) and [`DeleteEventHandler`](#deleteeventhandler).

### AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.AddSpeakerCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/AddSpeakerCategoryItem/AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommand.cs:13` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the write message that associates a category item (a `SpeakerCategoryItem` join row) with an existing speaker. Category items are how a speaker's topics and locality are modeled, so this is the command behind "tag this speaker".
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (marker); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) (referenced only to build the cache prefix); the module identifier aliases `SpeakerIdentifierType`, `SpeakerCategoryItemIdentifierType`, and `CategoryItemIdentifierType` (see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)).
- **Concept introduced, the cache-invalidating child-add command.** `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` assesses whether writes are modeled as distinct, single-purpose messages rather than fat service calls; this record is pure write intent with no behavior beyond declaring which cached reads it should evict. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses caching discipline: implementing [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) with `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Speaker).FullName}:"` (`AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommand.cs:19`) is what lets [`CachingCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#cachingcommanddecoratortcommand-tresult) drop every cached read keyed under the `Speaker` type once the write succeeds. The eviction runs on success only and outside the transaction, so a rolled-back write never blows the cache.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed record` with a three-parameter positional constructor (`AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommand.cs:13-16`): `SpeakerId` (the aggregate to load), a nullable `SpeakerCategoryItemId` (`:15`, where `null` means the database generates the join-row identity), and `CategoryItemId` (the item to attach). Its single member is the read-only `CachePrefix` (`:19`).
- **Why it's built this way**: records give value equality and immutability for free, and keeping the cache tag on the message rather than in the handler means the pipeline decorator, not the handler, owns eviction (the decorator order is spelled out in [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). The nullable identity parameter exists because the same aggregate method serves both an interactive create (let the database assign) and an import that carries a source-system id.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by `SpeakerCategoryItemsController` with an explicit `null` for the join-row id (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/SpeakerCategoryItemsController.cs:193`, dispatched through the handler injected at `:49`); validated by [`AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommandValidator`](#addspeakercategoryitemcommandvalidator); handled by [`AddSpeakerCategoryItemHandler`](#addspeakercategoryitemhandler).

### QuestionCreateRequest

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Questions.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/UseCases/Create/QuestionCreateRequest.cs:10` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the create-request DTO for a conference question (the reusable question definitions that feedback forms are built from). It doubles as the command: [`CreateQuestionHandler`](#createquestionhandler) implements `ICommandHandler<QuestionCreateRequest, ...>` directly against this type, so there is no separate `CreateQuestionCommand`.
- **Depends on**: [`ICreateRequest`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) (an empty marker used as a generic constraint by [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype)); [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating); [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) (cache prefix only); the `QuestionIdentifierType` alias.
- **Concept introduced, the request-as-command shape.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether the wire contract is an explicit, versionable type rather than the domain entity leaking outward: the controller binds this record straight from the request body (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/QuestionsController.cs:93`) and it is also the generic argument of the base controller (`:38`). `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` applies because the same object is what the pipeline dispatches. Marking it `ICacheInvalidating` (`QuestionCreateRequest.cs:13`) with the `Question` prefix means a successful create evicts the cached question reads exactly like the command records elsewhere in this group.
- **Walkthrough**: a `record class` (not `sealed`) with `init`-only members. `CachePrefix` (`:13`) is the invalidation tag. `Id` (`:16`) is present for contract symmetry but its XML comment states plainly that the handler auto-generates it and caller-provided values are ignored. `QuestionText` (`:19`) is the one `required` member. `QuestionEntity` (`:22`) and `QuestionType` (`:25`) are nullable strings that the domain later constrains to a fixed vocabulary. `Sort` (`:28`) drives display order and `IsRequired` (`:31`) marks the question mandatory.
- **Why it's built this way**: `init`-only members plus `required` on the one non-negotiable field give compile-time enforcement of the minimum payload while leaving the rest optional, and collapsing request and command into one type keeps a simple create slice to a single message (contrast the child-add flows, where the controller builds a distinct command record).
- **Where it's used**: bound by `QuestionsController` ([Group 20](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#questionscontroller)); validated by [`QuestionCreateRequestValidator`](#questioncreaterequestvalidator); translated to a domain entity by [`QuestionCreateRequestMapper`](#questioncreaterequestmapper); handled by [`CreateQuestionHandler`](#createquestionhandler).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `Id` is a settable part of the public contract even though it is always overwritten. The override is visible in the handler (`CreateQuestionHandler.cs:87`), not on this type, so reading the record alone would suggest the caller controls the id.

### QuestionDTOMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Questions.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/DTOs/QuestionDTOMapper.cs:12` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: a Mapperly source-generated mapper that projects a [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) domain entity onto a [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto) for the API boundary.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) (the framework contract); [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) and [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto); the `QuestionIdentifierType` alias; Riok.Mapperly's `[Mapper]` attribute (NuGet, source generator).
- **Concept introduced, compile-time DTO mapping via Mapperly.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether domain-to-contract translation is explicit and stable rather than implicit. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` and `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]` also apply: Mapperly emits the field-copy code at build time, so there is no runtime reflection, the mapping is steppable in a debugger, and an unmapped member is a build-time diagnostic instead of a silently dropped field. This is the shape every leaf mapper in the Conference module repeats.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed partial class` decorated with `[Mapper]` (`QuestionDTOMapper.cs:11-13`) implementing the three-parameter framework interface. The single-entity method is declared but not written: `public partial QuestionDTO MapToDTO(Question entity)` (`:16`), and the generator supplies the body. The collection overload `MapToDTOs` (`:19-23`) is hand-written: it null-guards with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull` (`:21`) and projects with a collection expression over the generated single-entity method (`[.. entityCollection.Select(MapToDTO)]`, `:22`).
- **Why it's built this way**: manual DTO mapping, here delegated to a generator rather than a runtime engine, is the framework convention per [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html). A dedicated mapper per entity keeps the projection next to the type it maps and gives the DI scan a concrete scoped service to register.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`CreateQuestionHandler`](#createquestionhandler) (`CreateQuestionHandler.cs:23`) to project the created entity, and resolved by the question read path for list and detail responses.

### UpdateRoomCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.UpdateRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/UpdateRoom/UpdateRoomCommand.cs:15` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the command that edits a room inside an event. A `Room` is a child of the `Event` aggregate, so the message must carry both ids: the aggregate to load and the child to change.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating); [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) (cache prefix); the `EventIdentifierType` and `RoomIdentifierType` aliases.
- **Concept reinforced, the cache-invalidating child-mutation command.** Same contract as [`AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommand`](#addspeakercategoryitemcommand), but the prefix is the parent aggregate's: `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Event).FullName}:"` (`UpdateRoomCommand.cs:26`). That is the interesting detail. Rooms have no independent cache namespace, so editing a room invalidates the whole `Event` cache surface, which is what keeps a stale nested room out of an already-cached event read. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]`, `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed record` with eight positional parameters (`UpdateRoomCommand.cs:15-23`): `EventId` and `RoomId` locate the child; `Name` (`:18`) and `Sort` (`:19`) are required scalars; `Capacity` (`:20`), `Floor` (`:21`), `Location` (`:22`), and `AccessibilityInfo` (`:23`) are the optional organizer-entered details. The parameter list is exactly the argument list of `Event.UpdateRoom` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:368-375`), so the handler forwards without reshaping.
- **Why it's built this way**: modeling rooms as children of `Event` rather than as their own aggregate means every room write goes through the parent, which is what lets the domain raise a single `RoomChanged` event with the owning event id (`Event.cs:386`) and keeps room ordering and uniqueness decisions in one place.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by `RoomsController` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/RoomsController.cs:179`, handler injected at `:88`); validated by [`UpdateRoomCommandValidator`](#updateroomcommandvalidator); handled by [`UpdateRoomHandler`](#updateroomhandler).

### AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommandValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.AddSpeakerCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/AddSpeakerCategoryItem/AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommandValidator.cs:8` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: a single-rule FluentValidation validator that guards [`AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommand`](#addspeakercategoryitemcommand) before its handler runs.
- **Depends on**: FluentValidation's `AbstractValidator<T>` (NuGet); the `CategoryItemIdentifierType` alias.
- **Concept introduced, input validation at the command boundary.** `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` assesses whether input is checked before business logic executes and whether the message returned is usable by a UI. The class is `sealed` with an expression-bodied constructor holding one rule (`AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommandValidator.cs:10-13`): `RuleFor(x => x.CategoryItemId).NotEqual(default(CategoryItemIdentifierType)).WithMessage("Category item ID is required.")`. It is discovered by the module's assembly scan and run by [`ValidatingCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#validatingcommanddecoratortcommand-tresult), which sits ahead of the transactional decorator, so a malformed command never opens a database transaction.
- **Why it's built this way**: only the cheap shape check lives here. The deeper rule (is this category item already attached to the speaker?) needs the loaded aggregate and therefore lives in `Speaker.AddSpeakerCategoryItem`, which returns the `Speaker.CategoryItem.Duplicate` invariant error (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Speakers/Speaker.cs:299-305`). Splitting "is the request well-formed?" from "is the operation allowed?" keeps each concern in one place, and a new command is wired simply by the existence of a validator class.
- **Where it's used**: run by the validating decorator ahead of [`AddSpeakerCategoryItemHandler`](#addspeakercategoryitemhandler).

### AddSpeakerCategoryItemHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.AddSpeakerCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/AddSpeakerCategoryItem/AddSpeakerCategoryItemHandler.cs:15` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the handler that attaches a category item to a speaker. It is the canonical "load aggregate, call domain method, save, map" template with no extra guards, and it is worth reading first because most write handlers in this module are variations on it.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) and [`SpeakerCategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakercategoryitem); [`SpeakerCategoryItemDTOMapper`](#speakercategoryitemdtomapper); `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging`.
- **Concept introduced, the load-then-delegate template plus source-generated logging.** `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` assesses whether each write is a thin, self-contained slice: command, validator, and handler sit in one folder and this file is the whole write path. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` assesses the handler contract, here `ICommandHandler<AddSpeakerCategoryItemCommand, Result<SpeakerCategoryItemDTO>>` (`:18`). `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses structured logging: the class is `sealed partial` so the `[LoggerMessage]` attribute (`:41-42`) can generate a strongly typed, allocation-free `LogCategoryItemAdded` helper whose message template names its parameters, which is what makes the log queryable in App Insights rather than a formatted string.
- **Walkthrough**
  - Primary constructor (`:15-18`): [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), a [`SpeakerCategoryItemDTOMapper`](#speakercategoryitemdtomapper), and `ILogger<AddSpeakerCategoryItemHandler>`. Note the repository is not injected: it is resolved from the unit of work (`:25`), which is the framework's rule for keeping one tracked context per scope.
  - `HandleAsync` (`:21`) resolves `IRepository<Speaker, SpeakerIdentifierType>` and loads the aggregate with the include-free overload `GetByIdAsync(command.SpeakerId, cancellationToken)` (`:26`). That overload queries the tracked `Table` deliberately (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/Repositories/EFReadRepository.cs:154-166`), so the entity is change-tracked and the later save is not a silent no-op.
  - A missing speaker returns `Error.NotFound` decorated with `WithSource(...)` and `WithTarget(...)` (`:27-28`), which is how the API layer later maps a domain failure onto a 404 without the handler knowing about HTTP.
  - It delegates the decision to `speaker.AddSpeakerCategoryItem(command.SpeakerCategoryItemId, command.CategoryItemId)` (`:30`); a failed [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) is passed straight through with its error list intact (`:31-32`).
  - On success it saves through the unit of work with `ConfigureAwait(false)` (`:34`), logs via the generated helper (`:36`), and returns `Result.Success(dtoMapper.MapToDTO(result.Value!))` (`:38`).
- **Why it's built this way**: pushing "may this category item be added?" into the aggregate keeps the handler as pure orchestration, so the interesting invariant stays unit-testable on [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) with no database, and the same shape recurs across the module. Returning a `Result` rather than throwing is the framework-wide error convention (see [Group 1](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result)).
- **Where it's used**: registered by the Conference application DI scan; invoked through the decorator pipeline for `SpeakerCategoryItemsController` posts ([Group 20](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakercategoryitemscontroller)).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the aggregate's duplicate check reads its `_speakerCategoryItems` collection (`Speaker.cs:299`), but this handler loads the speaker without an explicit include list, so whether that collection is populated depends on the navigation-population behavior described in [ADR-002](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/002-navigation-populators.html) rather than on anything visible in this file.

### CreateQuestionHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Questions.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/UseCases/Create/CreateQuestionHandler.cs:19` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the most interesting handler in this unit. It creates a question, but because question ids are shared with the Sessionize import, it must allocate an id out of a reserved range and cope with a concurrent create that grabs the same one.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork); `IServiceScopeFactory` (Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection); [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) resolved for [`QuestionCreateRequestMapper`](#questioncreaterequestmapper); [`QuestionDTOMapper`](#questiondtomapper); [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) and [`QuestionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questioninvariants); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept introduced, application-allocated ids in a reserved range with optimistic retry.** `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses how identity is assigned when a table is shared between an external system of record and local writes: Sessionize owns the low id space, so user-created questions are allocated from `QuestionInvariants.ManualIdRangeStart` (999,999,000) to `QuestionInvariants.ManualIdRangeEnd` (999,999,999) (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Questions/QuestionInvariants.cs:37` and `:40`). `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses recovery from a losing race: read-max-then-insert is not atomic, so instead of locking, the handler catches the resulting unique-key violation and retries with a recomputed id. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` applies through the two `[LoggerMessage]` helpers, one informational for the create (`:119-120`) and one warning that names the attempt number when a collision is retried (`:122-123`).
- **Walkthrough**
  - Primary constructor (`:19-24`): the unit of work, an `IServiceScopeFactory`, the request mapper, the DTO mapper, and the logger. `MaxManualIdAttempts` is a `private const int` of 3 (`:27`).
  - `HandleAsync` (`:30`) is a `while (true)` loop over attempts (`:38`). The first attempt runs `CreateCoreAsync` against the ambient unit of work (`:43-44`). Every later attempt opens a fresh DI scope (`:48`) and resolves a scoped [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) from it (`:49`), because the ambient `DbContext` still tracks the failed insert and would replay it.
  - The `catch` filter (`:52`) is the retry gate: it re-runs only while `attempt < MaxManualIdAttempts` and `IsUniqueKeyViolation(ex)` is true. Anything else, including the fourth collision, propagates.
  - `CreateCoreAsync` (`:64`) resolves the repository (`:69`), then reads every existing question inside the reserved range with `ignoreQueryFilters: true` (`:73-77`) so a soft-deleted question still reserves its id. `nextId` is `Max(Id) + 1`, or the range start when the range is empty (`:79-81`).
  - Range exhaustion returns a domain-style failure rather than throwing: `Error.Failure(nameof(CreateQuestionHandler), "Manual question ID range exhausted.")` (`:83-84`).
  - The caller's id is then unconditionally replaced with a `with` expression (`:87`), the request mapper turns the record into a [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) (`:89`), a failed mapping short-circuits (`:90-91`), and the entity is added and saved (`:95-96`) before the mapped [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto) is returned (`:100`).
  - `IsUniqueKeyViolation` (`:108`) walks the whole `InnerException` chain looking for the text `"duplicate key"`, case-insensitively (`:110-113`). The comment above it states the reason: the Application layer cannot reference EF Core or SQL client types, so detection is message-based and covers both SQL Server errors 2601 and 2627.
- **Why it's built this way**: a database identity column is not an option when an external import owns part of the key space, and a pessimistic lock would serialize every question create for a rare conflict. Bounded optimistic retry keeps the common path at one round trip. The message-based exception test is a deliberate trade: it preserves the Clean Architecture dependency rule (`[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]`) at the cost of coupling to a provider's error text.
- **Where it's used**: registered by the Conference application DI scan and invoked by the pipeline for `QuestionsController` posts (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/QuestionsController.cs:33` and `:93`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the `catch` filter deliberately lets the exception escape once attempts are exhausted, so a sustained collision surfaces as an unhandled exception mapped by the shared exception middleware, not as a `Result.Failure`. Also, `IsUniqueKeyViolation` matches on message text, so a non-SQL-Server provider (Cosmos or SQLite under [ADR-018](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/018-polyglot-persistence.html)) would not necessarily be recognized; nothing in this file pins the provider.

### QuestionCreateRequestMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Questions.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/UseCases/Create/QuestionCreateRequestMapper.cs:11` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the adapter that turns a validated [`QuestionCreateRequest`](#questioncreaterequest) into a [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question) domain entity by calling the aggregate's static factory.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype); [`Question`](group-17-conference-domain.md#question); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result).
- **Concept introduced, the request mapper as the only door into a factory.** `[Rubric §4, DDD]` assesses whether entities can be constructed in an invalid state: `Question.Create` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Questions/Question.cs:70-77`) combines the text, entity, type, source, and length invariants and returns a `Result<Question>`, so a bad request becomes an error list rather than a half-built object. Unlike a DTO mapper, which copies fields outward, a request mapper translates inward and is allowed to fail. Keeping it in its own class means the handler never touches the factory signature, which is what lets [`CreateQuestionHandler`](#createquestionhandler) depend on the generic interface instead of the concrete type.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed class` with one method, `CreateEntityAsync` (`QuestionCreateRequestMapper.cs:15`). It null-guards the request (`:17`), then returns `Task.FromResult(Question.Create(...))` (`:19-26`): the work is synchronous and the `Task` exists only to satisfy the async interface. The arguments map one-to-one from the request, with two things worth noticing. `QuestionEntity` and `QuestionType` are passed with the null-forgiving operator (`:22-23`) even though the request declares them nullable, and `questionSource` is the hard-coded literal `"User"` (`:26`), which is what distinguishes an organizer-authored question from an imported one (`QuestionInvariants.cs:34` lists the only valid sources as `Sessionize` and `User`).
- **Why it's built this way**: hard-coding the source at the mapper means the API surface cannot be used to forge a Sessionize-sourced question, and delegating every field check to the factory keeps validation in the domain instead of duplicated in the application layer.
- **Where it's used**: resolved by [`CreateQuestionHandler`](#createquestionhandler) through the `IEntityRequestMapper<Question, QuestionCreateRequest, QuestionIdentifierType>` constructor parameter (`CreateQuestionHandler.cs:22`) and invoked at `:89`; registered by the module's application service scan.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the `!` on the two nullable properties suppresses the compiler warning but does not prevent a null reaching the factory. In that case `QuestionInvariants.EnsureQuestionEntityIsValid` fails the membership test (`QuestionInvariants.cs:68-75`) and the call returns a `Result.Failure`, so the outcome is a validation error rather than a `NullReferenceException`. That behavior comes from the invariant, not from this file.

### QuestionCreateRequestValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Questions.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/UseCases/Create/QuestionCreateRequestValidator.cs:7` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`QuestionCreateRequest`](#questioncreaterequest). It owns no rules of its own; it composes a shared rule set.
- **Depends on**: FluentValidation's `AbstractValidator<T>`; [`QuestionTextRules<T>`](#questiontextrulest).
- **Concept introduced, rule composition with `Include`.** `[Rubric §16, Maintainability]` assesses whether the same constraint is written once: instead of repeating a required-plus-max-length rule in the create validator and again in the update validator, both `Include` the generic [`QuestionTextRules<T>`](#questiontextrulest), which is parameterized by a property selector (`QuestionCreateRequestValidator.cs:10`). `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` applies to the payload the rule set produces: it carries both a human message and a stable machine code (`Question.QuestionText.Required`, `Question.QuestionText.MaxLength`, `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Questions/Validation/QuestionValidationRules.cs:17-18`), and its length ceiling is `QuestionInvariants.QuestionTextMaxLength` (1000, `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Questions/QuestionInvariants.cs:13`), the same constant the EF configuration uses, so the UI limit and the column width cannot drift apart.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed class` whose entire body is one expression-bodied constructor (`:9-10`) calling `Include(new QuestionTextRules<QuestionCreateRequest>(p => p.QuestionText))`.
- **Why it's built this way**: `Include` merges the rule set's rules into this validator as though they were declared inline, so composition costs nothing at validation time while keeping one definition of "what a valid question text is".
- **Where it's used**: discovered by the module's validator scan and run by [`ValidatingCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#validatingcommanddecoratortcommand-tresult) ahead of [`CreateQuestionHandler`](#createquestionhandler).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: only `QuestionText` is validated here. `QuestionEntity` and `QuestionType` are left entirely to the domain factory (see [`QuestionCreateRequestMapper`](#questioncreaterequestmapper)), so their errors arrive as invariant failures rather than as field-level validation errors.

### SpeakerDTOMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.DTOs` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/DTOs/SpeakerDTOMapper.cs:17` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the aggregate-root mapper for [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker). It composes the two child mappers and, crucially, applies a PII redaction rule: the speaker's email is stripped from the response for anyone who is not an Organizer (BR-66).
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityDTOMapper<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientitydtomappertentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) and [`SpeakerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerdto); the child mappers [`SpeakerCategoryItemDTOMapper`](#speakercategoryitemdtomapper) and [`SpeakerQuestionAnswerDTOMapper`](#speakerquestionanswerdtomapper); [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) and [`RoleNames`](group-08-auth.md#rolenames); the [`Email`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#email) value object; Riok.Mapperly's `[Mapper]` and `[UseMapper]`.
- **Concept introduced, mapper composition with `[UseMapper]`, and authorization-aware projection.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether nested contracts map through their own dedicated mappers rather than duplicated inline projections: the two `[UseMapper]` fields (`SpeakerDTOMapper.cs:23-27`) tell the generator to route the nested collections through the same mappers the child endpoints use. `[Rubric §11, Security]` and `[Rubric §30, Compliance, Privacy & Data Governance]` assess whether sensitive fields are withheld at the boundary rather than filtered in the client: this is the one mapper in the family that is not a pure field copy, and it is the last point before serialization where the email can be dropped. The framework's broader PII stance (`PiiAttribute`, erasure vs soft delete) is [ADR-005](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/005-soft-delete-vs-erasure.html).
- **Walkthrough**
  - The class is `[Mapper] sealed partial` with a primary constructor taking the two child mappers and [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) (`:17-21`). Each child mapper is stored in a `[UseMapper]`-annotated private field (`:23-27`), which is how Mapperly discovers them.
  - `MapToDTO` (`:30`) is hand-written, not generated. It null-guards (`:32`), calls the generated projection `MapToDTOGenerated(entity)` (`:33`), and then applies the redaction: `currentUserService.IsInRole(RoleNames.Organizer) ? dto : dto with { Email = null }` (`:36`). Because [`SpeakerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerdto) is a `record class` with an `init`-only nullable `Email` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/Speakers/SpeakerDTO.cs:9` and `:27`), the `with` expression produces a redacted copy without mutating anything.
  - The generated half is the `private partial SpeakerDTO MapToDTOGenerated(Speaker entity)` declaration (`:46`). Renaming the public method and keeping a private generated one is the standard Mapperly technique for wrapping generated code with hand-written logic.
  - `MapToDTOs` (`:40-44`) projects the collection through the public `MapToDTO`, so the redaction applies to every element of a list response, not just single-entity reads.
  - `NullableEmailToString` (`:49`) is a private static converter the generator picks up to unwrap the [`Email`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#email) value object into the DTO's `string?`.
- **Why it's built this way**: enforcing BR-66 in the mapper rather than in each controller means every read path (list, detail, and any nested speaker projection) inherits it, and the `with`-copy keeps the redaction free of side effects on the entity or on a shared DTO instance.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`SpeakerEntityQueryService`](#speakerentityqueryservice) for the speaker read endpoints, into the speaker create and update handlers, and composed via `[UseMapper]` into the session, event, and speaker-join mappers ([`SessionSpeakerDTOMapper`](#sessionspeakerdtomapper), [`EventSpeakerDTOMapper`](#eventspeakerdtomapper), [`EventDTOMapper`](#eventdtomapper)) so nested speaker data is redacted identically.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the redaction depends on [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) reflecting the caller. In a background or gRPC context with no user principal, `IsInRole` returning false yields the redacted (safe) result, but that fallback behavior lives in the auth service, not here.

### UpdateRoomCommandValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.UpdateRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/UpdateRoom/UpdateRoomCommandValidator.cs:7` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`UpdateRoomCommand`](#updateroomcommand). Like [`QuestionCreateRequestValidator`](#questioncreaterequestvalidator) it declares no rules directly, but it composes six rule sets rather than one, which makes it the clearest example of the composition pattern in this group.
- **Depends on**: FluentValidation's `AbstractValidator<T>`; the six generic rule sets [`RoomNameRules<T>`](#roomnamerulest), [`RoomSortRules<T>`](#roomsortrulest), [`RoomCapacityRules<T>`](#roomcapacityrulest), [`RoomFloorRules<T>`](#roomfloorrulest), [`RoomLocationRules<T>`](#roomlocationrulest), and [`RoomAccessibilityInfoRules<T>`](#roomaccessibilityinforulest).
- **Concept reinforced, rule composition with `Include`.** `[Rubric §16, Maintainability]` and `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]`. Each `Include` line (`UpdateRoomCommandValidator.cs:11-16`) passes a property selector into a generic rule set, so the identical rules apply to the add-room command and to this update command without duplication. The rule bodies live in `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/Validation/RoomValidationRules.cs`: name is required and length-capped at `EventInvariants.RoomNameMaxLength` (`:16-18`), sort must be non-negative (`:28-30`), capacity is checked only `When` it is non-null (`:40-43`), and floor, location, and accessibility info are max-length only (`:54-56`, `:67-69`, `:80-82`). Every rule carries a stable error code such as `Room.Name.MaxLength`, so a client can branch on the code instead of parsing the message.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed class` with a block-bodied constructor (`:9-17`) containing six `Include(...)` calls in field order.
- **Why it's built this way**: the length ceilings all come from `EventInvariants`, the same constants the EF entity configuration applies to the columns, so validation and schema cannot drift.
- **Where it's used**: run by [`ValidatingCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#validatingcommanddecoratortcommand-tresult) ahead of [`UpdateRoomHandler`](#updateroomhandler).

### UpdateRoomHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.UpdateRoom` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/UpdateRoom/UpdateRoomHandler.cs:13` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the handler that applies a room edit through the owning event aggregate. Same template as [`AddSpeakerCategoryItemHandler`](#addspeakercategoryitemhandler), with two instructive differences: it loads the child navigation explicitly, and it returns a non-generic `Result` because a room update yields no DTO.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error); [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) and [`Room`](group-17-conference-domain.md#room); `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging`.
- **Concept reinforced, load-then-delegate, with an explicit include.** `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]`, `[Rubric §4, DDD]`, `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`. The `[Rubric §4, DDD]` point is the sharp one here: the handler never touches a `Room`. It hands the whole parameter set to `Event.UpdateRoom` (`:31-38`), which locates the child through `GetRoomOrNotFound`, delegates the field updates to `room.Update(...)`, and raises a `RoomChanged` domain event carrying the event id, room id, and name (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:377-388`). Reaching into the child collection from the application layer would bypass that event.
- **Walkthrough**
  - Primary constructor (`:13-15`): just [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) and `ILogger<UpdateRoomHandler>`. No DTO mapper, because the declared result is `Result`, not `Result<T>`.
  - `HandleAsync` (`:18`) resolves the `Event` repository (`:22`) and loads the aggregate with `includes: [nameof(Event.Rooms)]` and `asTracking: true` (`:23-27`). Both arguments matter: without the include the room would not be in memory for `GetRoomOrNotFound` to find, and without tracking the mutation would not be picked up by `SaveChangesAsync`.
  - A missing event returns `Error.NotFound` with source and target set (`:28-29`).
  - The domain call is forwarded verbatim (`:31-38`), then the handler branches on `result.IsSuccess` (`:39`): it saves with `ConfigureAwait(false)` and logs (`:41-42`) only on success, and returns the domain's `Result` either way (`:45`), so the failure's error codes reach the API unaltered.
  - `[LoggerMessage]` generates `LogRoomUpdated` with named `RoomId` and `EventId` properties (`:48-49`).
- **Why it's built this way**: `SaveChangesAsync` is what flushes the outbox and dispatches the domain events captured during the aggregate call (see [Group 4](group-04-events-outbox.md) and [ADR-003](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/003-outbox-dual-dispatch.html)), so guarding it behind `IsSuccess` guarantees a rejected edit publishes nothing.
- **Where it's used**: registered by the Conference application DI scan; invoked by the pipeline for `RoomsController` room updates (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/RoomsController.cs:88` and `:179`).

### LinkUserToSpeakerCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.LinkUser` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/LinkUser/LinkUserToSpeakerCommand.cs:13` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the write message an organizer sends to attach an application account to a speaker profile (BR-209). Two ids and nothing else: `SpeakerId` and `UserId` (`LinkUserToSpeakerCommand.cs:13`).
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) and [`ITransactional`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#itransactional) (both markers); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) (referenced only to build the cache prefix); the module identifier aliases `SpeakerIdentifierType` (a GUID here) and `UserIdentifierType` (an int owned by Identity).
- **Concept introduced, the command that declares its own transaction.** `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` assesses whether a write is modeled as an explicit single-purpose message: this record carries intent only, and the two marker interfaces tell the pipeline how to run it. `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting]` assesses whether such concerns are declared rather than hand-coded: implementing [`ITransactional`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#itransactional) (`:13`) opts the message into [`TransactionalCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#transactionalcommanddecoratortcommand-tresult), and the XML comment states the reason plainly (`:7-8`): the Speaker link and the outbox row that carries the cross-context User update must commit together or not at all. Implementing [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) with `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Speaker).FullName}:"` (`:16`) is what makes [`CachingCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#cachingcommanddecoratortcommand-tresult) drop every cached read keyed under the `Speaker` type after a successful link.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed record` with a two-parameter positional constructor (`:13`) and one member, the expression-bodied `CachePrefix` (`:15-16`). The cross-module identifier pairing is the notable part: `UserIdentifierType` is Identity's alias, carried here as a plain scalar because the two modules own separate databases and there is no foreign key to point at ([ADR-006](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/006-database-per-service.html)).
- **Why it's built this way**: the two markers move durability and cache eviction out of the handler and into the pipeline, so [`LinkUserToSpeakerHandler`](#linkusertospeakerhandler) reads as pure domain orchestration. Records give value equality and immutability for free.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by `SpeakersController`'s `PUT /Speakers/{id}/link` action from a `LinkUserRequest` body ([Group 20](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakerscontroller), `MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/SpeakersController.cs:346`, handler injected at `:49`); handled by [`LinkUserToSpeakerHandler`](#linkusertospeakerhandler).

### RemoveSpeakerCategoryItemCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.RemoveSpeakerCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/RemoveSpeakerCategoryItem/RemoveSpeakerCategoryItemCommand.cs:12` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the command that detaches a category-item association from a speaker. Category items are how a speaker's topics and locality are modeled, so this is the "untag this speaker" message.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) (cache prefix only); the `SpeakerIdentifierType` and `SpeakerCategoryItemIdentifierType` aliases.
- **Concept reinforced, the cache-invalidating child-mutation command.** Same shape as [`LinkUserToSpeakerCommand`](#linkusertospeakercommand) minus the transaction marker, and the prefix is again the parent aggregate's: `CachePrefix => $"{typeof(Speaker).FullName}:"` (`:17`). That is the point worth noticing. The join row has no cache namespace of its own, so removing one evicts the whole `Speaker` cache surface, which is what keeps a stale nested category item out of an already-cached speaker read. `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]`, `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`.
- **Walkthrough**: a `sealed record` with two positional parameters spread over three lines (`:12-14`): `SpeakerId` names the aggregate to load and `SpeakerCategoryItemId` names the join entity to remove. The only member is `CachePrefix` (`:16-17`). There is no [`ITransactional`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#itransactional) here because the whole write lands inside one aggregate and one `SaveChangesAsync`, with no cross-context event to keep atomic.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by `SpeakerCategoryItemsController`'s delete action ([Group 20](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakercategoryitemscontroller), `MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/SpeakerCategoryItemsController.cs:216`, handler injected at `:50`); handled by [`RemoveSpeakerCategoryItemHandler`](#removespeakercategoryitemhandler).

### SpeakerCreateRequest

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/Create/SpeakerCreateRequest.cs:10` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the create-request DTO for a conference speaker. It doubles as the command: [`CreateSpeakerHandler`](#createspeakerhandler) implements `ICommandHandler<SpeakerCreateRequest, Result<SpeakerDTO>>` directly against this type, so there is no separate `CreateSpeakerCommand`.
- **Depends on**: [`ICreateRequest`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icreaterequest) (an empty marker used as a generic constraint by [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype)); [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) (cache prefix); the `SpeakerIdentifierType` alias.
- **Concept reinforced, the request-as-command shape.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether the wire contract is an explicit, versionable type rather than the domain entity leaking outward: the controller binds this record straight from the request body (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/SpeakersController.cs:285`) and it is also the fourth generic argument of the base controller (`:59`). `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` applies to the folder: request, mapper, validator, and handler for Create all sit in `Speakers/UseCases/Create`. Marking it [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating) (`:10`, `:13`) means a successful create evicts the cached speaker reads exactly like the command records above.
- **Walkthrough**: a `record class` (not `sealed`) with `init`-only members. `CachePrefix` (`:13`) is the invalidation tag. `Id` (`:16`) is a `SpeakerIdentifierType`, and its comment records that it is Sessionize-assigned: the caller supplies the key rather than the database generating it, which is what lets an import be idempotent. Three members are `required`, so the record cannot be constructed without them: `FirstName` (`:19`), `LastName` (`:22`), and `FullName` (`:25`). The rest are optional `init` members: `Email` (`:28`), `Bio` (`:31`), `TagLine` (`:34`), `ProfilePicture` (`:37`), the `IsTopSpeaker` flag (`:40`), and the four profile links `TwitterHandle` (`:43`), `LinkedInUrl` (`:46`), `GitHubUrl` (`:49`), and `WebsiteUrl` (`:52`).
- **Why it's built this way**: `required` plus `init` gives compile-time enforcement of the minimum payload while leaving the rest optional, and collapsing request and command into one type keeps a simple create slice to a single message (contrast the child-mutation flows, where the controller builds a distinct command record).
- **Where it's used**: bound by `SpeakersController` ([Group 20](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakerscontroller)); validated by [`SpeakerCreateRequestValidator`](#speakercreaterequestvalidator); translated to a domain entity by [`SpeakerCreateRequestMapper`](#speakercreaterequestmapper); handled by [`CreateSpeakerHandler`](#createspeakerhandler).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `FullName` is `required` on the contract but never reaches the domain. [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) computes `FullName => $"{FirstName} {LastName}"` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Speakers/Speaker.cs:54`) and [`SpeakerCreateRequestMapper`](#speakercreaterequestmapper) does not pass it to the factory, so a caller-supplied value is accepted and discarded. The same is true of the four profile-link members, which the mapper also drops (`Email`, by contrast, IS forwarded). Nothing on this type says so.

### UnlinkUserFromSpeakerCommand

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.UnlinkUser` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/UnlinkUser/UnlinkUserFromSpeakerCommand.cs:12` · Level 8 · record

- **What it is**: the inverse of [`LinkUserToSpeakerCommand`](#linkusertospeakercommand): it severs the account-to-speaker link (BR-209). It needs only `SpeakerId` (`:12`), because the speaker row is where the link is stored.
- **Depends on**: [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating), [`ITransactional`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#itransactional), [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) (cache prefix), and the `SpeakerIdentifierType` alias.
- **Concept reinforced**: none new; see [`LinkUserToSpeakerCommand`](#linkusertospeakercommand) for the transaction and cache markers. The comment repeats the same rationale (`:7-8`): the Speaker unlink and the cross-context User update must be atomic. `CachePrefix` is identical (`:15`).
- **Walkthrough**: a one-parameter `sealed record` (`:12`) with the single `CachePrefix` member (`:14-15`). The user id is deliberately absent from the message: the handler reads the currently linked id off the aggregate before clearing it, so the emitted event can still name who was unlinked (see [`UnlinkUserFromSpeakerHandler`](#unlinkuserfromspeakerhandler)).
- **Where it's used**: constructed by `SpeakersController`'s `DELETE /Speakers/{id}/link` action (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/SpeakersController.cs:364`, handler injected at `:50`); handled by [`UnlinkUserFromSpeakerHandler`](#unlinkuserfromspeakerhandler).

### UserRegisteredHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Users.IntegrationEventHandlers` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Users/IntegrationEventHandlers/UserRegisteredHandler.cs:40` · Level 8 · class

- **What it is**: the Conference-side subscriber to Identity's [`UserRegistered`](group-24-identity-module.md#userregistered) integration event. When someone registers an account, this handler tries to find the speaker profile that belongs to them and links the two automatically (BR-207). It is the most security-sensitive type in this unit, and the most instructive: nearly every design decision in it was made in response to a specific failure.
- **Depends on**: [`IIntegrationEventHandler<in TIntegrationEvent>`](group-04-events-outbox.md#iintegrationeventhandlerin-tintegrationevent) closed over [`UserRegistered`](group-24-identity-module.md#userregistered); `IServiceScopeFactory` (Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) and [`IRepository<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#irepositorytentity-tidentifiertype); [`IEventBus`](group-04-events-outbox.md#ieventbus); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) and [`SpeakerLinkedToUser`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerlinkedtouser); the [`Email`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#email) value object; `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging`.
- **Concept introduced, the singleton event handler that opens its own scope.** `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` assesses whether cross-module reactions are event-driven rather than direct calls: Identity never references Conference, it publishes, and this class reacts. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` assesses whether that reaction survives a process split: it does, because delivery is the broker's job (locally RabbitMQ, Azure Service Bus in production) and this handler only sees the event object. The lifetime detail is the teachable one. Integration event handlers are registered as **singletons** by the framework scan (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:125-129`), but [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) is scoped, so a singleton cannot take it in its constructor. The handler therefore injects `IServiceScopeFactory` (`:41`) and creates one `await using` scope per event (`:51`) from which it resolves the unit of work and the event bus (`:52-53`). The class comment states this convention explicitly (`:36-38`).
- **Concept introduced, refusing to trust attacker-controlled input as an identity proof.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether an automated privilege grant is based on something the requester cannot forge. There is now exactly ONE match strategy, email equality (`:14-15`). A name-based fallback used to run whenever the email match missed, which covered Sessionize-imported speakers (the public `view/All` endpoint omits PII, so their `Email` is always null). It was removed as a security fix, and the comment spells out why (`:18-24`): first and last name arrive straight from the registration form and prove nothing, so anyone who knew a speaker's name could register under it and take over that speaker profile. Email-less speakers are now linked deliberately by an organizer through `PUT /Speakers/{id}/link` (see [`LinkUserToSpeakerCommand`](#linkusertospeakercommand)).
- **Walkthrough**
  - `HandleAsync` (`:45`) null-guards the event (`:47`), then does all of its work inside the per-event scope. It resolves `IRepository<Speaker, SpeakerIdentifierType>` from the unit of work (`:55`) rather than injecting it, which is the framework rule for keeping one tracked context per scope.
  - `TryMatchByEmailAsync` (`:130`) builds an [`Email`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#email) value object from the event payload and logs-and-bails when the address is malformed (`:137-142`). It then queries speakers with `where: s => s.Email != null && s.Email == emailVO` and `asTracking: true` (`:145-149`); tracking is required because the match is about to be mutated. The comment at `:135-136` records why the comparison is safe: [`Email`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#email) normalizes to lowercase on creation and EF stores the normalized value, so direct equality is effectively case-insensitive.
  - The result ordering is deliberate and worth reading twice: `.OrderBy(s => s.LinkedUserId.HasValue).ThenBy(s => s.Id).FirstOrDefault()` (`:154-157`). When an email is shared by two speaker rows, an arbitrary `FirstOrDefault` could pick an already-linked record, abandon the link, and then pick a different one on a retry. Preferring unlinked and then ordering by id makes the choice deterministic across attempts.
  - On a miss, `LogNameMatchCandidatesAsync` (`:167`) runs. This is a read-only organizer cue, not a fallback: it counts unlinked speakers whose first and last name equal the registration's (`:183-187`) and logs the count (`:191`). The matched rows are never returned, so no code path can link on a name. The comment (`:180-182`) notes that SQL Server's default `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS` collation makes the equality case-insensitive, and that the global soft-delete query filter excludes deleted rows automatically.
  - Three guards precede the link. A speaker already linked to a **different** user is left alone (`:66-70`). A speaker already linked to **this** user re-publishes [`SpeakerLinkedToUser`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerlinkedtouser) so Identity can re-sync, then returns without re-linking (`:74-80`), which makes redelivery idempotent. A failing `speaker.LinkUser(...)` is logged and abandoned (`:82-87`).
  - The happy path saves through the unit of work (`:92`) and then publishes [`SpeakerLinkedToUser`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerlinkedtouser) on [`IEventBus`](group-04-events-outbox.md#ieventbus) (`:95-97`) so Identity populates `User.LinkedSpeakerId`.
  - `LogAndRethrow` (`:124`) is an exception **filter**, not a catch body: it logs and returns `false`, so the exception keeps propagating and the `catch` block at `:101-105` is unreachable (the code says so at `:103`). The remarks explain the fix (`:111-122`): the handler used to swallow everything, which meant one transient database fault lost the auto-link permanently, because the delivery was already acked and nothing retried. Letting the exception through hands the decision to the delivery mechanism, which is built for it: the outbox retries to its limit and then dead-letters, and MassTransit redelivers and then moves the message to the error queue. Retrying is safe because the unique-index race it can lose resolves on the next attempt through the "already linked to a different user" guard.
  - Seven `[LoggerMessage]` partial methods (`:195-214`) cover every branch, which is what makes the auto-link auditable after the fact.
- **Why it's built this way**: ADC deliberately uses an integration event here where Store uses an in-process domain event for the same concept, because Identity and Conference are separate services with separate databases ([ADR-006](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/006-database-per-service.html), [ADR-003](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/003-outbox-dual-dispatch.html)). The auto-link is therefore eventually consistent, and the class comment is explicit about the user-visible consequence (`:29-34`): a brand-new user's first token does not yet carry the `speaker_id` claim, it appears on the next refresh, and the 5-minute output cache refreshes on expiry rather than being invalidated from here, because invalidating would require an AspNetCore dependency the Application layer must not take.
- **Where it's used**: registered as a singleton by the framework's integration-handler scan reached through `ScanModuleApplicationServices<ClassReference>()` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:106`), and wired to the broker in the Conference service host by `RegisterIntegrationEventConsumer<UserRegistered>()` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:320-321`), which attaches the generic [`IntegrationEventConsumer<TEvent>`](group-04-events-outbox.md#integrationeventconsumertevent) adapter. When `MessageBus:Provider` is unset the transport falls back to in-process, which is how integration tests boot the host without a broker (`Program.cs:318-319`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the unique-index race on `Speaker.LinkedUserId` is described in a comment (`:89-91`) but the index itself is defined in the Infrastructure EF configuration, not here.

### EventLiveValidationService

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/EventLiveValidationService.cs:18` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the Conference-side implementation of [`IEventLiveValidationService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#ieventlivevalidationservice), the narrow contract Engagement's conference-day live layer calls to learn whether an event or session is live and who is allowed to act on it.
- **Depends on**: [`IEventLiveValidationService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#ieventlivevalidationservice) (the interface, which lives in `Conference.Shared` so consumers need no reference to this assembly); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork); the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) and [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) aggregates; [`SessionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessioninvariants); [`CurrentEventSelector`](group-17-conference-domain.md#currenteventselector); the [`EventLiveInfo`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventliveinfo) and [`SessionLiveInfo`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionliveinfo) payloads; [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept introduced, a cross-module read contract implemented in the owning module.** `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` assesses whether cross-module dependencies flow through interfaces a process boundary can later intercept. Engagement owns the live experience but not the source of truth for events and sessions, so it asks Conference through this interface. In-process (tests, single-host runs) the DI container binds it to this class (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:102`); across processes the same interface is satisfied by [`EventLiveValidationServiceGrpcAdapter`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#eventlivevalidationservicegrpcadapter) and served by a gRPC endpoint, and Engagement's code does not change ([ADR-007](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/007-grpc-extraction.html)). When Conference is disabled entirely, the module registers [`DisabledEventLiveValidationService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#disabledeventlivevalidationservice) instead (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/ConferenceModule.cs:24`). `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]`: everything here runs over repository abstractions, with no EF or transport type in sight.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetEventLiveInfoAsync` (`:21`) resolves the `Event` repository from the unit of work (`:25`) and loads untracked with an empty include list (`:26-30`), returns `Error.NotFound` decorated with source and target when the event is missing (`:32-36`), and otherwise returns an [`EventLiveInfo`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventliveinfo) carrying the published flag and the computed UTC window (`:38-40`).
  - `GetSessionLiveInfoAsync` (`:44`) loads the session with `includes: [nameof(Session.SessionSpeakers)]` (`:49-53`), because the caller needs the speaker list to decide presenter rights (BR-236).
  - It then enforces the same eligibility rules the bookmark path uses, by calling the domain's own invariants rather than restating them: `SessionInvariants.EnsureNotServiceSession` (BR-91, `:63`) and `SessionInvariants.EnsureStatusIsEligible` (BR-49, `:67`). A failure is converted into a typed failure with the domain's error list intact (`:64-69`).
  - The parent event is loaded next (`:71-76`) with the same not-found treatment (`:78-82`), the window is computed (`:84`), and the active speaker ids are projected with the soft-deleted joins filtered out: `[.. session.SessionSpeakers.Where(ss => !ss.IsDeleted).Select(ss => ss.SpeakerId)]` (`:86-87`).
  - The returned [`SessionLiveInfo`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionliveinfo) (`:89-96`) carries the event id, the published flag, the window, the speaker ids, `IsPlenumSession`, and the event's `QuestionModerationDefault` (BR-233), so one call answers every question the live layer has.
  - `ComputeLiveWindowUtc` (`:102-106`) is a three-line delegation to [`CurrentEventSelector`](group-17-conference-domain.md#currenteventselector)`.GetLiveWindowUtc`, and the comment above it says why (`:99-101`): the window rule, the unknown-zone degradation, and the spring-forward-gap guard must stay identical to the ones the home surfaces and the now-next snapshot use. The rule itself lives in `Conference.Shared` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/Events/CurrentEventSelector.cs:64-83`): the window opens at `StartDate` 00:00 local and closes, exclusive, at `EndDate + 1 day` 00:00 local, converted from the event's IANA zone, degrading to treating the local dates as UTC when the zone id is unknown.
- **Why it's built this way**: centralizing "is this live and eligible" behind one interface, and reusing the domain invariants and the shared window math instead of copying them, gives both modules one authoritative answer. See [ADR-007](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/007-grpc-extraction.html) for the extraction rationale.
- **Where it's used**: registered scoped at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:102`; consumed by Engagement's live layer (LivePolls and SessionQuestions), reaching this class over gRPC in production.

### LinkUserToSpeakerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.LinkUser` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/LinkUser/LinkUserToSpeakerHandler.cs:20` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the handler for [`LinkUserToSpeakerCommand`](#linkusertospeakercommand). It updates the Conference side of a bidirectional link that spans two databases, and raises the event that updates the other side.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker); [`SpeakerLinkedToUser`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerlinkedtouser); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error); `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging`. Note what is **not** injected: there is no publisher service, because the event is raised on the aggregate.
- **Concept introduced, cross-context coordination captured in the same transaction.** `[Rubric §6, CQRS & Event-Driven]` and `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]`. Conference and Identity own separate databases ([ADR-006](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/006-database-per-service.html)), so there is no foreign key between `Speaker` and `User` and consistency has to flow through events. The load-bearing detail is the ordering: `speaker.AddDomainEvent(new SpeakerLinkedToUser(...))` runs BEFORE the single `SaveChangesAsync` (`:54`, then `:56`), so the outbox row is written inside the same transaction as the link ([ADR-003](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/003-outbox-dual-dispatch.html)). The class comment states the failure this removed (`:14-18`): with a post-save publish, a crash could commit the Conference-side link and lose the event that sets `User.LinkedSpeakerId`. The [`OutboxProcessor`](group-04-events-outbox.md#outboxprocessor) later routes the row to the registered [`IMessageBus`](group-04-events-outbox.md#imessagebus) transport. This is also why the command carries [`ITransactional`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#itransactional).
- **Walkthrough**
  - Primary constructor (`:20-22`): [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) and `ILogger<LinkUserToSpeakerHandler>`; the declared result is the non-generic [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), so no DTO mapper is needed.
  - `HandleAsync` (`:25`) resolves the repository (`:29`) and loads the speaker with the include-free, tracked overload (`:30`), returning `Error.NotFound` with source and target set when it is missing (`:31-32`).
  - The BR-208 uniqueness guard (`:34-46`) is the interesting part: it queries every speaker whose `LinkedUserId` equals the target user (`:35-38`) and fails with `Error.Invariant(code: "Speaker.UserAlreadyLinked", ...)` if **any other** speaker already holds that link (`:39-46`). The `s.Id != command.SpeakerId` test is what makes re-linking the same pair a no-op rather than an error.
  - `speaker.LinkUser(command.UserId)` (`:48`) is the domain decision. The aggregate refuses a speaker that is already linked, returning `Speaker.AlreadyLinked` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Speakers/Speaker.cs:253-262`), so the handler owns only the cross-row rule and the entity owns its own.
  - On success it raises the integration event on the aggregate (`:54`), saves (`:56`), and logs through the generated `LogUserLinkedToSpeaker` (`:58`, declared `:64-65`). The domain `Result` is returned either way (`:61`), so a rejection reaches the API with its error codes intact.
- **Why it's built this way**: splitting the rules (uniqueness across speakers in the handler, "already linked?" inside the aggregate) keeps each check where the data for it lives, and the pre-save event raise is a deliberate durability fix rather than a style choice.
- **Where it's used**: registered by the Conference application scan; invoked through the decorator pipeline by `SpeakersController`'s link action ([Group 20](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakerscontroller), `SpeakersController.cs:345-347`). The emitted event is consumed on the Identity side to set `User.LinkedSpeakerId`.

### RemoveSpeakerCategoryItemHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.RemoveSpeakerCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/RemoveSpeakerCategoryItem/RemoveSpeakerCategoryItemHandler.cs:13` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveSpeakerCategoryItemCommand`](#removespeakercategoryitemcommand). It is the canonical "load the aggregate with its children, delegate, save" template for a child removal.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) and its [`SpeakerCategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakercategoryitem) child; [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error); `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging`.
- **Concept introduced, the include-plus-tracking pair that a removal requires.** `[Rubric §4, DDD]` assesses whether children are mutated through the root: the handler never touches a [`SpeakerCategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakercategoryitem), it calls `entity.RemoveSpeakerCategoryItem(...)` (`:31`), which locates the child, calls its `Delete()`, and raises `SpeakerCategoryItemChanged` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Speakers/Speaker.cs:363-377`). Reaching into the collection from here would skip that event. `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` covers the load arguments, and both of them are load-bearing: `includes: [nameof(Speaker.SpeakerCategoryItems)]` puts the child in memory for the domain method to find, and `asTracking: true` is what makes the soft-delete flag survive `SaveChangesAsync` (`:23-27`). This is the distinction between the add and remove handlers across the module: an add appends and needs neither.
- **Walkthrough**: the `sealed partial class` primary constructor takes the unit of work and a typed logger (`:13-15`). `HandleAsync` (`:18`) resolves the repository (`:22`), loads (`:23-27`), returns `Error.NotFound` when the speaker is missing (`:28-29`), delegates (`:31`), and on success only saves with `ConfigureAwait(false)` and logs through the generated `LogCategoryItemRemoved` (`:32-37`, declared `:42-43`). The domain `Result` is returned unchanged (`:39`).
- **Why it's built this way**: guarding `SaveChangesAsync` behind `IsSuccess` matters more than it looks. That one call is the boundary that stamps audit fields, dispatches domain events, and writes outbox rows, so a rejected removal publishes nothing.
- **Where it's used**: registered by the Conference application scan; invoked by the pipeline for `SpeakerCategoryItemsController` deletes ([Group 20](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakercategoryitemscontroller)).

### SessionBookmarkValidationService

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/SessionBookmarkValidationService.cs:12` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the Conference-side implementation of [`ISessionBookmarkValidationService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#isessionbookmarkvalidationservice). Engagement stores bookmarks but does not own sessions, so before it accepts one it asks this service whether the session may be bookmarked at all.
- **Depends on**: [`ISessionBookmarkValidationService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#isessionbookmarkvalidationservice); [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork); the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) aggregate; [`SessionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessioninvariants); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept reinforced, the cross-module read contract.** The same shape as [`EventLiveValidationService`](#eventlivevalidationservice): a scoped implementation registered at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:99`, replaced by [`SessionBookmarkValidationServiceGrpcAdapter`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#sessionbookmarkvalidationservicegrpcadapter) when the caller is out of process ([ADR-007](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/007-grpc-extraction.html)), and by [`DisabledSessionBookmarkValidationService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#disabledsessionbookmarkvalidationservice) when the Conference module is switched off (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/ConferenceModule.cs:23`). `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]`, `[Rubric §4, DDD]`: the eligibility rules are not restated here, they are the domain's own invariants called by name.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `ValidateSessionForBookmarkAsync` (`:15`) loads the session untracked with no includes (`:19-24`), because it only reads two scalars, and returns `Error.NotFound` when it is missing (`:26-30`).
  - It then applies exactly two rules: `SessionInvariants.EnsureNotServiceSession` for BR-91, no bookmarking a break or a lunch slot (`:33`), and `SessionInvariants.EnsureStatusIsEligible` for BR-49, only `Accepted` or unset statuses (`:38`). The second is returned directly as the method's result. Each invariant carries its own stable code, `Session.IsServiceSession` and `Session.StatusIneligible` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Sessions/SessionInvariants.cs:91-114`), so Engagement can branch on the code rather than the message.
  - `GetSessionIdsByEventAsync` (`:42`) is the bulk half of the contract: it loads every session for an event untracked (`:47-51`) and returns just the ids (`:53`). Engagement uses it to scope a user's bookmarks to one event without ever holding a session entity.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the rule bodies in [`SessionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessioninvariants) means the bookmark path, the live layer ([`EventLiveValidationService`](#eventlivevalidationservice) calls the same two methods), and the domain itself cannot drift on what "eligible" means. Returning ids rather than DTOs from the bulk method keeps the cross-service payload minimal.
- **Where it's used**: registered scoped at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:99`; called by Engagement's bookmark handlers, over gRPC in the deployed topology.

### SpeakerCreateRequestMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/UseCases/Create/SpeakerCreateRequestMapper.cs:11` · Level 9 · class

- **What it is**: the adapter that turns a validated [`SpeakerCreateRequest`](#speakercreaterequest) into a [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) entity by calling the aggregate's static factory.
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype); [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result).
- **Concept reinforced, the request mapper as the only door into a factory.** `[Rubric §4, DDD]` assesses whether an entity can be constructed in an invalid state: `Speaker.Create` (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Speakers/Speaker.cs:105-152`) parses the email into an [`Email`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#email) value object, combines the first- and last-name invariants, assigns the client-supplied id or generates one, and raises `SpeakerChanged`, returning a `Result<Speaker>` throughout.
