### ConferenceCategoryNavigationPopulator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Categories` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Categories/ConferenceCategoryNavigationPopulator.cs:11` · Level 10 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the navigation populator for the [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category) aggregate. It loads the one child collection ([`CategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitem)) that EF Core cannot materialize through `.Include()` on this model (`ConferenceCategoryNavigationPopulator.cs:7-9`). It is the simplest populator in the Conference module: a single descriptor and an empty class body.
- **Depends on**: [`DeclarativeNavigationPopulator<TEntity>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#declarativenavigationpopulatortentity) (the base it extends, closed over `Category`), [`ChildNavigationDescriptor<TEntity, TParentId, TChild, TChildId>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#childnavigationdescriptortentity-tparentid-tchild-tchildid) (the descriptor shape), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (passed straight through to the base), and the [`Category`](group-17-conference-domain.md#category)/[`CategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#categoryitem) entities.
- **Concept reinforced, declarative child loading over hand-written joins**: the mechanism is taught in [Group 11](group-11-navigation-populators.md#declarativenavigationpopulatortentity) ([ADR-002](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/002-navigation-populators.html)); the job here is pure binding. The subclass supplies data, not an override: the base constructor takes the `IUnitOfWork` plus a collection-expression array of descriptors (`ConferenceCategoryNavigationPopulator.cs:11-22`) and owns the bulk fetch-and-assign algorithm. `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]`: Template Method configured by data rather than by virtual methods. `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]`: the Application layer describes hydration with repository abstractions and property selectors, with no EF Core namespace anywhere in the file.
- **Walkthrough**: one `ChildNavigationDescriptor<Category, ConferenceCategoryIdentifierType, CategoryItem, CategoryItemIdentifierType>` (`ConferenceCategoryNavigationPopulator.cs:15`) with four settings: `PropertyName = nameof(Category.CategoryItems)` (`:17`), `ParentKeySelector = e => e.Id` (`:18`), `ChildForeignKeySelector = child => child.CategoryId` (`:19`), and `AssignAction = (e, categoryItems) => e.SetCategoryItems(categoryItems)` (`:20`). The assign action goes through the aggregate's own `SetCategoryItems` mutator rather than a back-door property setter, so hydration respects the same guard rails a business operation would. Note the parent-key type: the Conference module's category alias is `ConferenceCategoryIdentifierType`, not a bare `CategoryIdentifierType`, because the module also has category *items* and shares the workspace-wide alias convention (see [primer](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)).
- **Why it's built this way**: one descriptor per collection means adding a child navigation is a data edit, not a new query method, and every aggregate in the module hydrates through the same code path ([ADR-002](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/002-navigation-populators.html)).
- **Where it's used**: registered as the `INavigationPopulator<Category>` implementation by the Conference Application module registration, `services.TryAddScoped<INavigationPopulator<Category>, ConferenceCategoryNavigationPopulator>()` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:64`), and resolved by the navigation-population pipeline whenever a `Category` is read with `CategoryItems` requested.

### GetNowNextHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.NowNext` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/NowNext/GetNowNextHandler.cs:20` · Level 10 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the handler that builds the now-next snapshot: the sessions running at the query instant plus the next starting batch, for one published event or the auto-selected current-or-next event (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:12-19`).
- **Depends on**: [`IQueryHandler<in TQuery, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#iqueryhandlerin-tquery-tresult), [`GetNowNextQuery`](#getnownextquery), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) and [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) aggregates, [`CurrentEventSelector`](group-17-conference-domain.md#currenteventselector) (event auto-selection and live-window math), [`CalendarExportMapper`](#calendarexportmapper) (eligibility plus wall-clock-to-UTC conversion), the [`NowNextDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#nownextdto) and [`NowNextSessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#nownextsessiondto) shapes, `Result`, and the BCL `TimeProvider`.
- **Concept introduced, a `TimeProvider`-injected clock for a time-bucketed read**: the handler takes `TimeProvider timeProvider` in its primary constructor (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:20-22`) and reads `GetUtcNow()` once (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:29`), so "now" is a single injected, testable instant rather than an ambient `DateTime.UtcNow` sampled repeatedly through the method. `[Rubric §14, Testability]`: a fake `TimeProvider` lets a test place the wall clock precisely inside or across a session boundary. `[Rubric §12, Performance and Scalability]`: pairing this handler with the 30-second cache on its query keeps a per-load widget cheap.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - Selects the event via `SelectEventAsync` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:90-113`): an explicit id loads that event with its `Rooms` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:96-101`); a null id loads all published events and defers to [`CurrentEventSelector`](group-17-conference-domain.md#currenteventselector)`.SelectCurrentOrNext` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:107-112`). A missing or unpublished event returns `Error.NotFound` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:32-36`).
  - Loads the event's sessions, builds a room-id to name map, and resolves the IANA time zone, falling back to `TimeZoneInfo.Utc` on `TimeZoneNotFoundException` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:38-51`).
  - Filters sessions through `CalendarExportMapper.IsExportable` and projects each to a row carrying both the local and the UTC start/end via `CalendarExportMapper.ToUtc` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:53-56`, `GetNowNextHandler.cs:80-88`).
  - "Now" is the rows whose `[StartsAtUtc, EndsAtUtc)` interval contains the instant, ordered by start then room name with `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:58-62`). "Next" is the batch sharing the earliest future start, so parallel tracks surface together rather than one arbitrary winner (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:64-72`).
  - Computes the event's live flag from `CurrentEventSelector.GetLiveWindowUtc` and returns a `NowNextDTO` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:74-77`).
- **Why it's built this way**: reusing the calendar-export eligibility and DST conversion keeps the now-next view consistent with the exported schedule (one rule, two readers), and the earliest-future-start batching matches how attendees think about "up next" across concurrent tracks ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 8).
- **Where it's used**: dispatched behind the Conference now-next endpoint and the home-screen widget.

### PublicConferenceVisibility

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Common` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Common/PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:28` · Level 10 · class (static)

- **What it is**: the one definition of "what may an anonymous or non-privileged caller read from the conference catalog", expressed as three id-list resolvers. Callers turn the returned lists into `Id IN (...)` specifications, so closing a visibility leak in this file closes it on every public read at once (`PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:10-15`).
- **Depends on**: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) (read repositories only), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event), [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session), and [`SessionSpeaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionspeaker) entities, [`PublicSessionStatusSpecification`](#publicsessionstatusspecification) (the BR-49 status allow-list), [`CrossSourceSpecification`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#crosssourcespecification), [`AndSpecification<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#andspecificationtentity-tidentifiertype), and [`InlineSpecification<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#inlinespecificationtentity-tidentifiertype).
- **Concept introduced, authorization-by-id-list instead of authorization-by-join**: the three rules are stated in the remarks (`PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:17-21`): an event is visible when published (BR-108); a session is visible when its event is visible AND its status is on the BR-49 allow-list; a speaker is visible when they have at least one eligible session inside the scoped published-event set (BR-239). Every rule is resolved as a **scalar id projection**, never as a navigation join such as `s.Event.IsPublished` (`PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:22-26`). Three things follow: the criteria stay translatable on any engine (the polyglot safeguard of [ADR-018](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/018-polyglot-persistence.html)), each aggregate keeps a by-id boundary to the others so a future extraction can answer the same question over a service call, and the resulting specifications pass the architecture fitness test that bans navigation-property filters. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether authorization is centralized and fail-closed: this class is a single choke point, and every "nothing visible" path returns an empty list, which the callers render as an `IN ()` that matches no rows rather than as an unfiltered read. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` and `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]`: id lists cross an aggregate boundary; joins do not.
- **Walkthrough**: three public resolvers plus one private helper, and the ordering between them is the rule hierarchy.
  - `GetPublishedEventIdsAsync` (`PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:36-47`) projects `e.Id` where `e.IsPublished`, untracked (`:42`), then materializes once so callers embed a stable collection EF can translate into an `IN` (`:45-46`). That materialize-once comment is load-bearing: re-enumerating a lazy sequence inside an expression tree is what makes such a filter fail to translate.
  - `GetVisibleSessionIdsAsync` (`PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:56-77`) builds the event scoping through [`CrossSourceSpecification`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#crosssourcespecification)`.BuildAsync`, passing `principalPredicate: e => e.IsPublished`, `dependentForeignKey: s => s.EventId`, and `localPredicate: PublicSessionStatusSpecification.StatusCriteria` (`:62-69`), then projects the matching session ids (`:71-74`). It uses the *same* helper and the *same* criteria the public session read filter uses, so a session hidden from the session list can never stay reachable through a speaker or junction read (`:60-61`).
  - `GetVisibleSpeakerIdsAsync` (`PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:99-127`) takes an optional `eventId` scope. With a scope, the published-event set is narrowed to that one id, and an unpublished or unknown scoped event narrows it to empty rather than raising an error (`:108-112`), which is the fail-closed reading of BR-108. An empty scope, or an empty eligible-session set, short-circuits to `[]` (`:114-119`). Otherwise it projects the distinct `SpeakerId` values off the `SessionSpeaker` join for those sessions (`:121-126`). The remarks record a real leak this shape fixed (`:92-98`): the `EventSpeaker` join is deliberately NOT consulted, because the Sessionize import writes a row there for every speaker in the response, so reading it as a visibility grant published the whole imported roster and made the filter vacuous. The session link is the only acceptance signal a speaker carries.
  - `GetEligibleSessionIdsAsync` (`PublicConferenceVisibility.cs:134-151`) is the private variant used by the speaker rule, which needs to narrow an *already resolved* event scope. It ANDs a `PublicSessionStatusSpecification` instance with an `InlineSpecification` over the scoped id list (`:141-143`), which keeps the criteria a translatable `IN` filter with no navigation join, and reuses the one status allow-list rather than restating it.
- **Why it's built this way**: a single shared definition is the only way five independent read handlers can agree on what "public" means. The two entry points into [`PublicSessionStatusSpecification`](#publicsessionstatusspecification) (the raw `StatusCriteria` expression for composition, an instance for specification algebra) exist precisely so this class can use whichever form each call site needs without a second copy of the predicate.
- **Where it's used**: five public-read handlers call it: [`GetPublicSessionSpeakerFilterHandler`](#getpublicsessionspeakerfilterhandler) (`GetPublicSessionSpeakerFilterHandler.cs:24`), [`GetPublicSessionCategoryItemFilterHandler`](#getpublicsessioncategoryitemfilterhandler) (`GetPublicSessionCategoryItemFilterHandler.cs:25`), [`GetPublicSpeakerFilterHandler`](#getpublicspeakerfilterhandler) (`GetPublicSpeakerFilterHandler.cs:26`), [`GetPublicSpeakerCategoryItemFilterHandler`](#getpublicspeakercategoryitemfilterhandler) (`GetPublicSpeakerCategoryItemFilterHandler.cs:26`), and [`GetPublicEventSpeakerFilterHandler`](#getpubliceventspeakerfilterhandler), which calls two of the three resolvers (`GetPublicEventSpeakerFilterHandler.cs:31`, `:38`). All five live under `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the class is `static` and takes its `IUnitOfWork` per call, so it is not injectable and cannot be substituted in a test; callers are tested against it directly. Cost is another thing the source does not hide: the speaker rule issues up to three sequential round-trips (events, sessions, join rows), which is the price of avoiding a cross-aggregate join.

### RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.RemoveSessionCategoryItem` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/RemoveSessionCategoryItem/RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:13` · Level 10 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveSessionCategoryItemCommand`](#removesessioncategoryitemcommand). It is the canonical "remove a child from the session aggregate" shape that the two sibling handlers below vary from.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) aggregate and its [`SessionCategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessioncategoryitem) child, `Result`, and `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging`.
- **Concept introduced, load-tracked-then-mutate-through-the-aggregate**: the recurring pattern for child removal. `HandleAsync` (`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:18-39`) loads the session with its `SessionCategoryItems` and `asTracking: true` (`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:22-27`), returns `Error.NotFound` stamped with the handler as source and `Session` as target when absent (`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:28-29`), then delegates the actual removal to the domain method `entity.RemoveSessionCategoryItem(...)` (`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:31`) so the aggregate enforces its own invariants. Only on success does it `SaveChangesAsync` and log; the domain `Result` is returned unchanged either way (`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:32-38`). `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]`: the handler never mutates child state directly, it asks the aggregate root. `[Rubric §13, Observability and Operability]`: logging goes through the source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` partial (`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:41-42`), the zero-allocation, compile-checked logging pattern used on every handler in this module.
- **Walkthrough**: the primary constructor takes `IUnitOfWork` and a typed `ILogger<RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler>` (`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:13-15`); the generated `LogCategoryItemRemovedFromSession` method is declared at `RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler.cs:41-42` at `Information` level. Note that `asTracking: true` is not incidental: the aggregate mutation has to be observed by the change tracker for the subsequent save to emit anything.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping removal logic in the aggregate and cache eviction on the command (via [`ICacheInvalidating`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icacheinvalidating)) leaves the handler as pure orchestration: load, delegate, save, log.
- **Where it's used**: registered by the Conference Application module registration; invoked from the session-category management endpoint.

### RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.RemoveSessionQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/RemoveSessionQuestionAnswer/RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:14` · Level 10 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerCommand`](#removesessionquestionanswercommand). The same load-delegate-save shape as [`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler`](#removesessioncategoryitemhandler), with an ownership guard added before the removal.
- **Depends on**: everything the sibling handler depends on, plus [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice) and [`RoleNames`](group-08-auth.md#rolenames) (`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:14-17`).
- **Concept introduced, per-record ownership authorization inside the command slice (BR-52 / BR-53)**: after loading the session with its `SessionQuestionAnswers` (`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:24-31`), it finds the target answer among the non-soft-deleted ones and, if the caller is not an `Organizer` and did not create it, returns `Error.Forbidden` with code `SessionQuestionAnswer.NotOwner` (`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:34-42`). Only then does it delegate to `entity.RemoveSessionQuestionAnswer(...)` (`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:44`). `[Rubric §11, Security]`: attendees may delete only their own answers, checked against the record's `CreatedBy` (the audit field stamped by the persistence interceptor) versus `currentUserService.UserId`, with organizers exempt. `[Rubric §6, CQRS and Event-Driven]`: the authorization decision lives in the slice that owns the operation, not scattered across controller attributes.
- **Walkthrough**: the guard is written so that a *missing* answer (`answer is not null` fails at `RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:35`) skips the ownership branch and falls through to the domain call, which is what produces the not-found style failure. A caller who cannot be identified would dereference `UserId!.Value`, so this handler is only reachable behind authenticated endpoints. The success path saves and logs via the generated `LogQuestionAnswerRemovedFromSession` (`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:45-49`, `RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:54-55`).
- **Where it's used**: invoked from the session question-answer endpoint.

### RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.RemoveSessionSpeaker` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/RemoveSessionSpeaker/RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:13` · Level 10 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`RemoveSessionSpeakerCommand`](#removesessionspeakercommand). The same load-delegate-save shape as [`RemoveSessionCategoryItemHandler`](#removesessioncategoryitemhandler), plus a fallback for resolving the owning session when the command omits `SessionId`.
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) aggregate and its [`SessionSpeaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionspeaker) child, `Result`, and logging.
- **Concept introduced, resolving the parent aggregate from a join id**: the DELETE endpoint takes the session id as an optional query parameter, but the UI's generic delete sends only the join-entity id, so `SessionId` arrives as the default `0` (the comment records exactly this, `RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:24-26`). When `SessionId == default` the handler queries for the session whose `SessionSpeakers` contains the join id, taking the first match (`RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:28-36`); otherwise it loads by id directly (`RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:37-44`). Both branches include `SessionSpeakers` and load `asTracking: true`, so the two paths hand the domain call an identically hydrated aggregate. `[Rubric §9, API and Contract Design]`: the handler adapts to a real client quirk without weakening the domain call, which still targets exactly one aggregate. `[Rubric §12, Performance and Scalability]`: the fallback is an `Any(...)`-predicate scan used only on the id-less path, not on the hot one.
- **Walkthrough**: after resolving the session (or returning `Error.NotFound`, `RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:46-47`), it delegates to `entity.RemoveSessionSpeaker(...)` (`RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:49`), then saves and logs on success via the generated `LogSpeakerRemovedFromSession` (`RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:50-54`, `RemoveSessionSpeakerHandler.cs:59-60`). The log statement writes `command.SessionId`, so on the fallback path it records the defaulted `0` rather than the resolved session id.
- **Where it's used**: invoked from the session-speaker management endpoint.

### SessionCreateRequestMapper

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/Create/SessionCreateRequestMapper.cs:11` · Level 10 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the adapter that turns a validated [`SessionCreateRequest`](#sessioncreaterequest) into a [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) domain entity by calling the aggregate's `Create` factory (`SessionCreateRequestMapper.cs:7-9`).
- **Depends on**: [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) (the framework contract it implements, closed over `Session`/`SessionCreateRequest`/`SessionIdentifierType`, `SessionCreateRequestMapper.cs:11-12`), the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) factory, and `Result`.
- **Concept introduced, request-to-entity mapping as its own step**: the create pipeline separates "shape the input" (this mapper) from "orchestrate the use case" (the handler). `CreateEntityAsync` (`SessionCreateRequestMapper.cs:15-34`) guards the null request with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull` (`:17`), then forwards the request fields positionally into `Session.Create(...)` (`:19-33`), returning that factory's `Result<Session>` wrapped in an already-completed `Task`. There is no `async` here at all: the mapping is synchronous, and the `Task` exists only to satisfy the interface, which other entities implement with genuinely asynchronous lookups. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: single responsibility, the mapper knows the factory's argument order and nothing else. `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]`: the mapper decouples the request DTO from the domain constructor so the handler depends only on the interface and never on this class. Manual mapping over reflection-based mapping follows [ADR-001](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/001-manual-dto-mapping.html).
- **Walkthrough**: fourteen positional arguments, in factory order: `Id`, `Title`, `Description`, `StartsAt`, `EndsAt`, `Status`, `IsServiceSession`, `IsPlenumSession`, `LiveUrl`, `RecordingUrl`, `AccessibilityInfo`, `ResourceLinks`, `EventId`, `RoomId` (`SessionCreateRequestMapper.cs:20-33`). Three request fields are deliberately absent from that list: `IsInformed`, `IsConfirmed`, and `Duration` are accepted on [`SessionCreateRequest`](#sessioncreaterequest) but never reach `Session.Create`, so the factory's own defaults win for those on a create.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`CreateSessionHandler`](#createsessionhandler) as `IEntityRequestMapper<Session, SessionCreateRequest, SessionIdentifierType>`, so the handler is constructed against the interface and this class is only named at registration.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: whether the three unmapped fields are an intentional "set them on update, not on create" policy or an oversight is not stated in this file or in the factory's documentation.

### SessionCreateRequestValidator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/Create/SessionCreateRequestValidator.cs:7` · Level 10 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the FluentValidation validator for [`SessionCreateRequest`](#sessioncreaterequest). It composes reusable per-field rule sets rather than restating each rule inline (`SessionCreateRequestValidator.cs:6`).
- **Depends on**: `FluentValidation` (`AbstractValidator<T>` and its `Include`) and the shared `Session*Rules<T>` classes from `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.Validation` (`SessionCreateRequestValidator.cs:1-2`).
- **Concept introduced, composed validation via reusable rule includes**: the constructor (`SessionCreateRequestValidator.cs:9-19`) calls `Include(...)` once per field rule set, each rule class generic over the request type and constructed with a property selector, for example `Include(new SessionTitleRules<SessionCreateRequest>(p => p.Title))` (`:11`). `Include` folds the other validator's rules into this one, so the composite reports a single flat error list. Because the rule classes are generic over the *request* type rather than tied to one DTO, the create and update requests can share the identical rule instance shape. `[Rubric §24, Forms/Validation/UX Safety]` and `[Rubric §15, Best Practices and Code Quality]`: a rule such as title length or URL format is defined once, so it cannot drift between the create and update paths. The validator runs in the CQRS pipeline before the handler executes.
- **Walkthrough**: eight includes, covering `Title`, `EventId`, `Description`, `Status`, `LiveUrl`, `RecordingUrl`, `AccessibilityInfo`, and `ResourceLinks` (`SessionCreateRequestValidator.cs:11-18`). Note what is *not* validated here: `StartsAt`/`EndsAt` ordering and the room assignment are enforced further in, by the domain factory and by [`CreateSessionHandler`](#createsessionhandler)'s BR-130 room check, because both need data this validator does not have.
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the validation stage of the create pipeline for `SessionCreateRequest`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the composed `Session*Rules` classes live in the `Sessions.Validation` namespace and are covered in their own sections; this one covers only how the create validator assembles them.

### UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.UpdateSessionQuestionAnswer` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/UpdateSessionQuestionAnswer/UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:14` · Level 10 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the handler for [`UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerCommand`](#updatesessionquestionanswercommand). It mirrors [`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler`](#removesessionquestionanswerhandler) line for line, enforcing the same BR-52 / BR-53 ownership rule before applying an edit instead of a removal (`UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:10-13`).
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), [`ICurrentUserService`](group-08-auth.md#icurrentuserservice), [`RoleNames`](group-08-auth.md#rolenames), the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) aggregate, `Result`, and logging.
- **Concept introduced**: none new; the ownership guard is the one taught on [`RemoveSessionQuestionAnswerHandler`](#removesessionquestionanswerhandler). Load the session with its answers, tracked (`UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:24-31`); block a non-organizer editing another user's answer with `Error.Forbidden` code `SessionQuestionAnswer.NotOwner` and the message "You can only update your own answers." (`UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:34-42`); then delegate to `entity.UpdateSessionQuestionAnswer(command.SessionQuestionAnswerId, command.AnswerValue)` (`UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:44`). The pair is a small, deliberate duplication: two use-case folders, one authorization rule stated twice rather than a shared base class that would couple the slices.
- **Walkthrough**: the success path saves and logs via the generated `LogSessionQuestionAnswerUpdated`, which records only the answer id (`UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:45-49`, `UpdateSessionQuestionAnswerHandler.cs:54-55`).
- **Where it's used**: invoked from the session question-answer edit endpoint.

### CreateSessionHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Sessions.UseCases.Create` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/Create/CreateSessionHandler.cs:22` · Level 11 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the command handler for creating a session, and the richest write path in this group. It assigns manual ids in a reserved range, validates the room assignment (BR-130 cross-event plus double-booking), delegates entity construction to the mapper, persists, maps the result to a DTO, and retries on a concurrent id collision (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:15-21`).
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), [`IEntityRequestMapper<TEntity, TCreateRequest, TIdentifierType>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#ientityrequestmappertentity-tcreaterequest-tidentifiertype) (satisfied by [`SessionCreateRequestMapper`](#sessioncreaterequestmapper)), [`SessionDTOMapper`](#sessiondtomapper), the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) and [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) aggregates, [`SessionInvariants`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessioninvariants) (the reserved manual-id range), [`SessionRoomScheduling`](#sessionroomscheduling) (the BR-130 room rules), the [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto) result shape, `Result`, plus `IServiceScopeFactory` and logging (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:22-27`).
- **Concept introduced, application-assigned ids with a bounded retry on collision**: session ids are application-assigned because the `int` primary key IS the Sessionize id (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:76-78`). When the caller supplies no id (organizer create, where the request `Id` defaults to `0`), `CreateCoreAsync` reads every existing row inside the reserved manual range with `ignoreQueryFilters: true` so soft-deleted rows still reserve their id, then takes `Max(Id) + 1` or the range start when the range is empty (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:79-89`); an exhausted range returns a failure rather than wrapping around (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:91-92`). That range is `SessionInvariants.ManualIdRangeStart` = 999,999,000 through `ManualIdRangeEnd` = 999,999,999 (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Sessions/SessionInvariants.cs:41`, `:44`), deliberately above any real Sessionize id. Because two concurrent creates can compute the same next id, `HandleAsync` wraps the attempt in a bounded loop of `MaxManualIdAttempts` = 3 (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:30`, `CreateSessionHandler.cs:42-62`); a duplicate-key failure recomputes the id in a fresh DI scope via `scopeFactory.CreateAsyncScope()` so a clean `DbContext` is used, since the ambient one still tracks the failed insert (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:51-55`). `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]`: id allocation is an explicit, range-partitioned concern rather than a database identity column, which is what lets an imported id and a hand-created id share one table. `[Rubric §12, Performance and Scalability]`: the collision path is exceptional and capped at three attempts, not a lock on the hot path.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - An explicit caller id (a Sessionize import, for example) is respected as-is and gets a single attempt with no id recomputation, because a collision there is a genuine caller error (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:37-40`).
  - `CreateCoreAsync` (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:69-136`) takes the unit of work as a parameter rather than closing over the injected one, which is exactly what makes the fresh-scope retry possible. It resolves the manual id when unset and rewrites the request with a `with` expression (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:94`).
  - The room rules run only when `RoomId` has a value (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:100`): the parent event is loaded untracked with its `Rooms` (`:102-107`, `Error.NotFound` when missing at `:108-109`), then `SessionRoomScheduling.ValidateRoomAssignmentAsync` checks that the room belongs to this event (BR-130, error code `Session.RoomId.CrossEvent`) and that the `[StartsAt, EndsAt)` slot does not overlap another session in the same room (`:111-121`, with `excludeSessionId: null` because nothing exists yet to exclude). The comment at `:97-99` explains why the event load is inside this branch: a room-less session has nothing to validate and create has no other use for the event.
  - It then maps via `requestMapper.CreateEntityAsync` with an early return on failure, adds, saves, logs, and returns `Result.Success(dtoMapper.MapToDTO(entity))` (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:124-135`).
  - `IsUniqueKeyViolation` (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:143-152`) walks the whole exception chain looking for the text "duplicate key", case-insensitively. Detection is message-based because the Application layer cannot reference EF Core or SQL Server types, and both SQL Server errors 2601 and 2627 carry that wording (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:138-142`).
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping organizer-created ids in a reserved high range prevents them from colliding with future Sessionize-assigned ids, and the fresh-scope retry is the only reliable way to recover from a duplicate-key race without leaking EF types into the Application layer. The double-booking half of the room check is documented in [`SessionRoomScheduling`](#sessionroomscheduling) as a deliberate SOFT guard: probe and insert are separate statements, so two concurrent organizer writes can both pass it, accepted because the endpoint is organizer-only and the outcome is repairable (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/Validation/SessionRoomScheduling.cs:16-25`). Structured logging uses the source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` partials `LogSessionCreated` and `LogManualIdCollision` (`CreateSessionHandler.cs:154-158`), the collision one at `Warning` level so a retry storm is visible in telemetry.
- **Where it's used**: registered as the handler for [`SessionCreateRequest`](#sessioncreaterequest); invoked from the session create endpoint and from the Sessionize import path.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the manual-id scan loads every row in the reserved range to compute a maximum rather than projecting one scalar, so its cost grows with the number of hand-created sessions; nothing in the file bounds that. Message-based exception matching is also locale-sensitive by nature, and the code notes only the SQL Server error numbers, not what a different provider would report.

### DeleteEventHandler

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Events.UseCases.Delete` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Events/UseCases/Delete/DeleteEventHandler.cs:16` · Level 10 · class (sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the one delete in the Conference module that is not the framework's generic handler. Deleting an [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) must also soft-delete every [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) that belongs to it, and a session is a separate aggregate the event cannot reach, so this handler loads both graphs and hands them to a domain service (`DeleteEventHandler.cs:11-15` states exactly that rationale: BR-127 for the session cascade, BR-72 for the event's own children).
- **Depends on**: [`ICommandHandler<in TCommand, TResult>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#icommandhandlerin-tcommand-tresult) closed over [`DeleteEntityCommand<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#deleteentitycommandtentity-tidentifiertype)`<Event, EventIdentifierType>` and [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) (`DeleteEventHandler.cs:19`), [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), [`IEventCascadeDeletionDomainService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#ieventcascadedeletiondomainservice) (implemented by [`EventCascadeDeletionDomainService`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventcascadedeletiondomainservice)), the [`Event`](group-17-conference-domain.md#event) and [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) aggregates, [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), and `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging` (`DeleteEventHandler.cs:1-7`).
- **Concept introduced, replacing a generic framework handler by registering a more specific one**: `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]` assesses whether a general mechanism can be specialized without being forked, and `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` assesses whether one use case can deviate without disturbing its neighbours. The framework ships [`DeleteEntityHandler<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#deleteentityhandlertentity-tidentifiertype), which loads one entity by id, calls `Delete()`, and saves (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/UseCases/DeleteEntityHandler.cs:21-35`). Sessions, Speakers, Categories, and Questions all take that generic registration; `Event` alone is bound to this class instead (`MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:54` versus `:58`, `:62`, `:66`, `:71`). Because both implement the same closed interface, `ICommandHandler<DeleteEntityCommand<Event, EventIdentifierType>, Result>`, the substitution is invisible above: [`EventsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#eventscontroller) takes that interface in its constructor (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:50`) and passes it straight to the [`AggregateRootEntityControllerBase<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType, TCreateRequest>`](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#aggregaterootentitycontrollerbasetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype-tcreaterequest) delete slot (`EventsController.cs:57-58`). No route, no request shape, and no other slice changes.
- **Concept introduced, the include list is what makes an in-memory cascade work**: `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]` assesses whether the aggregate stays the owner of its own invariants. `Event.Delete()` soft-deletes the event and then walks its three owned child lists in memory (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/Event.cs:280-311`), and `Session.Delete()` does the same over its three (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Sessions/Session.cs:271-302`). A child that was never materialized is a child the loop cannot see. That is why the `includes` arrays on `DeleteEventHandler.cs:29` (`Rooms`, `EventSpeakers`, `EventQuestionAnswers`) and `DeleteEventHandler.cs:38` (`SessionSpeakers`, `SessionQuestionAnswers`, `SessionCategoryItems`) match the collections those two `Delete()` overrides iterate, one for one. They are not there to shape a response: this handler returns a bare [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) with no payload. `asTracking: true` on both loads (`DeleteEventHandler.cs:30`, `:40`) is equally load-bearing, since an untracked graph would make the subsequent save a silent no-op.
- **Walkthrough**: a primary constructor taking `unitOfWork`, `eventCascadeDeletionDomainService`, and a typed `ILogger` (`DeleteEventHandler.cs:16-19`).
  - `HandleAsync` (`DeleteEventHandler.cs:22-52`) resolves the write repository through [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork) rather than injecting one (`DeleteEventHandler.cs:26`) and loads the event with its owned children (`:27-31`). A missing event returns `Error.NotFound` stamped with source and target (`:32-33`), the standard failure shape of the [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) pattern ([ADR-013](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/013-result-pattern.html)).
  - The second read is the cross-aggregate half: the [`Session`](group-17-conference-domain.md#session) repository's `GetAllAsync` with the child includes and the predicate `s => s.EventId.Equals(entity.Id) && !s.IsDeleted` (`:36-41`). `EventId` is a plain scalar column on `Session`, not a navigation, which is what lets this stay a single translatable query.
  - `eventCascadeDeletionDomainService.CascadeDelete(entity, sessions)` (`:44`) is the only decision point. The implementation deletes each session first and returns the first failure unchanged, only then deleting the event (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Services/EventCascadeDeletionDomainService.cs:16-30`), so a refused session delete leaves the event untouched.
  - Only on success does the handler call `unitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync(cancellationToken)` and log (`:45-49`); the failure path returns without saving (`:51`), discarding the in-memory mutations the aborted cascade already applied.
  - `LogEventDeleted` is a source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` partial method (`:54-55`), which is why the class is `partial`. `[Rubric §13, Observability and Operability]`: the log line is emitted once, after the save, with the event id as a structured field.
- **Why it's built this way**: the split is deliberate. *What* cascades is a business rule, so it lives in a pure domain service with no infrastructure dependencies (`EventCascadeDeletionDomainService.cs:7-12`); *which rows to load* is an application concern, so it lives here. Atomicity comes from one `SaveChangesAsync` covering both aggregates: they are entities of the same module and therefore the same `ADC_Conference` database ([ADR-006](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/006-database-per-service.html)), so EF wraps the whole cascade in a single transaction. The domain events each `Delete()` raised (`EventChanged` with `DomainEntityState.Deleted` at `Event.cs:307`, `SessionChanged` at `Session.cs:298`) are captured into the outbox in the same save ([ADR-003](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/003-outbox-dual-dispatch.html)). Soft-delete, not erasure, is the workspace default: `Delete()` sets `IsDeleted` and the context's named `SoftDelete` query filter hides the rows from every later read (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/DbContexts/ApplicationDbContext.cs:255`).
- **Where it's used**: registered as the `Event` delete handler at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:54` and reached through `DELETE /Events/{id}` on [`EventsController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#eventscontroller), which requires the `EventsManage` permission at the controller level (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:43`, [ADR-020](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/020-permission-based-authorization.html)) and overrides `DeleteAsync` to evict three output-cache tags afterwards, `conference:events`, `conference:sessions`, and `conference:rooms`, because the cascade invalidates all three read surfaces (`EventsController.cs:352-365`). Covered by [`DeleteEventHandlerTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#deleteeventhandlertests) (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Tests/Events/UseCases/DeleteEventHandlerTests.cs:15`), which mocks both repositories and the domain service.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: [`DeleteEntityCommand<TEntity, TIdentifierType>`](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#deleteentitycommandtentity-tidentifiertype) implements `ICacheInvalidating` only, not `ITransactional` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/UseCases/DeleteEntityCommand.cs:11`), so the Transactional decorator does not wrap this handler ([ADR-014](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/014-cqrs-decorator-pipeline.html)); the single `SaveChangesAsync` is the whole atomicity story. The `!s.IsDeleted` clause on `:39` restates what the global soft-delete filter already enforces, since the repository only bypasses filters when `ignoreQueryFilters` is set and this call leaves it at its default (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Persistence/Repositories/EFReadRepository.cs:26-40`). The save uses `ConfigureAwait(false)` (`:47`) while the two reads do not; CA2007 is switched off repo-wide in ADC (`MMCA.ADC/.editorconfig:348`), so nothing enforces either choice here. Finally, the session load is unpaged: an event with a very large session count loads every session and its children into memory in one batch, and no source comment records a ceiling for that.

### SpeakerEntityQueryService

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:15` · Level 10 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the only per-entity read service the Conference module writes by hand. It is a five-line subclass of the framework's generic [`EntityQueryService<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#entityqueryservicetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) whose entire contribution is one dictionary entry: the DTO field `FullName` maps to the entity expression `(FirstName + " " + LastName)` (`SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:28-31`).
- **Depends on**: the base [`EntityQueryService<TEntity, TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#entityqueryservicetentity-tentitydto-tidentifiertype) closed over [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) / [`SpeakerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerdto) / `SpeakerIdentifierType` (`SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:21-22`), and the five constructor dependencies it forwards: [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), [`INavigationMetadataProvider`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#inavigationmetadataprovider), [`IEntityQueryPipeline`](group-03-querying-specifications.md#ientityquerypipeline), [`SpeakerDTOMapper`](#speakerdtomapper), and [`INavigationPopulator<in TEntity>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#inavigationpopulatorin-tentity)`<Speaker>` (`SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:15-20`). Note that the mapper parameter is the *concrete* [`SpeakerDTOMapper`](#speakerdtomapper), narrowed from the base's `IEntityDTOMapper<...>` parameter.
- **Concept introduced, a server-authored property map as the bridge between a DTO contract and a queryable column**: `[Rubric §9, API and Contract Design]` assesses whether the public sort and filter vocabulary is stable and independent of the storage model, and `[Rubric §12, Performance and Scalability]` assesses whether that vocabulary still resolves to server-side SQL rather than in-memory work. `Speaker.FullName` is a computed C# property (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Speakers/Speaker.cs:54`) that is explicitly *not* mapped to a column (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Infrastructure/Persistence/EntityConfiguration/SpeakerConfiguration.cs:67-68` calls `builder.Ignore(p => p.FullName)`). A client that asks to sort by `FullName` is therefore asking for something the database has no column for. The map turns that request into a dynamic-LINQ fragment the provider can render: `QueryFieldService.ApplySorting` looks the name up, and on a hit passes the mapped string straight into `query.OrderBy(...)` as `"(FirstName + \" \" + LastName) ascending"` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/QueryFieldService.cs:144-153`); the filter path resolves the same entry before applying its strategy (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/Filtering/QueryFilterService.cs:84-97`). The result is one `ORDER BY` over concatenated columns instead of a page fetched and re-ordered in the host.
- **Concept introduced, why a map hit is trusted**: `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether client-supplied strings can widen what a query touches. Validation runs before the pipeline and accepts a map hit unconditionally, without reflecting over the mapped value, precisely because map values are *server-authored* and are deliberately not plain property names (`QueryFieldService.cs:260-296` documents the rule; the base wires it at `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/EntityQueryService.cs:223-228`). A name with no map entry has to resolve to a real public property of the entity or the sort falls back to the default instead of reaching Dynamic LINQ (`QueryFieldService.cs:119-148`). So the trust boundary is the class you are reading: whatever a subclass puts in this dictionary is executed as a query fragment, which is exactly why the dictionary is `private static readonly` and not configurable from outside.
- **Walkthrough**: three things happen, and that is the whole file.
  - The primary constructor takes the five services and forwards them unchanged to the base (`SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:15-22`). No field is kept; nothing is overridden about how a query executes.
  - `PropertyMap` is a `private static readonly IReadOnlyDictionary<string, string>` with the single `FullName` entry (`SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:28-31`), built once at class load rather than per request.
  - `DTOToEntityPropertyMap => PropertyMap` (`SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:34`) overrides the base's `protected virtual` hook, whose default is an empty case-insensitive dictionary (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/EntityQueryService.cs:56-61`). The base then threads that map into both validation (`EntityQueryService.cs:225-227`) and the query parameters handed to the pipeline (`EntityQueryService.cs:425-437`).
- **Why it's built this way**: subclassing is the registration hook. Every other Conference read is served by the closed generic registered directly, for example `EntityQueryService<SpeakerCategoryItem, SpeakerCategoryItemDTO, ...>` at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:96`; only `Speaker` needs a non-default map, so only `Speaker` gets a named subclass, registered as `IEntityQueryService<Speaker, SpeakerDTO, SpeakerIdentifierType>` at `DependencyInjection.cs:61`. That keeps [ADR-034](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/034-generic-entity-query-layer.html)'s generic read layer intact: the specialization is additive data, not a second query implementation. `[Rubric §1, SOLID]`: the base is open for extension through one protected member and closed to modification.
- **Where it's used**: injected into [`SpeakersController`](group-20-conference-api-grpc.md#speakerscontroller) as `IEntityQueryService<Speaker, SpeakerDTO, SpeakerIdentifierType>` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/SpeakersController.cs:45`), which is what serves the list and detail speaker endpoints. The map is not theoretical: the speaker list page sorts by `"FullName"` ascending on every fetch and turns its search box into a `("contains", term)` filter on the same field (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.UI/Pages/Speaker/SpeakerList.razor.cs:145`, `:158`, the [`SpeakerList`](group-21-conference-ui.md#speakerlist) page's sortable, filterable `FullName` column at `SpeakerList.razor:100`). Covered by [`SpeakerEntityQueryServiceTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#speakerentityqueryservicetests) (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Tests/Speakers/SpeakerEntityQueryServiceTests.cs:15`), which pins the exact expression string as a constant (`:17`) so a silent edit fails the build.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the mapped expression assumes both name parts are non-null strings; SQL Server's concatenation semantics, not this class, decide what a null part renders as. The base's default map is case-insensitive (`EntityQueryService.cs:61`) while this override supplies a plain `Dictionary` with the default comparer (`SpeakerEntityQueryService.cs:28`), so lookups of `fullname` or `FULLNAME` miss the map here; a miss then falls back to reflecting `FullName` on the entity, which does exist (`Speaker.cs:54`), so the filter path still resolves but the sort would order by a property EF cannot translate. No test in the repository exercises a differently cased sort column, so the runtime outcome of that path is not determinable from source.

### SpeakerNavigationPopulator

> MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Speakers` · `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Speakers/SpeakerNavigationPopulator.cs:11` · Level 10 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) aggregate's declaration of which child collections have to be loaded by hand when EF Core cannot `Include()` them. The class has an empty body: everything it says is said in the two descriptors passed to its base constructor (`SpeakerNavigationPopulator.cs:11-31`).
- **Depends on**: [`DeclarativeNavigationPopulator<TEntity>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#declarativenavigationpopulatortentity)`<Speaker>` as its base (`SpeakerNavigationPopulator.cs:13`), two [`ChildNavigationDescriptor<TEntity, TParentId, TChild, TChildId>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#childnavigationdescriptortentity-tparentid-tchild-tchildid) instances closed over [`SpeakerCategoryItem`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakercategoryitem) and [`SpeakerQuestionAnswer`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speakerquestionanswer) (`:15`, `:22`), and [`IUnitOfWork`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#iunitofwork), which it only forwards (`:12-13`).
- **Concept introduced**: the descriptor mechanism itself is taught in group 11 on [`DeclarativeNavigationPopulator<TEntity>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#declarativenavigationpopulatortentity) and [`ChildNavigationDescriptor<TEntity, TParentId, TChild, TChildId>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#childnavigationdescriptortentity-tparentid-tchild-tchildid); this class is the binding, alongside its three siblings [`EventNavigationPopulator`](#eventnavigationpopulator), [`SessionNavigationPopulator`](#sessionnavigationpopulator), and [`ConferenceCategoryNavigationPopulator`](#conferencecategorynavigationpopulator). What is worth learning *here* is when it actually fires. The base loops the descriptors and loads one only if its `PropertyName` appears in `navigationMetadata.UnsupportedIncludes` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/Navigation/DeclarativeNavigationPopulator.cs:27-41`), and a navigation is classified unsupported only when the parent and child resolve to different physical data sources, or to Cosmos (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/Query/NavigationMetadataProvider.cs:96-99` calling `HaveIncludeSupport`, defined as `first == second && first.Engine != DataSource.CosmosDB` at `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Services/DataSourceService.cs:31-32`). In ADC today every Conference entity lives in the one `ADC_Conference` SQL Server database, so both navigations classify as *supported*, EF `Include()` handles them, and this populator's loop does nothing. It is standing capacity for a source split, not code on today's hot path ([ADR-002](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/002-navigation-populators.html), [ADR-018](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/018-polyglot-persistence.html)). `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses whether the persistence strategy can change without rewriting the layers above: the read path already routes every child load through this abstraction, so moving `Speaker` to its own source changes classification, not code.
- **Walkthrough**: two descriptors, four members each, and both follow the same shape.
  - `SpeakerCategoryItems` (`SpeakerNavigationPopulator.cs:15-21`): `PropertyName = nameof(Speaker.SpeakerCategoryItems)` is the string the base matches against the metadata; `ParentKeySelector = e => e.Id` extracts the parent key; `ChildForeignKeySelector = child => child.SpeakerId` is an `Expression`, not a delegate, because the base builds a `WHERE SpeakerId IN (...)` predicate out of it; `AssignAction = (e, categoryItems) => e.SetSpeakerCategoryItems(categoryItems)` hands the grouped results back to the aggregate.
  - `SpeakerQuestionAnswers` (`:22-28`): identical apart from the child type, the property name, and the setter.
  - The load itself is one batched query per descriptor: [`ChildNavigationDescriptor<TEntity, TParentId, TChild, TChildId>`](group-11-navigation-populators.md#childnavigationdescriptortentity-tparentid-tchild-tchildid)`.LoadAsync` forwards to `NavigationLoader.LoadChildrenPropertyAsync`, which collects the distinct parent ids, issues a single `WHERE ParentFK IN (...)` query, groups the rows, and assigns (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/Navigation/ChildNavigationDescriptor.cs:37-47`, `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/NavigationLoader.cs:102-106`). Two descriptors means at most two extra round-trips for a whole page of speakers, not two per speaker. `[Rubric §12, Performance and Scalability]`: the N+1 is designed out at the loader, so a populator author cannot reintroduce it.
- **Why it's built this way**: the assignment goes through the aggregate rather than around it. `SetSpeakerCategoryItems` and `SetSpeakerQuestionAnswers` are `internal` methods on the domain entity (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Speakers/Speaker.cs:381-382`, `:458-459`) that delegate to the framework's `SetItems` helper, and the Application project can call them only because the Domain project grants it `InternalsVisibleTo` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain.csproj:3`). The public surface stays read-only: both collections are exposed as `IReadOnlyCollection<T>` over private backing lists (`Speaker.cs:60`, `:66`). `[Rubric §4, Domain-Driven Design]` and `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]`: an infrastructure concern (batch loading) refills the aggregate without any layer gaining write access to its collections, and the Application layer takes no EF dependency to do it.
- **Where it's used**: registered as `INavigationPopulator<Speaker>` at `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:60`, one line above the [`SpeakerEntityQueryService`](#speakerentityqueryservice) registration that consumes it (`:61`). The base query service calls `NavigationPopulator.PopulateAsync` as part of every pipeline execution (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Services/EntityQueryService.cs:439-444`). Covered by [`SpeakerNavigationPopulatorTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#speakernavigationpopulatortests) (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application.Tests/Speakers/SpeakerNavigationPopulatorTests.cs:9`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the descriptors name properties and setters as strings and lambdas resolved at construction, so renaming or adding a child collection on [`Speaker`](group-17-conference-domain.md#speaker) compiles fine here and simply stops that collection from ever being populated in a split-source deployment; nothing in the build fails. The two `SpeakerId` foreign keys must exist on the child entities for the expression to translate, which is a persistence-model fact this file assumes rather than states.
